# MEMORY - tatara-operator

- 2026-08-10 (the brainstorm gate Task now MINTS PARKED, and the gate prompt stops instructing self-approval) **`mintGateTask` (`internal/restapi/outcome.go`) was the ONE Task-mint site that bypassed the intake funnel**: it set neither `Spec.InitialState` nor `Spec.InitialParkReason`, so the reconciler minted it live at `new`, triage routed kind=implement to `refined`, a ticket was admitted, and an opus agent ran for minutes on a proposal NO HUMAN HAD APPROVED - posting a `## Plan` comment on the thread, submitting `action=approved` and getting refused `no-maintainer-comment`, then parking `awaiting-human`. Nothing implemented, one full agent session and one forge comment spent, every single time. It now mints `StateNew` + `backlog-sweep` via `gateTaskMintParkReason`, which is the SAME verdict `MintStage`'s clause 2 reaches for a bot-authored comment-free issue on every other path - reusing an existing `UnparkHuman` reason on purpose: the first non-bot comment releases it through `driveCommentUnpark`, it is deliberately NOT in `NeedsLiveRoom` so that release does not additionally wait on pod capacity, and `MintParked` emits no park counter so the park-rate alert stays clean. INTENDED BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: a parked proposal no longer burns a `maxOpenTasks` slot (`StageActive` excludes parked), which LOOSENS the cap rather than tightening it; the brainstorm refill law is untouched because `proposalPending` keys on Issue status, never on Task park state. `autoApproveTataraProposals` ON keeps the old live mint byte-for-byte, because the carve-out grants that agent with no comment to cite. The prompt half shipped with it: the "omit approving_maintainer AND approval_citations together, a tatara-proposed issue has no comment to cite" paragraph is the CARVE-OUT'S instruction and is now gated on the same flag (`implementCitationRule`); flag off, the agent is told the opposite and given `action=discuss` as the exit, because that licence was deterministically driving it into the refusal it could never pass.
- 2026-08-10 (feat/579-shutdown-log-gate, #579) **#557 fixed the Reconcile boundary; the ERROR lines that fire "Tatara operator error recurring" are written BELOW it, by dependencies, through the reconcile-scoped contextual logger - so the alert re-fired under a new `msg` instead of stopping.** `classifyReconcileShutdown` can only see what a reconciler RETURNS. `client-go` `rest/request.go:1196` logs `Unexpected error when reading response body` at ERROR when `io.ReadAll(resp.Body)` sees the cancelled request context (its `default:` branch; the analogous benign `http2.StreamError` gets `V(2).Info`), and controller-runtime `pkg/manager/internal.go:533` logs `error received after stop sequence was engaged` with `leader election lost` - deterministic on every graceful shutdown of the leaseholder under `LeaderElectionReleaseOnCancel`. Both go through `klog.FromContext(ctx)` / `cm.logger`, i.e. our JSON handler, ~60us before the boundary classifier declares the same cancellation "not an error". Fix is a `slog.Handler` decorator (`internal/obs/shutdown_log.go`) that re-levels ERROR->INFO iff a `ShutdownGate` is engaged AND a rule matches. **Three things the issue did not have and each of which would have shipped a broken fix.** (1) `logr.slogSink.log` calls `handler.Handle(context.Background(), record)` (logr@v1.4.3 `slogsink.go:94`) - the reconcile ctx never reaches the handler, so `labels.go:58`'s ctx half is UNUSABLE there; the gate supplies that half by reading the signal ctx's `Err()` at emission time (an `atomic.Pointer`, not a goroutine-set flag: the ERROR lands inside the stop sequence and a wakeup can lose that race), and the record supplies the error half because slog still holds the live `error` object, not its text. (2) `ctrl.SetLogger` was called TWICE - bootstrap in `main()`, then the configured logger in `run()` - and the second is a silent NO-OP: `delegatingLogSink.Fulfill` nils its promise on first Fulfill (`pkg/log/deleg.go:79,194`). controller-runtime, klog and client-go had been on the BOOTSTRAP logger since June, so `LOG_LEVEL` never applied to dependency logs and wrapping only `run()`'s logger would have changed nothing. `setupLogging` is now the only `ctrl.SetLogger` caller. (3) The downgrade marker must NOT be written to `action`: slog's JSON handler does not dedupe keys and Loki's `| json` is last-wins, so a marker on `action` makes the re-levelled line unfindable by its own action (hard rule 12 puts `action` on nearly every line). Keys are `shutdown_downgrade=<rule>` + `original_level=ERROR`. **Rules are pinned to their emission site on purpose.** `cancelled_request` = any record attr holding an error with `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` - `DeadlineExceeded` deliberately NOT matched, same reasoning as `labels.go:56`, so a grace-period overrun keeps its ERROR. `leader_election_lost` = the upstream msg AND `err.Error() == "leader election lost"`, pinned to the PAIR so any other runnable failure arriving on the same errChan (a webhook `Shutdown` deadline) keeps its ERROR; matching an upstream string is the only lever controller-runtime offers and a test locks it. **REJECTED: excluding the `msg` in `tatara-observability/alerts/tatara-logs.yaml`** - that is the third time this shape was proposed (#558 closed after it was tried twice) and it blinds the rule to genuine body-read failures. The downgraded lines stay countable at `INFO` rather than being dropped, so a real drain is still debuggable; at `LOG_LEVEL>=warn` they are dropped, which is the consistent reading of a line reclassified as a lifecycle event. The `Reconciler error` lines seen in the same Loki window are a REAL unpark-vs-deleted-Task bug (`drive unparks: unpark: apply on mt-i-...: tasks.tatara.dev "..." not found`), out of scope here and NOT fixed.
- 2026-08-10 (O3 point of no return, THE CEILINGS ARE GONE AND ONLY THE 24h DEAD-MAN SWITCH SURVIVES) **`maxTurnsPerTask`, `maxReviewRounds` and `maxPodRecreations` all measured how much an agent had DONE and parked it as if that were evidence of being STUCK - a 400-turn Task is a long job, a converging implement/review pair is not a loop, and a pod that died four times is a crash to alert on, not a Task to kill.** All three enforcement paths are deleted; `stage.ResidencyCapAll` (24h, all three live states, formerly 24h/6h/4h) is the one bound left, and it is a CONSTANT rather than a CRD field because structural-schema pruning drops unknown fields SILENTLY and the failure mode of a silently-pruned dead-man switch is no dead-man switch at all. Four decisions worth keeping. **(1) THE REAPER STAND-DOWN WAS NOT OPTIONAL AND HAD TO BE IN THIS PR.** `reaper.go`'s #237 idle backstop reaps a pod at 30m when no turn is in flight - which is *exactly* what a conversation waiting on a human looks like, and `idlePodReapMinutes` (30) sits below `ConversationIdleDefault` (60), so every such conversation was already being reaped at the half-hour mark. That was survivable ONLY because `maxPodRecreations` terminated the resulting churn after a few laps; deleting the cap without the stand-down would have made an abandoned conversation rotate a pod every 30 minutes for 24h (~48 pods) and trip the very `operator_pod_recreations_total` alert that replaced the cap - manufacturing the alert's own false positives. A LIVE, un-parked Task inside its `ConversationIdle` budget now owns its pod (read off the SAME `stage.ArmedClock` + `ConversationIdle` pair `reconcileClocks` reads, never a reimplementation); past it `liveHandoffAndPark` owns the teardown and #237 regains full reach. **(2) THE MULTIPLE-CLOCKS UNPARK DEFECT IS REAL AND THIS PR HIT IT TWICE.** `stage.Unpark`'s `ReasonNoOutcome` arm and `reenterParkedOnReview` BOTH declined re-entry on `Stats.Turns >= maxTurnsPerTask` - so a Task whose park you cleared was refused by a SECOND clock reading the same retired ceiling, and no operator action could recover it. Both gates are deleted with the ceiling. Generalise: park is a flag, but the conditions that write it are several independent clocks, so clearing `parkReason` by hand is never a fix - the owner hand-repaired three helmfile review Tasks that re-parked twice on different clocks (`admission-starved`, then `stage-deadline`) doing exactly that. **(3) THE MIGRATION IS A ONE-SHOT SWEEP, NOT A FOURTH RE-ENTRY CLASS.** The three reasons moved to a new `stage.UnparkRetired`, which `stage.Unpark` deliberately treats as `UnparkNever` (`DeclineNoReentry`) - giving it an arm there would turn a migration into a permanent retry loop AND hold every un-migrated leftover alive past `ParkRetention`, because the reaper's `unparkFires` probe calls that same function. `driveRetiredUnparks` stamps `tatara.dev/retired-park-migrated` BEFORE it un-parks: crashing between the two leaves a Task still parked and never retried (visible, inert, hand-fixable), whereas stamping second leaves a Task that un-parks on every pass forever. Skips anything parked >48h - old wreckage means a stale branch and a moved-on issue, and waking it burns tokens rediscovering that. `resumeNoReentryParks` was extended to cover `UnparkRetired` too, or the one-reply guarantee would have silently regressed for exactly the population this release is about. **(4) DROPPING THE RECREATION GATE FROM `podStoppedNoOutcome` IS A REAL BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, not bookkeeping**: a live-state Task whose pod object is absent now parks `no-outcome` on the FIRST pass instead of the fourth. It is correct (the predicate is a fact about now, not a count) and `no-outcome` is `UnparkTimer` so it re-drives - but it broke six residency tests that had been relying on the old gate to stay un-parked, and any future fixture in a live state needs its pod seeded. **NAMED RISK, ACCEPTED EXPLICITLY BY THE OWNER:** a boot-crash loop is now bounded ONLY by the 24h cap - at the `PodReadyTimeout` cycle that is a few hundred pods worst case, strictly worse than before - with `sum by (project) (increase(operator_pod_recreations_total[1h])) > 6` (critical, tatara-observability #105) as the sole compensating control. `stats.podRecreations` therefore keeps being counted at every attempt; stopping the count to match the deleted cap would blind the only thing left watching. The four `AgentSpec` fields and the `ParkReason` enum members STAY (helmfile sets three of them; removing an enum member breaks validation on the next status write of every object already carrying it) - the fields are `// Deprecated:` with zero readers, which forced dropping them from `restapi`'s `agentDTO`/`taskDTO` too, since staticcheck SA1019 makes "zero readers" a compile-time property rather than a promise. `make test` (4184 pass / 0 fail / 1 skip) and `make lint` (0 issues) green; semver:minor.

- 2026-08-09 (fix/submit-outcome-write-then-400, #578) **`submit_outcome` WROTE the outcome claim and THEN 400'd, and the "no open MR" precondition was kind-locked to review - together they burned 7 pod runs / 3 pod recreations on `mt-i-mtg-decks-22-3f4594d8ce81d4d0` and parked it `no-outcome` with `OutcomeAccepted=True`.** Found only because the promtail node-coverage fix landed the same day and made agent-pod stdout visible in Loki; before that the sole trace was `parkReason=no-outcome`, which reads as "the agent never answered". **Defect A (root):** `claimOutcomeFingerprint` ran at the TOP of `postOutcome`, above the terminal/parked/kind gates, above the payload decode and above every kind handler's read-only validation block, so EVERY 4xx mutated `status.conditions` first (`OutcomeAccepted{Reason=Outcome,Message=<fp>}`) and failed second. `release()` made the NET effect nil, but the write was OBSERVABLE (the agent's `task_get` saw it and correctly concluded the write had landed, then re-submitted) and BEST-EFFORT (a failed release leaves the claim until `OutcomeClaimTTL`). Fix: the claim is now **LAZY** - `peekOutcomeClaim` (read-only Get) at the top keeps the replay-200 / in-flight-409 verdicts, `outcomeCtx.claim()` takes the atomic CAS at each handler's validation/execution boundary, `release()` no-ops when `!o.claimed`, and `commit()` auto-claims as a backstop so nothing can commit unclaimed. Concurrency is UNCHANGED: the C7 contract is "claimed before any SIDE EFFECT", and validation is not one - two identical POSTs both peek free, both validate (idempotent), and the CAS still lets exactly one win. Head-moved (review) now deliberately holds NO claim at all: it stamps nothing on the Task and its mirror resync is idempotent. `incidentComment`'s target validation was hoisted into a read-only `commentTarget()` called from `incident()` BEFORE the `alertRules` spec merge - that was the one path that mutated the SPEC before a 400. **Defect B:** both `no-open-mr` sites gated the `terminalNoop`/`takenOverNoop` 2xx carve-outs on `spec.kind == "review"`. `stage.AgentKindFor` keys `awaiting-review` on the STATE, not spec.kind (`internal/stage/liveness.go`), so a kind=ISSUE Task there runs an `agentKind=review` pod - and when its own MR merged out of band and a human comment woke it, spec.kind was "issue", the gate did not apply, and it hit the hard 400. The carve-outs are now KIND-AGNOSTIC. **The 400 that stays is the one a retry can clear**: an EMPTY owned-MR set (not terminal by definition) is the mint/binding fault `repairMRBinding` heals. **Semantics chosen for B, and why (do not "simplify" this back):** the endpoint answers a non-retryable **2xx no-op** and does NOT finalize; the CONVERGENT RECONCILER edge finalizes - the same division `terminalNoop`/#33 already documented. Accepting the outcome for real was rejected on downstream reading: `LegalFor` GUARD 2 (`reviewGateOpen`) and the merge pipeline would drive a `merging` pass over already-merged MRs, and recording a review against a merged MR inflates `reviewRounds` and `operator_review_outcome_total` for a thread nobody reads. **The reconciler half was REQUIRED, not optional:** `terminalMREdge` is kind-locked to review and both convergent call sites (`reconcileClocks`, the `ensureStagePod` pre-dispatch guard) gated on `spec.kind == "review"`, so a kind=issue Task at awaiting-review with all MRs merged had NO finalize at all and NO committed outcome to reach `reviewAdvanceEdge` with - a permanent deadlock re-armed by every human comment (`stage.Unpark`'s non-review arm carries no `DeclineMergedMR` guard and no round counter). NEW `ownMRsShippedEdge` (reviewpost.go) is the non-review sibling; `externalTerminalEdge` is the one resolution both sites now call, and the kind gate moved OUT of the call sites INTO the edge resolution. **Its target is `merged`, NOT `done`** - shipped is not delivered: the merge-order cursor, deploy ledger, issue closes and `deliveredAt` all live past `merged`, `LegalFor` GUARD 3 refuses awaiting-review->done for non-review kinds for exactly that reason, and `ReconcileMerging` already has the idempotent already-merged branch this lands on. It REFUSES the merged branch while any owned MR still has `pendingReview != nil` (that is GUARD 2's condition; firing through it could only produce an `IllegalTransitionError`, and the ordinary drain path owns that decision). **Defect C lives in tatara-claude-code-wrapper, not here** (`cmd/wrapper/app.go` `finalizeTurn` -> `transcript.FailedCriticalOutcome` -> `app.reprompt`): it re-prompted on ANY `is_error` tool_result with "This is mandatory: call %s again... Do not finish the turn until the call succeeds", which is what converted one doomed 400 into a pod-recreation loop. Fixed in its own MR - a 4xx is a client error and by definition not retryable. `make test lint` green; semver:patch.

- 2026-08-09 (O2 stall state machine, inactivity is a question now, not a verdict) **The old `turnTimedOut -> stalledTurnStop` branch had exactly one input - silence - and silence is not evidence: a parent blocked on a subagent, an agent inside one long tool call, and a genuinely wedged wrapper are indistinguishable from outside, and no further clock arithmetic makes them distinguishable.** `reconcileStall` asks instead (`POST /v1/probe`), and only escalates when the agent cannot or will not answer. Four non-obvious calls. (1) **The ESC must precede the stop, and it is what makes the stop mean anything.** `stalledTurnStop` opens by waiting for idle and only then submits the handoff turn; a wedged turn never goes idle, so in production that wait times out and the sequence lands on the operator's synthetic note essentially every time - "graceful" was a misnomer. `POST /v1/interrupt` cancels the turn (~40ms, mid-tool, session/sessionId/JSONL/workspace/context all surviving), so the wrapper reaches idle for real and the AGENT writes its own handoff. The plan's "wait StalledTurnHandoffWait, then stalledTurnStop" is implemented as the stop's OWN `MaxWait`, not a second blocking sleep: adding one would double the reconcile block for nothing. (2) **Every path that cannot get an answer falls back to the same `stalledTurnStop` call with the same arguments - including a plain transport error, not only `ErrProbeUnsupported`.** An unreachable or 5xx-ing wrapper leaves the operator with exactly the evidence the pre-probe operator had permanently, which is none, so it does what that operator did. This makes the fallback single-branched and provably never worse than today, which is the property that lets O2 deploy ahead of the W4 wrapper. (3) **`agent.HasAgentHandoffNoteSince(task, podStartedAt)` - the bare `HasAgentHandoffNote` the plan named would have been a live-fire footgun.** The notes journal is per-TASK and every graceful stop ends by putting a handoff note in it, so a CONTINUATION pod is born into a Task that already carries one; wiring the unscoped predicate as a stop trigger kills every replacement pod at boot, forever. Caught by the mutation check, not by reading. (4) The three `tatara.dev/stall-probe-*` annotations join the four turn annotations in `clearTurnAnnotations`, because a NEW wrapper 404s an UNKNOWN probeId exactly as an OLD wrapper 404s the route - so a probeId that outlived its pod silently downgrades the next pod to the pre-probe fallback for the rest of its life (#566's shape with a new key). Also fixed O1's deliberate residual: `PollOnce` evaluated the stall predicate BEFORE the activity refresh and `continue`d, so an operator restarting mid-subagent-run judged the turn on annotations nobody had updated - the exact case O1's subagent-awareness was built for. RESIDUAL, accepted: an agent that writes a handoff note in EVERY pod rotates pods at boot cadence rather than at TTL cadence. Uncharged to the recreation budget on purpose (a TTL rotation is not charged either), visible as `action=agent_requested_stop`, and covered by O3's `operator_pod_recreations_total` churn alert.

- 2026-08-09 (O1 stall observability, the operator could not see a subagent and so could not see a healthy agent) **`refreshLastActivity` stamped `turn-last-activity-at` from `GET /v1/messages/{turnId}`.lastActivityAt alone, which tails only the PARENT transcript - and a parent blocked on a `Task` tool call writes nothing to it while its child works: one measured run went silent for 2095 seconds (35 minutes) against a 1800s stall window, with a completely healthy agent underneath.** The poll backstop now also reads `GET /v1/session`.lastSubagentActivityAt and stamps the LATER of the two, so each 30s pass re-bases the deadline off subagent work. `SessionInfo.LastSubagentActivityAt` / `OutstandingSubagentCalls` are POINTERS and that is the load-bearing decision, not a style choice: an old wrapper omits both, and plain values would decode as "no activity ever" / "no subagents running" - two claims the old wrapper never made, and exactly the false-idle reading being fixed. Same shape on the wire surface: 404/405 from `/v1/probe`, `/v1/probe/{id}`, `/v1/interrupt` maps to the sentinel `ErrProbeUnsupported` so an operator rolled ahead of its wrapper falls back verbatim; every other status stays a real error so a BROKEN new wrapper is never silently downgraded to "old". **ContractVersion deliberately STAYS 4**: the endpoints are purely additive and the absence is handled, so bumping would drag tatara-cli along and burn the +-1 skew window on a change that breaks nothing, leaving the next genuine break with no room - #544 with the pins swapped. Known residual, accepted for this phase: `PollOnce` evaluates `isTurnTimedOut` BEFORE the refresh and `continue`s, so a turn whose annotation is ALREADY past the window (operator restart mid-subagent-run) does not get the subagent refresh on that pass; steady state is unaffected because the 30s cadence keeps the annotation fresh, and O2's probe-before-kill removes the residual entirely. Also split `operator_agent_pod_ttl_expired_total` with a `cause` label (`ttl|stall|eviction|idle`) - it counted a routine hourly rotation and a hung-agent kill as the same event, so the series could not say which it was looking at - and added `operator_pod_recreations_total{project,kind}`, counted at the ATTEMPT including the one that exhausts the budget, because it is the input to the alert that replaces the `maxPodRecreations` cap in O3 and a loop that ends in a park is exactly the shape whose last attempt must not go missing.

- 2026-08-09 (#523, dedup key needed category but not from CommonLabels) **`incidentDedupKey` (project+alertname only, per #398's WP3 fix) was collapsing genuinely unrelated incidents onto one tracker - #523's own refire history carries category=`memory_inconsistent`, `workspace_broken`, and `directive_contradiction`, three different root causes, all landing on the same issue.** The naive fix (hash `CommonLabels["category"]` in) would reintroduce #398 verbatim: #398's own thread shows category=`tool_error` co-firing alongside category=`other` under ONE alertname, which drops `category` out of Grafana's CommonLabels intersection for that evaluation even though the rule identity hasn't changed - so hashing CommonLabels-sourced category churns the key exactly like the `severity` example in `TestIncidentDedupKey`'s `398_common_label_member_set_churn_collapse` case. The fix instead reads `category` per-item (`GrafanaAlertItem.Labels`, never `CommonLabels`) and requires unanimity across every co-firing instance (`alertCategory`); a mixed batch resolves to `""` (no category component), matching pre-fix behaviour rather than picking an arbitrary, order-dependent instance. Consequence accepted as one-time and correct: every ALREADY-OPEN tracker's key changes (rule-only -> rule+category), so on the next firing each stops matching its old tracker and opens a fresh one - the old trackers were already meaningless dedup buckets straddling multiple root causes, so re-keying is the actual fix, not a side effect to work around.

- 2026-08-09 (#570, THE CEILING WAS ONLY EVER HALF A CEILING - creation is gated now, counting is not) **`LiveHasRoom` had five callers and none on the mint path, so the ceiling bounded RESUMPTION and left CREATION unbounded - which makes a queued human reply the lowest-priority thing in the project.** Measured on project-mtg with an effective ceiling of 1: `mt-i-mtg-decks-24` entered a live state at 11:13:35 with `-23` already live, while `-22`/`-24` sat parked `awaiting-human` holding the human's answers as non-bot `pendingEvents` and were refused 21 times each with `decline=no-live-room`, unanswered for ~55 minutes; `enforceLivePodCeiling` could not converge it because both live Tasks were mid-turn and `evictionCandidates` excludes those. The fix is `liveMintBudget` (`livepods.go`), consulted by `admit()` at the ONE place a QueuedEvent becomes a Task, `ceiling - live - pending - owed`. **The entry above from earlier today said a real fix meant changing what the ceiling COUNTS (live pods rather than live-state Tasks) across five call sites, and called it NOT DONE. That is still not done and is still not needed** - the asymmetry was never in the predicate, it was in which paths asked it. `pending` (minted Tasks still at `new`) is load-bearing: without it two admit passes seconds apart both read `live==0` and both mint. `owed` is the fairness term and the difference between fixing the count and fixing the outage: a gate that only asked "is there room" would let a fresh mint take the slot the instant a conversation ended, because the dispatcher is watch-driven and `driveUnparks` is paced - so a queued human reply RESERVES its slot. **`owed` asks the REAL predicate (`stage.Unpark` on a DeepCopy with `LiveHasRoom=false`, insisting on `DeclineNoLiveRoom` specifically), exactly as `unparkFires` does, and that is the whole wedge-safety argument**: a cheap "parked + live state + non-bot event" approximation would also match a park that can never re-enter for another reason (rounds exhausted, MR merged, issues all closed) and would reserve a live slot FOREVER, silently stopping the project taking any work. `admitTicket` stays ungated - its Task is already live and would count itself into permanent starvation - and `stage.Enter`, `submit_outcome` and triage stay ungated because the agent has already done the work by then.

- 2026-08-09 (#561, THE INVERTED-IDLENESS COMMENT - do not "fix" this back) **`conversationIdleSince` returning `time.Time{}` for a Task with no `ConversationLastEventAt` was defended in its own doc comment ("it has never had an event and is the least likely to be mid-exchange"), and that reasoning is exactly backwards for a Task created seconds ago.** The zero time cannot distinguish an ANCIENT conversation nobody ever replied to from one that has not had TIME to produce an event, and `sortByIdleThenName` is ascending, so the never-stamped Task sorted FIRST and the newest work in the project was evicted before anything else. The fallback is now `ConversationLastEventAt` -> `StateEnteredAt` -> `CreationTimestamp`, three claims of NEWNESS, weakest last. The argument is written out at length above the function precisely because the old comment reads plausible and someone will otherwise revert it. **BUT NOTE THE NIL BRANCH IS UNREACHABLE IN PRODUCTION** and that is the more important half: `stage.stampEnter` is the ONLY writer of `status.state` and it stamps `ConversationLastEventAt` on entry into every live state, so a live Task always carries one. The fix is correct and defensive; it is not what was evicting Tasks.

- 2026-08-09 (#561, WHAT WAS ACTUALLY EVICTING TASKS - unstarted is not idle) **the eviction victims were picked with a perfectly good idle stamp: their OWN, seconds old.** Counted off Loki for the 2026-08-08 eviction storm: 74 `live_evicting` lines, median lag from the victim's own `idle_since` to its eviction **53.5 seconds**, 38 of the 74 inside a minute. `mt-r-tatara-cli-97-*` was created 06:43:57.933, went `new -> awaiting-review` at .941, was admitted 06:43:58.339 and evicted 06:44:00.382 - **2.45 seconds old, never having held a turn** - logged as "the longest-idle conversation" with `idle_since=06:43:57Z`, its own state-entry instant. A seconds-old stamp is simply the oldest thing on offer in a project whose live set is itself churning. `evictionCandidates` now excludes any Task with `status.stateWorkStartedAt == nil` (the POD-READY stamp, cleared by `stampEnter` on every state entry) alongside the existing `stage.TurnInFlight` exclusion: no turn 0 was ever submitted, so there is no conversation to end and eviction reclaims no warmth - it destroys the newest work plus the admission slot the queue granted one second earlier, and the Task re-queues for the pod it never got, so the ceiling churns instead of converging. It cannot wedge: unstarted is an eternal fact about the past (a Task never becomes unstarted again), and both exits are already bounded - the pod becomes ready, or clock 1 parks it `admission-starved`. **The mid-turn guard was NOT what left the new Task as the only candidate**, which was the obvious hypothesis and is wrong: the same logs show the operator held ZERO turns for the whole eviction window (a 2.95s cascade expired all 44 of them nine seconds before the first eviction), so every live Task was a candidate. `live_evict_skipped` fires only later, 06:50:35 onwards, once turns were resubmitted - and `incident-qe-71d3a99079311014-dq96g`, evicted ~30 times in a retry loop, was never targeted again after it submitted a turn at 06:47:02, which is the mid-turn guard demonstrably working.

- 2026-08-09 (#561, THE CEILING OVERSHOOT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A RACE - and its measured magnitude was an already-fixed error loop) **`live=3 ceiling=2` needs no race to explain: `stage.Enter` is the only writer of `status.state`, it takes no ceiling input, and entering a live state is exactly what `countLive` counts.** `LiveHasRoom` gates only the webhook (`pending_events`), the un-park paths (`unpark.go`, `reaper.go`) and `ownership_redeliver` - every FORWARD transition into `refined`/`under-implementation`/`awaiting-review`, whether from triage in `transition.go` or from an agent's `submit_outcome` in `restapi/outcome.go`, consults nothing, and must not: the agent has already submitted, so refusing the edge wedges the Task. The one chokepoint that CAN refuse, `DispatcherReconciler.admitTicket`, is useless here because the Task is already live by the time its ticket is admitted, so gating it on `countLive` would count the Task against itself and starve it forever. A real fix means changing what the ceiling COUNTS - live PODS (`podStartedAt != nil`) rather than live-state Tasks - which is a semantic change to a predicate whose own doc comment warns that a cleverer version "stops bounding pods at all: a cost blowout with no error and no counter anywhere", across five `LiveHasRoom` call sites. NOT DONE, deliberately. **The magnitudes in the logs (`live=7`, `live=31` against `ceiling=2`) are not admission overshoot at all:** 37 of 38 `live_evict_error` lines on 08-08 are two Tasks whose synthetic handoff note carried `agent: "clarify"`, which the Task CRD enum rejected, so `liveHandoffAndPark` errored, `maxLivePodEvictionsPerPass=1` meant the pass evicted nothing, and the same victim was re-selected against a frozen `idle_since` for minutes. #540 already fixed that by re-admitting `clarify` to `notes[].agent` as a historical record - its own doc comment names this exact symptom ("That broke enforce-live-pod-ceiling into a reconcile error loop").

- 2026-08-09 (memory-stack hardening) **the pinned lightrag digest `sha256:67ccf8d9f74eb29da872bf8b3e6513605f5ac601fb0509c5b0ca16d98d2d307d` IS `ghcr.io/hkuds/lightrag:v1.4.16`** - resolved by pulling tag manifests from ghcr and matching `docker-content-digest`. That mattered because #502's readinessProbe now targets `GET /documents/status_counts`, which upstream only added around v1.4.5; on an older image it would 404 and leave every lightrag permanently NotReady. Verified at that exact tag: router prefix `/documents`, no path or query params, and unauthenticated (auth short-circuits at `utils_api.py` "Acept all request if no API protection needed" because `LIGHTRAG_API_KEY` and `AUTH_ACCOUNTS` are both unset in `lightragEnv`). Note the path is NOT on LightRAG's default `WHITELIST_PATHS` (`/health,/api/*`), so if an API key is ever introduced the probe needs an `X-API-Key` header rather than a widened whitelist - widening it also re-opens `POST /api/chat`.

- 2026-08-09 (memory-stack hardening) **liveness on lightrag must stay on `/health`, not the data-plane path, and that is a deliberate asymmetry with readiness.** The #502 wedge was TRIGGERED by a ~2h CNPG outage and outlived it. No failure budget separates "Postgres is down for two hours" from "this process is permanently wedged", so a data-plane liveness probe would restart-loop lightrag through every Postgres outage - the same anti-pattern tatara-memory removed from its own startup path in tatara-memory#103. Draining endpoints is the answer to a dead dependency; restarting is the answer to a dead process.

- 2026-08-09 (memory-stack hardening) **`tatara-memory` is NOT multi-replica safe, so #528's `replicas`/PDB half was deliberately not shipped.** Read at tatara-memory `0835385`: the analytics recompute worker coordinates only through an in-process `sync.Mutex` + `inflight` map (`internal/codegraph/worker.go:77,160`) and claims work with a bare `SELECT` over dirty repos (`analytics_store.go:13`), so two replicas recompute the same repo, collide on `code_communities`' `(repo, community)` PK, never clear `dirty=true`, and repeat every 30s forever - each paying duplicate OpenAI labelling calls. `ResetRunningItems` (`internal/ingest/pgstore.go:360`) is global and unfiltered, so one replica's 5-minute `periodicResume` flips the other's in-flight items back to `pending`. The per-process pool budget (`config.go:359`) has no notion of replica count. What IS already safe: the ingest item claim is `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, and job state is entirely in Postgres, so `GET /ingest-jobs/{id}` answers correctly from either replica. Fixing this belongs in tatara-memory (advisory lock on the two singleton loops + an age filter on `ResetRunningItems`), not here. Separately: a PDB at `replicas=1` is either a permanent drain block (`minAvailable:1`) or a no-op (`maxUnavailable:1`), so it buys nothing until that lands.

- 2026-08-09 (memory-stack hardening) **CNPG `spec.startDelay` is a KILL budget, not a patience budget, and the kill is destructive.** CNPG derives the postgres startup probe's `failureThreshold` as `ceiling(startDelay/10)` (`cluster_types.go:365`), so an instance that cannot finish startup is killed on that period and each kill restarts crash recovery from the beginning - which is why mem-mtg-pg-1's first-probe-failure to first-kill gap was exactly 3600s and the loop ran 22 times without converging. Any finite budget with restart-from-scratch semantics can fail to converge; the point of raising it to 6h is to stop the operator's own render from GUARANTEEING that, and to stop shredding the `--previous` logs hourly. `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` is set at the Cluster (not just in tatara-memory's pgx runtime params) because LightRAG's asyncpg pool sets none of these and is equally capable of parking a transaction.

- 2026-08-08 (MR6, THE RULING) **the #521 migration is WITHDRAWN and the state reset is ACCEPTED. `internal/migrate` is deleted whole, along with its `cmd/manager` wiring and the refuse-to-start gate.** The finding below stands and is the reason: CRD structural pruning applies on the READ path, so once the narrowed CRD is served no GET returns `status.stage` on any object and a migrator has nothing to read. The maintainer ruled against every remedy that would have made it readable - no `PreserveUnknownFields`, no `LegacyStage*` fields, no second release, no v1alpha2. Every one of the 129 live Tasks therefore comes back STATELESS and re-derives through the ordinary create edge in `reconcileStage`. That is now the INTENDED behaviour, not the H5 defect: the four MEMORY entries below it about migrator classification (B1 fresh-vs-unreadable, B2 conflict-retry replay, the migration census, the retention anchors) all describe DELETED CODE and are kept only as the record of how the ruling was reached.

- 2026-08-08 (MR6, THE ONE GUARD ON THE RESET) **the reset must not resurrect terminal work, and `internal/controller/reset_guard.go:terminalResetTarget` is the only thing standing between it and 8 delivered + 9 rejected Tasks re-entering the live lifecycle** - which would reopen their issues, respawn agents and redo merge requests that already landed. It runs ON THE CREATE EDGE (inside `reconcileStage`'s `Status.State == ""` branch), not as a startup pass: idempotent for free because stamping a state closes that branch permanently, cannot block startup because a failure is a requeue rather than a CrashLoopBackOff, reads the mirrors through the cache this reconciler already uses, and has NO CUTOVER WINDOW - a Task that arrives stateless a week later from a restored backup is guarded identically. `stage.Transitions` gained `(create) -> done` and `(create) -> rejected` for it (24 edges -> 26), because `stage.Enter` is the only writer of `status.state`. It deliberately does NOT fire `operator_task_terminal_total`: `TaskTerminalEntry` drops `from == ""` edges, and a one-off spike of 17 fake terminals would poison the 29 observability rules that divide by that counter.

- 2026-08-08 (MR6, WHAT THE GUARD KEYS ON, and why each signal is trustworthy) **only fields that SURVIVE pruning, and each one has exactly one production writer.** `stage`/`stageReason` are pruned; every OTHER status field is still declared and comes back intact, and the owned Issue/MergeRequest mirrors are separate objects under a CRD #521 did not narrow, so nothing about them is pruned at all. (1) `status.deliveredAt` -> `done`: two writers, `CloseIssuesOnDelivery` and `forceDocTimeout`, both setting it in the SAME `FitTask` mutation as `EnterStage(..., done, ...)`; nothing clears it. (2) `status.documentedBy` -> `done`: written only over `batch.Spec.DocumentsTasks`, and `mintDocBatch` builds that list exclusively from Tasks it reads at `State == done`, so its presence proves the operator observed this Task done - independent evidence, since a `refined -> done` finish stamps no `deliveredAt`. (3) the owned Issue mirrors, taken as the UNION of the controller ownerRef and `status.issueRefs` (union is the conservative direction: the guard refuses on a single non-terminal mirror, so seeing more can only move the answer towards "leave it stateless"). Any mirror with `status.status == rejected` -> `rejected(declined)`, because `recordProposalDecline` is its only writer and it runs on the issue-closed stop path. Every mirror at `status.status == done` -> `done`, because `CloseIssuesOnDelivery` is its only writer and it is the delivery stamp. Every mirror `state == closed` with no platform verdict -> `rejected(issue-closed)`: a human closed it, which is exactly what `ApplyIssueClosedStop` terminates at. **THE BIAS IS ONE-DIRECTIONAL AND IT IS IN THE CODE:** one open mirror, zero mirrors, or a mirror whose CR is gone all return `("", "")` and the Task stays stateless. Work evidence (`mrRefs`, `mergeCursor`, notes, pod stamps, review counters) is explicitly NOT terminal evidence - it proves the Task was DOING something, never that it FINISHED. Re-triage is noisy and harmless; a wrong terminal strands real work behind a state with no exit.

- 2026-08-08 (MR6, EXPECTED BLAST RADIUS - check this after the deploy) **129 live Tasks. ~108 parked + ~4 others re-triage from scratch; 17 must NOT.** Capture the truth BEFORE `helm upgrade` (afterwards `status.stage` is unreadable forever): `kubectl -n tatara get tasks -o json | jq -r '.items[] | select(.status.stage=="delivered" or .status.stage=="rejected") | .metadata.name' > /tmp/terminal-before.txt` - expect 17 lines. After the rollout settles, the guard held iff every one of them is terminal: `kubectl -n tatara get tasks -o json | jq -r '.items[] | select(.metadata.name as $n | $names | index($n)) | [.metadata.name, .status.state, .status.stateReason] | @tsv' --argjson names "$(jq -R -s -c 'split("\n")[:-1]' /tmp/terminal-before.txt)"` - EVERY row must read `done` or `rejected`; a single `new`/`refined`/`under-implementation` is the guard failing open and resurrecting shipped work. Cross-check the guard's own log, which must show ~17 lines and each name at most once: `kubectl -n tatara logs deploy/tatara-operator | jq -r 'select(.action=="terminal_reset_guard") | [.resource_id, .state, .state_reason] | @tsv'`. PromQL for the same shape: `sum(operator_task_state{state=~"done|rejected"})` >= 17 immediately after rollout (decaying as the reaper drains it - the retention clocks re-anchor at the reset, so 24h for rejected and 48h for done from CUTOVER, not from the original delivery), and `sum(operator_task_state{state="new"})` spiking to ~112 then draining as triage runs. **A SECOND, SEPARATE CONSEQUENCE OF DROPPING THE MIGRATOR:** the 61 live Tasks carrying the deleted `spec.kind=clarify` are no longer rewritten to `implement`. `spec.kind` is immutable and the enum is validated on WRITE, so 1.33 ratcheting lets them keep reading `clarify`; `triageTarget` has no `clarify` row, so each parks ONCE at `triage-stalled` and is reaped by the 7-day park retention. Loud, bounded, deliberately not special-cased - watch `kubectl -n tatara logs deploy/tatara-operator | jq -r 'select(.action=="triage_unknown_kind") | .resource_id' | sort -u | wc -l`, which should approach 61 and then stop.

- 2026-08-07 (MR6 fix round 3, MEASURED against envtest k8s 1.33.0) **BLOCKING: CRD structural pruning is applied on the READ path, not only on write, so the one-shot migrator cannot read `status.stage` at all once the narrowed CRD is served.** The package doc, the design and the plan all rest on the opposite premise, and the second adversarial review made a weaker version of the same assumption ("EVERY status write by the still-running old operator prunes stage off that object"). `internal/migrate/migrate_envtest_test.go:TestNarrowedCRD_PrunesLegacyStatusOnRead` measures it: write `status.stage=reviewing` under a wide schema, narrow the schema, write NOTHING, and the very next GET returns `status: {issueRefs:[...]}` with no `stage`. The object in etcd is untouched and irrelevant - nothing the operator can call returns the field. The consequence is total: every one of the 118 live Tasks reads as "no legacy stage, no new state" on the migrator's first List, so the ones carrying work evidence classify unreadable and the manager refuses to start (permanent CrashLoopBackOff), and the ones carrying only a mint stamp classify fresh and the create edge rewinds them to `new`. Fixes B1 and B2 below are both still correct and both still necessary; neither can reach this, because it is not a classification bug. NOT FIXED HERE: the remedy is an API-contract decision the design doc explicitly ruled on ("status.stage is REMOVED, not aliased") and it is the coordinator's to make. Three candidates, priced in the test's doc comment: `+kubebuilder:pruning:PreserveUnknownFields` on `Task.Status` for one release (cheapest, but it disables pruning for the whole status subtree and the migrator must then clear the legacy keys itself instead of relying on pruning); explicit `LegacyStage`/`LegacyStageReason`/... migration-only fields on `TaskStatus`, cleared by the migrator and deleted alongside `internal/migrate` (the distinct names keep the design's anti-aliasing objection inapplicable); or v1alpha2 plus a conversion webhook, already priced and rejected. **RULED ON 2026-08-08: skip the migration, accept the reset, guard only the terminals. See the three entries above; `internal/migrate` and every remedy priced here are deleted.**
- 2026-08-07 (MR6 fix round 3, B1) **a minted-but-not-started Task is FRESH, not unreadable, and "status is completely empty" only ever covered a sub-second window.** Minting is TWO writes - Create, where the apiserver ignores status entirely, then `stampMintStatus`, a full `Status().Update`. So the STEADY STATE of a Task the still-running old operator sweeps up during the pass is `{issueRefs:[...]}`: non-empty status, no stage, no state. Classifying that unreadable fails the pass, the manager refuses to start, `/readyz` never passes, the old operator is never terminated, and it mints more of them - a permanent loop, not a race. The discriminator is now `mintTimeStatusKeys` = `{issueRefs, mrRefs, humanReviewRounds}`, the COMPLETE set of status fields the old operator writes at mint time (origin/main intake.go:209/:289/:463/:492; `humanReviewRounds` is CARRIED off the MergeRequest CR so it can be non-zero on a Task that never ran a turn). Everything else is evidence of work and still fails the pass, which is what keeps H5's original requirement intact: a Task that did work and lost its stage must never be rewound to `new` by the create edge. The list fails in the safe direction - a status field added later counts as evidence by default, so the error is refusing to start (loud) rather than rewinding (silent).
- 2026-08-07 (MR6 fix round 3, B2) **the migrator's conflict retry replayed a stale plan and could not have done otherwise.** Both writes were wrapped in `RetryOnConflict` and both re-Got, but the status closure Got a TYPED `v1alpha1.Task`, which has no `Stage` field, so re-derivation was structurally impossible; the only re-check (`live.Status.State != ""`) can never be tripped by the old operator, which writes `status.stage`. Concrete loss: a Task at `reviewing` at snapshot time that the old operator drove to `delivered` during the pass got `awaiting-review` stamped over it. Both closures now go through `reDerive`, which re-reads UNSTRUCTURED and re-runs `MapOne`. When the legacy stage has since been pruned, the SNAPSHOT plan is used rather than nothing and the event is logged: the prune destroyed information and created none, and leaving the object stateless hands it to the create edge, which is worse.
- 2026-08-07 (MR6 fix round 3, migration census) **two buckets deviate from the design doc's mapping table, deliberately, and the design line is the one that is wrong.** Both sit inside its 38-Task `parked(awaiting-human)` line ("-> new, or awaiting-review for the 15 kind=review"), which predates the doc's OWN general rule - stated one line below it for the no-outcome / review-loop-exhausted / ownership-lost buckets - that a park maps "per parkedFromStage". Park is orthogonal to state under #521, so the state a Task parked AT is the state it un-parks INTO. The 23 with `parkedFromStage=clarifying` map to `refined`, not `new`: the gate runs at `refined`, and sending them to `new` re-triages a Task already past triage and discards the only position information the object carries. The 15 `kind=review` with `parkedFromStage=(create)` map to `new`, not `awaiting-review`: they never entered a stage, so there is nothing to restore, and `new` re-derives rather than assumes - and it is not a one-way door, because `LegalFor` GUARD 5 keeps `new -> awaiting-review` open for exactly `kind=review`. Pinned in `TestMapOne_CoversTheLiveCensusExactly`.
- 2026-08-07 (MR6 fix round 3, retention anchors) **the retention clocks deliberately start at the migration, and the original anchor is kept on `tatara.dev/migrated-stage-entered-at` instead.** Re-arming `stateEnteredAt`/`parkedAt` off the pre-#521 `stageEnteredAt` is one cheap read but it is not cheap in consequence: measured against the live census it makes 66 of the 118 Tasks due for collection on the FIRST reap pass after boot (9 rejected + 4 failed past the 24h `RejectedRetention`, 7 delivered past the 48h `DeliveredRetention`, 46 non-backlog-sweep parks past the 7d `ParkRetention`), and `ReapTerminal` carries no per-pass budget, so they go in one sweep with a terminal issue comment each. That directly defeats `AnnMigratedFrom`, whose stated job is that a mis-mapped object can be identified after the fact - an object deleted before anyone looks at the migration's output cannot be identified at all. The 52 `parked(backlog-sweep)` anchors are unaffected either way: `reapParked` never ages them out.
- 2026-08-07 (MR6 fix round 3, reason vocabulary) **`status.stateReason` carries NO CRD enum, so an off-vocabulary value is not rejected by the apiserver - it just sits there and the next reader trusts it.** The four live `failed` Tasks carried `pod-recreation-exhausted`, `operator-error` and `merge-blocked`, ALL THREE OF WHICH ARE PARK REASONS, and the migration copied them straight into the reject slot on the very Tasks it deliberately sends to `rejected`. `MapOne` now guards with `stage.IsRejectReason` and falls back to `declined`; the exact original stays on `tatara.dev/migrated-from`, which is where the recovery audit trail belongs.
- 2026-08-07 (MR1 correction, measured against v1.43.0 in the live cluster) **MR1's stranding diagnosis was WRONG and its own new logging disproved it on the first pass.** The claim was that `iss-tatara-operator-510/512/520/523/524` were stranded by DANGLING controller ownerRefs. They were not: all five owning Tasks EXIST and are legitimately `parked(backlog-sweep)`, the new per-issue line reports them as `reason:"issue_owned"` (correct behaviour, not a defect), and `operator_sweep_stale_owner_repaired_total` fired ZERO times across the first full sweep pass. The ownership-liveness fix STAYS EXACTLY AS MERGED - `own.ControllerOwner` genuinely never checks the named Task exists, which is a real code defect worth hardening - but it is hardening, not a diagnosis, and nothing in the observed population was broken that way. The error was a bad premise handed to the MR1 author and its reviewer, not their reading of it: an early `kubectl get tasks | jq 'select(test("521"))'` was read as a full listing and reported 3 Tasks in the namespace when there were 118.
- 2026-08-07 (MR1 correction, the real cause) **the stranding is the PER-PASS MINT BUDGET crossed with the 4h per-repo scan stagger, and MR1's logging named it verbatim on its first pass:** `"resource_id":"iss-tatara-operator-502" "reason":"mint_budget_bound"`, same for `503` and `iss-tatara-observability-93`, in a pass logging `minted_triaging=1` + `minted_parked=4` = 5 = `maxNew` exactly. `reposDueForScan` phase-shifts each repo across a 4h `issueScan` period, so a backlog drains at roughly five issues per repo-window and everything under the cut line is deferred indefinitely. MR1's earlier entry recorded the creation budget as "ruled out from the live cluster"; it was not ruled out, and that line has been corrected in place.
- 2026-08-07 (MR1 correction, STILL OPEN) **the throughput ceiling is unfixed.** MR1 made it VISIBLE (the per-issue skip reason plus `operator_sweep_orphan_stranded_seconds`) and deliberately did not raise it. Three candidates, none chosen: raise `maxNewTasksPerSweep`; make the budget prefer the OLDEST stranded orphan rather than forge-listing order, since the cut line is currently arbitrary; or requeue immediately when a pass ends budget-bound with orphans still outstanding instead of waiting out the next 4h window. Let the stranded-seconds gauge collect data before picking - the choice turns on whether the backlog is bursty or steady, and nothing measured that until now.
- 2026-08-07 (#521 review, the idle clock) **THE IDLE CLOCK BOUNDS A CONVERSATION, NOT AGENT WORK, and the discriminator is whether a TURN IS IN FLIGHT.** Promoting `conversing`'s 60m idle clock to all three live states deleted the WORK budget each of them had (implementing 6h, reviewing 4h), and `Status.ConversationLastEventAt` has exactly three writers of which only one - `AppendTaskEvent`, on a HUMAN webhook comment - ever moves it during work. A silently-working implement agent therefore parked at `awaiting-human` after 60 minutes and needed a human comment or aged out at 7d. `ArmedClock` now returns `ClockNone` for a live state while `stage.TurnInFlight` is true, and otherwise measures from the LATEST of `ConversationLastEventAt`, `StateWorkStartedAt` and the turn-complete annotation. The pod-ready term is the ADMISSION half of the same bug: a Task that queued three hours entered its pod with a three-hour-old conversation stamp and parked on the first reconcile after pod-ready, before turn 0 was ever submitted. THE RATCHET THIS ADMITS, recorded rather than discovered later: both extending terms are re-stamped by a pod ROTATION, so an ABANDONED conversation on a project whose `agentPodTTLSeconds` is at or below its `conversationIdleMinutes` (the DEFAULTS are both 3600) can rotate a pod instead of parking, repeatedly. Bounded three ways - `ResidencyExceeded`, `maxTurnsPerTask` (every rotation costs a turn 0) and `maxPodRecreations` - so it cannot run forever, but it is a cost regression against the old `conversing` park and the honest fix is pod-TTL headroom over the idle window, which is a design change and was NOT taken here. REJECTED the alternative of restoring a per-state WORK budget: for the three live states that table would have been 24h/6h/4h, value-for-value identical to `residencyCaps`, leaving two clocks measuring nearly the same thing with different carry semantics. The two bounds now COMPOSE instead: the idle clock fires when the state is QUIET, `ResidencyExceeded` when it has been LONG, and at most one is armed at a time. `internal/stage` reads the two turn ANNOTATIONS to do this and that does not make it impure - they are declared in `api/v1alpha1` beside the status fields; `controller.taskHasInflightTurn` delegates to `stage.TurnInFlight` so the dispatcher and the clock can never disagree about whether an agent is working.
- 2026-08-07 (#521 review, the pod-side half) **`agent.PodTTLSeconds` takes the MAX of the project TTL and the conversation idle window for a live state, never the substitution.** `conversing` substituted, and #521 widened the substitution - which rotated an implement pod every idle window, MID-TURN, with no completed turn to hand off from, so the stop force-deleted it. Fixing only the Task-level idle clock would have been cosmetic: the pod died at 60m either way. The substitution only ever bought "an idle conversation's pod does not outlive the park about to tear it down", and the park tears it down regardless, so a LONGER pod TTL costs nothing on the idle path. The idle window is still taken when it is the longer of the two, which is the original conflict the function was written for.
- 2026-08-07 (#521 review, the pause) **`ResidencyExceeded` honours `paused`, and it is the WORST clock to leave unguarded.** The backstop ran before `ArmedClock` with no pause guard, and every live residency cap (24h/6h/4h) is at or below the 24h admission budget - so it DOMINATED the pause carve-out that exists precisely so the kill switch is not a backlog shredder. Pausing a project for longer than four hours parked every `awaiting-review` Task at `stage-deadline`, which is `UnparkNever`, so the backlog aged out at ParkRetention and was reaped. The two admission cases deleted from `TestReconcilerAppliesTheThreeClocks` are restored. Admission-starved really IS unreachable for a live state through the full reconciler now (residency is checked first and its caps are lower) - that part of the deleted note was correct; the pause part was not.
- 2026-08-07 (#521 review, LegalFor) **`under-implementation -> done` and `new -> awaiting-review` were CALLER-GATED, which is not gated.** Both edges' Trigger prose names a kind (documentation batch, kind=review) and only `triageTarget` and the outcome handler happened to honour it; guards 4 and 5 move the rule onto the edge where the other three already live. Same pass: `triageTarget` had a `documentation` row returning `under-implementation`, which is NOT in the table's `new` row, so a documentation Task reaching triage would have errored on every reconcile forever. The row is DELETED rather than made legal - docbatch.go mints the nightly batch at `under-implementation` through the CREATE edge and it never sees triage - and the no-row branch parks at `triage-stalled` instead. Also `stage.Enter` now refuses every pseudo-target: `Reap` is a real `To` in the table (done and rejected both carry it), so `Legal(done, Reap)` was TRUE and `Enter` would have stamped `status.state = "(reap)"`.
- 2026-08-07 (feat/521-lifecycle-and-agent-merge, #521) **status.stage/stageReason DELETED, not aliased.** Re-pointing `StageParked` would have let a site keep comparing against a value that can never match; the deletion is what made `go build` enumerate every production call site. `TaskDone` is now EXACTLY `{done, rejected}`: `TaskDone(parked)==true` IS the #521 bug, because it skipped `intake.createTaskRaceSafe`'s live-twin branch so the Create 409ed and control reached the delete, deleting a live Task and cascading its owned Issue and MergeRequest mirrors. `resume.go` was first deleted ENTIRELY (291 lines) on the reasoning that `TaskDone(parked)==false` let the sweep's ordinary re-mint cover it. **THAT WAS WRONG and the review caught it:** a parked Task RETAINS controller ownership, so `resolveLiveIssueOwner` finds a live owner, `IsOrphanIssue` answers `issue_owned`, and nothing re-mints for the full seven days of ParkRetention - by which time the human's reply is gone from pendingEvents and they must comment a SECOND time. It is RESTORED, and the restoration is honest about what it is: an EARLY REAP plus re-mint, not an un-park. The old bug is fixed at the source instead - the funnel returns a typed `MintOutcome` rather than a `created bool`, so `resumeOne` can tell a re-mint from a tombstone delete. The fix cannot live at the intake/sweep seam: the fresh mint's `IntakeTaskName` is the name the parked owner already holds, `TaskDone(parked)` is false, so `createTaskRaceSafe` takes its live-twin branch, returns `MintExistingLive`, and `repairIssueBinding` binds the Issue straight back to the parked Task. The old Task has to GO before a fresh one can exist, and no seam predicate can make it go.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, the parkReason wedge) **parkReason is a stringly flag and a writer that sets State and forgets to clear it wedges a Task forever with a stale reason** - the same silent-drift genre as #521 itself. ONE mitigation: `stage.Enter` returns `*StillParkedError` on every non-park edge while parked, and `stage.Unpark` is the only function that clears the flag. `stage.UnparkTakeover` is the ONE documented exception that clears it AND moves state, because a re-taken MR resumes at `merged` rather than wherever the ownership flip caught it; it validates against its OWN two-target allow-list plus GUARD 1, deliberately NOT the transition table, so `under-implementation -> merged` cannot leak to any other caller.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, the live-pod ceiling) **the predicate is `live(state) && parkReason == ""` and BOTH clauses are load-bearing.** The failure mode is SILENT UNDER-COUNTING: `merged -> awaiting-review` is a legal edge, so a Task can re-enter a live state from an operator-driven one, and any predicate cleverer than these two clauses misses it and stops bounding pods entirely - a cost blowout with no error and no counter. Also fixed `operator_conversing_entry_declined_total`'s reason label, which was the literal `unresolved` on all 27 declines in the live cluster; the replacement `operator_live_entry_declined_total` has a closed five-member vocabulary (`controller.LiveEntryDeclineReasons`) and a test that none of them is `unresolved`.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, the residency bound) **generalising liveness replaces a live state's WORK clock with an IDLE clock, so `under-implementation` lost the 6h absolute bound `implementing` had.** `stage.ResidencyExceeded` is a SEPARATE check in `reconcileClocks`, not a second return from `ArmedClock` - `ArmedClock` returns exactly one clock by construction and that is what makes the model auditable. It reads `StateElapsedSeconds`, so it is cumulative across a park round trip; a fresh cap per re-entry is the unbounded-loop shape #480 killed. Consequence: `stage.Park` now folds this state's residency into `StageElapsedCarrySeconds` on EVERY park reason, not just the two timeouts, and `Unpark` always preserves it. Only a genuine state TRANSITION launders it.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, the transition table) **23 edges, not the plan's 21.** `refined -> done` is required because brainstorm, refine and incident finish without opening an MR, so `awaiting-review -> done` and `deployed -> done` are both unreachable for them. `under-implementation -> done` and `(create) -> under-implementation` are the 22nd and 23rd, and the plan missed both because it never traced the nightly DOCUMENTATION BATCH: it is MINTED straight into implementation work (no driving issue to triage, no approval to gate - a nightly batch is the operator's own decision, already made) and it FINISHES at `done(doc-timeout)` when it declines or its 2h budget elapses, having opened no MR. Neither `(create) -> refined` nor `refined -> done` reaches it. Both were caught by tests, not by reading.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, spec.kind) **the migrator rewrites 61 Tasks' spec.kind from clarify to implement, which required ADDING `implement` to the Spec.Kind enum and to `unconstrainedKinds`** - it was never a valid ORIGIN kind. The real breakage was never the outcome kind-match gate: `status.agentKind` is EMPTY on all 118 live Tasks (every one is in a pod-less stage), so nothing could 409. It is that `spec.kind=clarify` has no state to route into out of `new`. `triageTarget` therefore accepts `implement` now, which is NOT a hole in the approval gate: `refined` is where the gate RUNS, and the only edge out of it into `under-implementation` is the one restapi grants.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, UnparkClass) **DEVIATION FROM THE PLAN, and the reason is the counter.** The plan put eleven park reasons in `UnparkTimer`, including the eight that used to target `failed` (operator-error, object-too-large, triage-stalled, agent-contract-mismatch, merge-order-missing, admission-starved, merge-auth-refused, fold-adoption-unverified). NONE has a bounding counter and there is no CRD field to hold one, so classifying them as timer-retriable is either a lie or an unbounded retry loop. They stay `UnparkNever`, which reproduces today's behaviour exactly: they age out at ParkRetention and the sweep re-mints the still-open issue as a NEW Task. `UnparkTimer` is the three that carry a real bound: merge-timeout, deploy-timeout, no-outcome. At cap a timer un-park RE-PARKS with merge-blocked / deploy-blocked (both `UnparkNever`) rather than entering the deleted `failed`.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, the migration) **118 Tasks, measured 52/38/5/2/1/9/7/4, migrated in one synchronous pass before `mgr.Start()`. If it cannot complete the manager REFUSES TO START** - a mixed population is the silent-drift shape that produced #521. Restart mid-migration is a non-event: idempotent per object, keyed on `status.state != ""`. It reads the old fields UNSTRUCTURED because the Go type no longer has them, and `MapOne` is a pure function so the 16-bucket census is testable with no cluster. `failed -> rejected` is a DELIBERATE deviation SCOPED TO THE MIGRATION ONLY: those four are past their useful life with retention clocks running, and resurrecting a merge-blocked Task re-arms work against stale MRs. The maintainer's `failed -> new/refined` rule governs FUTURE failures.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, the gate) **`approvingMaintainer` is a BOUND CROSS-CHECK, never a second authority.** `approver-not-maintainer` and `approver-mismatch` are its own refusals instead of collapsing into `citation-not-maintainer`, and BOTH ARE GATED ON `declared != ""`: `autoApproveTataraProposals` is live on two of three Projects and releases a bot-authored proposal with NO human comment at all, so ungated they would refuse every auto-approved proposal. `approvingMaintainer` and `approvalCitations` travel as a PAIR (both, or neither); `planNoteId` stays unconditionally required because the plan pin is orthogonal to who approved. A refusal is a 200 `{granted,reason,declared}` and DOES NOT PARK: under the merged model the agent is alive and should be told no and keep talking, where the old clarify pod was dead after its one turn.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, NOT doing) No recency rule on the citation (an EARLIER approving comment must stay citable or ordinary threads deadlock). No approver!=author rule (the reporter is the only maintainer on most issues here; the bot-identity exclusion transfers, the human one does not). `author_association` DEFERRED (server-computed and unforgeable, genuinely stronger, but it defends against maintainerLogins drift which cannot occur in a one-name list). **SELECTIVE QUOTING REMAINS OPEN:** "go ahead" is a substring of "do not go ahead until CI is green", and plan-pinning closes approve-then-swap-scope, a DIFFERENT attack. The mitigation is DETECTION, not prevention: the operator posts one confirmation comment naming the approver and quoting what was cited, so a bypass is visible on the thread within minutes. It is bot-authored, so the enqueue filter drops it and it can never wake the Task that produced it.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, the plan pin) `Task.status.pinnedPlanNoteId` is a NEW field the plan did not name, and the 50-note spill is why it exists: notes are capped with drop-oldest and spilled to tatara-memory, so a long-running Task can spill the very plan note the gate pinned and `plan-note-missing` would fire on a legitimate submit. The ID alone is retained; the pin the gate re-checks is the sha256 on `Issue.status.approval.planHash` at GRANT, so nothing has to survive the spill for the check to work. `planPinRefusal` fails OPEN on a read error and on a spilled note: refusing on drift you cannot prove would bounce every submit during an apiserver blip.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, SweepIssueKind) **`SweepIssueKind` moved from "clarify" to "implement", which CHANGES `IntakeTaskName`'s deterministic name for every future sweep-minted issue Task** - the kind is both the `mt-<c|i>-` head segment and an input to the sha256 suffix. The 52 live backlog Tasks keep their old `mt-c-...` names and are NOT renamed (a rename is delete + create, which cascades their owned Issue and MergeRequest mirrors - exactly the population MR1 exists to stop manufacturing). Nothing breaks, and the reason is `resolveLiveOwner`: the old-named Task still CONTROLLER-OWNS its Issue CR, so `IsOrphanIssue` answers `SweepSkipIssueOwned` and the sweep never re-mints it under the new name. Do not "fix" this by renaming.
- 2026-08-07 (#521, observability) `operator_conversing_*` renamed to `operator_live_*`; `operator_task_stage`/`_stage_age_seconds` to `operator_task_state`/`_state_age_seconds`; `operator_task_terminal_total`'s `{stage,stageReason}` to `{state,stateReason}` and its state label narrowed to `done|rejected`; `operator_task_parked_total`'s to `{state,parkReason}` and it is now the ONLY counter of a stall, since a park is no longer a terminal transition. New: `operator_task_residency_exceeded_total{state,kind}` and `operator_task_parked_with_live_pod_repaired_total{project,park_reason}`. **tatara-observability's deployed alert rules still key on the OLD stage vocabulary and WILL break** - a sixth repo, out of scope for this landing, already open at ROADMAP.md:39.
- 2026-08-08 (#521, review round, the plan pin) **The pin's two halves resolved the note DIFFERENTLY and that was the whole defect.** The grant took `planNoteId` from the agent and ran it through `findNote`, which matches on ID ALONE; the re-check resolved `status.pinnedPlanNoteId`, the newest note of kind `plan`. So a cited handoff/turn note was hashed AS the approved plan (the pin then guards an artifact nobody approved), and a cited SUPERSEDED plan note pinned a hash the re-check could never reproduce (drift reported on a plan nobody touched). Both are now one call, `planNote(t)` -> `findPlanNote(t, pinnedPlanNoteID(t))`, used verbatim at grant and at re-check; the declared `planNoteId` is demoted to a DECLARATION THAT MUST AGREE, exactly like `approvingMaintainer` against the citation, refused as `plan-note-not-plan` (vocabulary is now 11). **`findPlanNote` also refuses an EMPTY id, and that was a live second bug:** `agentNote` appends the operator's own notes with NO id, so `findNote(t, "")` matched the first of them - any Task with an empty `pinnedPlanNoteId` re-hashed an operator note on every submit and bounced back to `refined` forever. Do not re-derive either half locally; that divergence is the bug.
- 2026-08-08 (#521, review round, the mint) **`MintParked` replaces `parkAtMint`: the create edge's state and `Spec.InitialParkReason` are ONE status write.** #521 split them (`r.enter` then `r.parkAtMint`), which is the same non-atomicity annotations were rejected for - a crash, conflict or eviction between the two leaves the Task LIVE with no park reason, and a live Task gets worked. The sweep mints 52 `parked(backlog-sweep)` owners through this edge, so the window was 52 agent pods that were never meant to spawn. `EnterStage`'s body is now `enterStage`, shared, with an optional `parkReason` applied by `stage.Park` INSIDE the one `FitTask` mutation, after `stage.Enter` (Enter refuses a parked Task, so the order within the closure is still load-bearing). D1's mint exclusion is preserved STRUCTURALLY rather than by passing nil metrics: `enterStage` never emits the park counter, and `MintParked` refuses any Task whose `status.state != ""`, so no genuine park can reach it and go uncounted.
- 2026-08-08 (#521, review round, Enter's reasons) `stage.Enter` now validates `reason` against the TARGET's vocabulary (`IsRejectReason` / `IsDoneReason`) instead of the 36-member global set, which had accepted a park-class reason as a `rejected` or `done` stateReason. **It is scoped to those two states on purpose and the other six keep the global check:** `stage.CIRed`'s re-implement edge really does enter `under-implementation` with `ci-red`, a PARK-vocabulary reason on a live state, so "a non-terminal takes no reason" would be a rule this machine does not follow. That `stateReason=ci-red` write contradicts the CRD field doc ("It is NOT a park reason") and is write-only - every production reader of `StateReason` reads it on a terminal (`issue_controller` on `rejected(issue-closed)`, `docbatch` on `done(doc-timeout)`) - but it was NOT touched here.

- 2026-07-28 (fix/merge-cursor-ci-red-guard, #476, review-fix round) **Four findings on the red-CI gate below, all real, all fixed.** (1) CRITICAL: the gate's error return in `reconcileClocks` ran BEFORE the HandoffDeadline check and BEFORE `stage.ArmedClock`, and returned `handled=true` - a persistent `GetPRState` failure (revoked token, forge 5xx) would have wedged the Task in `reviewing` holding its admitted concurrency ticket, reaching NO F.4 clock, inside the one function whose contract is "no stage without an exit deadline", and with the one-shot `advanceAfterReview` already dead on the same error. **The two gate sites are now explicitly asymmetric: the merging-side gate is the guard (it sits on a 60s requeue and cannot wedge), the reviewing-side one is an OPTIMISATION and FAILS OPEN.** A blip there costs one pointless promotion, which the merging gate then undoes within 60s. Do not "harden" it into an error return. (2) `stage.CIRed`'s merged/finished boundary reads `anyMerged(mrs)`, which is a MIRROR read on an HOURLY sweep; `ReconcileMerging` gets the truth free from `stampMerged`, but the reviewing-side callers did not, so an MR merged out of band (the C.9 accepted risk) plus a red sibling routed to `implementing` and would have re-proposed merged code. `ciRedAtReviewedHead` now folds its LIVE `st.Merged` observation onto the in-memory mirror copy and keeps scanning rather than returning on the first red - the merged boundary outranks the red one. (3) `CIRedExitTotal.Inc()` and the exit log fired BEFORE `EnterStage`, which `transition.go` explicitly forbids ("it would count once per retry"); moved after the write, along with `ClearMergeCursorStalled`. (4) The bounce told the implement pod nothing: the bundle renders `mr.status.ciStatus`, the STALE mirror value whose staleness is this gate's entire premise, so three laps would have been three pods guessing. `enterCIRed` now appends an operator note (repo, PR, reviewed SHA, `attempt N of 3`) exactly as the request_changes path appends `reviewBeltNote`; `appendOperatorNote` was split into a driver-free `appendOperatorNoteTo` for it.
- 2026-07-28 (fix/merge-cursor-ci-red-guard, #476) **A review verdict says nothing about CI, and `merging` treated a FAILED check as something that could still resolve.** helmfile!1358 went red at 01:41:30Z, the review approved it at 01:44:21Z and `reviewAdvanceEdge` promoted it into `merging` at 01:44:24Z without ever reading CI; `ReconcileMerging` then re-read the same static failure every 60s (`stallMerge("ci-not-green")`) for the full 4h budget, parked `merge-timeout`, and `driveUnparks` re-entered `merging` 3s later - up to `MaxMergeReentries`, ~16h and four alert firings to re-derive a verdict that was final in three minutes. Fixed with CYCLE 5: `stage.CIRed` + `ReasonCIRed`/`ReasonCIBlocked`, taken from BOTH sides of the promotion (`advanceAfterReview` and its level-triggered twin in `reconcileClocks`, and the merging gate itself), bounded by `Status.CIRedReentries`/`MaxCIRedReentries=3` -> `failed(ci-blocked)`. **Three decisions worth not re-litigating.** (1) The read is LIVE (`GetPRState`), never `mr.status.ciStatus`: the mirror had last synced at 01:41:25Z, FIVE SECONDS before the job failed, so a mirror-based guard would have read `pending` and promoted anyway. (2) ONLY `CIStatus == "failure"` trips it - `""`, `pending` and `running` are not verdicts and still stall on the 60s poll, or every Task bounces on a race with its own CI; a red head that is not the REVIEWED head is also ignored, since that is the head-moved bounce's business. (3) `anyMerged(mrs)` parks `ci-red` instead of re-implementing - the same merged/finished boundary `reenterParkedOnReview` refuses to cross - and `ci-red` is deliberately NOT in `reentryReasons`, because an F.6 re-entry would put the Task straight back on the merge path it just proved it cannot take. **Out of this repo:** #476's action items 4-6 (the `tatara-cd` rules `ffrz33ttsiscgb` at >1h and `efr5wlsjagtmoa` at >2h both fire against a 4h budget, both blame a single-entry `spec.mergeOrder` instead of the operator's own `reason` field, and neither carries a `runbookURL`) live in `tatara-observability`; nothing in this repo renders those rules.
- 2026-07-29 (reingest-schedule-test-clock) **`TestRepoReconcile_ScheduleNoDoubleFireWithinInterval` was a time-of-day landmine that had nothing to do with the PR it reddened: it seeded `lastScheduledReingest = now-1m` against a hard-coded `0 6 * * *` and asserted "not due", which is FALSE for the 60 seconds after 06:00 UTC.** CI run 30426381616 (PR #498) reconciled `sch4` at `06:00:55Z` with the seed at `05:59:55Z`, so the 06:00 boundary genuinely lay inside `(base, now]` and `scheduleNextReingest` correctly fired - the log line `ingest_schedule_fire resource_id=sch4 schedule="0 6 * * *"` at 06:00:55Z is the proof, and the sibling `* * * * *` repos (`sch1`, `audit3repo`, `r2f3s-repo`) fired in the same second as designed. Production code was NOT touched and was never wrong; the diff (`internal/controller/queue_controller.go`, `project_controller.go`, `obs/queue_metrics.go`) does not reach the repository reingest scheduler at all, and #496's "a blocked event schedules nothing" `blockedPoll()==0` path is confined to `DispatcherReconciler`. Fixed by `cronAtOffsetHours(n)` in `repository_controller_test.go`, which derives the fire hour from the current UTC hour: for `n` in [2,22] the fire hour is never the current or the preceding hour, so no boundary can fall inside the seeded window at ANY wall-clock minute (verified by replaying all 1440 minutes of a day through `cron.ParseStandard`). `TestRepoReconcile_ScheduleRequeuesWhenNotDue` got the same treatment at `n=12`, which also makes its `<= 6h` clamp assertion actually exercise the clamp instead of accidentally passing on a sub-6h fire. NOT changed: `mkRepo`'s default `"0 6 * * *"` - the same 06:00 CI window reddened exactly one test out of the whole package, so no other test is sensitive to the default, and churning ~20 files to chase a proven non-issue is not worth it.
- 2026-07-29 (admission-retry-amplification, issue #496) **A blocked QueuedEvent must not poll at all: the pool_full hold was O(queue depth) wasted reconciles per unit time, and it amplified exactly when the operator was most loaded.** Measured live: 877 `operator_admission_blocked_total` increments in 15 minutes across ~20 queued events (58.5/min), decomposing exactly as 40/min from the flat 30s `RequeueAfter` in `DispatcherReconciler.Reconcile` plus 20/min from the 60s dispatcher backstop re-enqueuing EVERY pending event. Both halves are pure duplication because **a reconcile of ANY QueuedEvent walks and admits the WHOLE project pool** - N blocked events produce N copies of one answer. Fixed by (a) `blockedPoll()` returning 0 when `BackstopEvents` is wired, so the hold schedules nothing, and (b) `pendingPoolHeads` making the backstop push ONE representative per pool per sweep instead of one per event (2/project/sweep, O(1)). Nothing about a blocked event is time-dependent: a freed slot already wakes admission through `Watches(&Task{}, mapTaskToQE)`, a new event through `For(&QueuedEvent{})`. **The wakeup cannot be lost** because the backstop sweep is LEVEL-triggered over authoritative cluster state and UNCONDITIONAL - deliberately NOT skipping saturated pools, which was the tempting variant of this fix and the one that turns a bounded one-sweep delay into an indefinite stall (`TestBackstop_DoesNotSkipASaturatedPool` exists solely to forbid it). The 30s poll is KEPT for `BackstopEvents == nil` (unit tests, any wiring without the leader-only sweep): with no safety net the poll IS the safety net. Also pre-seeded `operator_queue_depth`/`operator_queue_inflight` per (project,class) from the ProjectReconciler, because both were only ever written from the QueuedEvent reconcile - the `tatara/normal` series was ABSENT for 5.4h after a rollout rather than reading 0, and absence vs zero is indistinguishable on every dashboard and in any depth-based alert. NOT changed: `maxConcurrentAgents: 5` (lives in tatara-helmfile, a capacity/cost decision), and the per-event `heldOnUsage` requeue, which has the same O(depth) shape but a different unblock trigger (an unwatched fleet store refreshed on `UsagePollInterval`, which may be shorter than the backstop interval) - noted, not folded in.
- 2026-07-29 (sweep-adopt-pr, issue #477) **`agent.TaskBranch` is NOT unique per Task, and `taskForBranch` returning the first list-order match is what made the sweep retry a permanently-impossible PR adoption every 4h.** `TaskBranch` keys on `(branchKind, source.number, title slug)` and `branchKind` collapses review/brainstorm/clarify/refine into `chore`, so two Tasks minted for the SAME source issue share one head branch (live: `infrastructure-clarify-2026-07-26-t9n9n` and `...-07-27-gtwgp` both on `tatara/chore-26`, with `mr-helmfile-1358` controller-owned by the latter); an implement Task and its takeover collide the same way (both `feat`). The sweep picked the non-owning twin, `ClassifyPR` said `PRAdopt`, and `ownMergeRequest` refused ("already has controller owner X") - byte-identically, forever. Fixed by making the MR mirror's CURRENT controller owner the disambiguator: `sweepPRs` reads the CR BEFORE the branch lookup and passes `claimedBy` into `taskForBranch`, which returns the claimant when it shares the branch and otherwise picks deterministically (still-pushing > newest > name) instead of depending on list order. `ClassifyPR` clause 1b (`PRClaimed`) is the net for a claimant on a DIFFERENT branch: a skip counted on the new `operator_sweep_skipped_total`, never a sweep error. **The incident report's stated root cause was WRONG and was NOT implemented**: it blamed parked/`merging` Tasks for never releasing MR ownership and asked the reaper to release on park. `parked` is RE-ENTRANT (`parked -> approved|merging` on `ownership-lost`, `DrainStandDownMerge` re-drives it), so a parked Task holds its MR ON PURPOSE - releasing on park would break the takeover resume path. No quarantine/backoff state was added either (fix target 2): once the impossible adoption is never attempted, there is nothing to back off from.
