- 2026-07-12 (adversarial re-review of the Request C review-fix pass, 5 must-fix + 4 minor) A SECOND review found the F1/F4 fix incomplete and two more agent-reachable gaps. **M1 (writeback bypass):** `checkRemainingScopeHardFail` was called from `finishImplement` and from `doWriteBack`'s post-switch fallthrough, but `doWriteBack` dispatches `case "triageIssue": return r.writeBackIssue(...)` BEFORE that fallthrough, and `writeBackIssue`'s `out.Action == "implement"` branch calls `r.writeBackOpenChange` directly - a triageIssue-implement Task with declared RemainingScope sailed straight through. Fixed by hoisting the check ABOVE the kind switch in `doWriteBack` (it no-ops on kinds that never set RemainingScope). Also added a kind gate to the REST `POST /tasks/{t}/change-summary` handler (was the only outcome-writing endpoint with none) rejecting `IsProjectScopedKind` kinds. **M2 (close-directive regex):** `closeDirectiveRE` only matched `clos(e|es|ed)|fix(es|ed)?|resolv(e|es|ed)` immediately followed by `#N` - missed the `-ing` inflections, `Implements`, a colon, an `issue`/`issues` word, and every ref past the first in a comma list. Widened to cover the full GitHub/GitLab keyword set + inflections + optional colon/issue-word + comma/`and` ref lists, and `neutralizeUnapprovedCloses` now rewrites per-ref (approved refs byte-preserved when the whole directive is clean, unapproved refs stripped even inside a mixed list). **M3 (fail-open on List error):** both `unapprovedSystemicSiblings` and `withApprovedSystemicGroup` returned "nothing to worry about" (nil / task unchanged) on a client List error - now fail CLOSED (all siblings treated as unapproved / SystemicGroup emptied) rather than threading a new error return through call sites mid-writeback. **M4 (stale lock, no successor Task):** REVERTED - see the 2026-07-12 de-scope entry below; `ImplementationLocked`/`ImplementationLockedAt` and the whole approval fan-out feature this fixed a gap in were removed two entries later in this same de-scope pass, not shipped. **M5 (links sync self-defeats):** `syncSiblingLinks` swallowed every GetIssue/EditIssue error but the caller stamped `Status.LinksSyncedURLs` unconditionally - under SCM rate-limiting the exact retry the gate exists for never happened. `syncSiblingLinks` now returns a `clean bool` (a permanent 404/410 counts as done via the existing `isPermanentTargetGone`, a transient error does not) and the caller only stamps on a clean sweep. **m6/m7:** added a stable name tie-break to `newestTaskForSource` (metav1.Time is second-granularity) and made `issueHasRecordedApproval` newest-wins (was any-match, asymmetric with the lock's own resolution) - the auto-approve sentinel intentionally still counts, only the doc comment claiming "identity-verified HUMAN ... ONLY" was corrected to state the truth. (Kept post-de-scope: `newestTaskForSource` is still used by `issueHasRecordedApproval` and the clarify bundle for reasons independent of the fan-out feature this round was reviewing.) **m8:** `allIssueSiblingURLs`/`issueURLFromRepoURL` now take an optional repo-slug -> URL map so a self-hosted GitLab project's sibling links use its real host instead of the gitlab.com fallback. **m9:** `checkRemainingScopeHardFail` now posts the same kind of gated conversational comment the other terminal paths use (explains the declared remaining scope) and swaps the phase label to declined, instead of leaving an implementation-labelled issue with no PR and no explanation. **Left as-is (reported, not fixed):** the scope guard only fires when a Task actually reaches `doWriteBack`/`finishImplement`; a Task whose PR already opened (PrURL set, WritebackPending cleared) that later posts a NEW change_summary with RemainingScope during a review-fix turn has no reconcile path that re-evaluates it - doing this properly needs new terminal-state semantics for "PR already open but scope declared incomplete after the fact" (close the PR? revert auto-merge? who decides?), which is a design decision, not a mechanical fix, so it was not attempted here. Full `make generate manifests` (ImplementationLockedAt), `go test ./... -race -count=1`, `golangci-lint run` (0 issues), `pre-commit run --all-files` all green.

- 2026-07-12 - Request C: removed maybeOpenFollowupIssue entirely - a non-empty ChangeSummary.RemainingScope now hard-fails the Task (Phase=Failed, reason=IncompleteImplementation) instead of filing a follow-up issue. FollowupIssueURL/RemainingScope CRD fields kept (API compat) but RemainingScope is now vestigial-on-success (its presence is always a failure signal).

- 2026-07-11 (autonomy-dedup-crd-hygiene batch, Phases 2-4) **Incident dedup, item 4a auto-approve, sibling cross-linking.** (Phase 2) Incident dedup identity moved from labels (`tatara.dev/alert-group` Task label, `tatara/alert-group-<hash>` issue label) to `TaskSpec.DedupKey`: `matchIncidentByDedupKey` Lists incident Tasks and filters by `Spec.DedupKey` + a recorded `Status.DiscoveredIssues[0]` instead of a label-selector/issue-label match. The reaper now spares a terminal incident Task while its tracked issue is still open (`incidentTrackerIssueStillOpen`, reads only cached `Status.WorkItems` state - fails toward retaining on any uncertainty), since the Task itself is now the dedup anchor and premature GC would drop the lookup mid-incident. (Phase 3, SECURITY-SENSITIVE) `proposalKind()` derives the `tatara-proposed-by:<kind>` marker kind from `ProposedIssueSpec.Incident` only - refine never creates `ProposedIssue` Tasks today (confirmed via grep), so only brainstorm/incident are distinguished; extend `proposalKind` if refine gains a propose path later. Auto-approve (path c) is inserted inside the ALREADY-SHIPPED path (a)/(b) `if task.Status.ApprovedByMaintainer == ""` block in `lifecycle_triage.go`'s implement arm, attempted only after path (b)'s maintainer-participation scan found no approver - a live human approval always wins over the mechanical auto-approve when both are available in the same reconcile. Bot-authorship for path (c) reuses the existing `isBotAuthoredProposal(proj, task)` (reads `task.Spec.Source.AuthorLogin`, no extra SCM call) rather than a fresh live-issue-author read, since the Task already carries that value from ingestion. `triageReader` gained a memoized `getIssueContent` so `isTataraAuthored`/`tataraProposedByKind` share one `GetIssue` fetch per reconcile instead of two. (Phase 4) `syncSiblingLinks` maintains a `<!-- tatara-links:start/end -->` managed block idempotently via `SpliceLinksBlock`; `parseIssueURL` (new `internal/controller/issue_url.go`) is shared across the Phase-2 reaper spare check and the Phase-4 sibling linker to avoid a duplicate GitHub/GitLab issue-URL parser.

- 2026-07-11: A verified maintainer's thread comment again releases a parked `implement` triage outcome (operator scans thread via `approvingMaintainer`, records `ApprovedByMaintainer`, falls through to `onImplement`). Reverses the earlier "a comment alone no longer releases" gate; safe because the operator - not the agent - verifies the commenter is a `MaintainerLogins` member (closed by default; SCM read error and non-maintainer/empty-list all fail closed). Manual `tatara-approved` label path unchanged.
- 2026-07-11 (fix/reaper-phase-race-pod-blip) **Orphan reaper no longer deletes a live wrapper pod mid-continuation, and the poll+reap ticker now runs on the leader only.** Grafana triage of wrapper pod `tatara-tatara-gh-tatara-operator-issue-203` (6 distinct pod UIDs in 24h, `kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total==0` on every incarnation => delete/recreate churn, not a crash) traced the blip to `reaper.go` `orphanReason` reaping on `isTerminal(task.Status.Phase)` ALONE: `phase=Succeeded` is set on turn-batch drain (`task_terminate reason=NoPendingSubtasks`), but the task is frequently revived by `resetAgentRun` to continue in the SAME warm pod (front-half implement handoff `triageImplementAction` terminal=false; conversation reactivation). Observed for task `scan-qe-84k9j`: `phase=Succeeded` 12:03:57Z -> `reaped ... reason="task phase Succeeded"` 12:04:01Z -> `lifecycle_transition Triage->Conversation` 12:04:02Z (pod killed while lifecycle still live). FIX (1): gate the phase branch on `task.Status.DeployState == ""` - an empty DeployState marks a one-shot (non-conversational) task genuinely done (reap now, unchanged); a lifecycle task showing transient `phase=Succeeded` has a non-empty DeployState, so it is skipped by the phase branch and handled by the lifecycle-terminal branch (Done/Stopped/Parked) or the idle backstop (`IdlePodReapAfter`, default 30m, issue #237, `!taskHasInflightTurn` guarded). Every `resetAgentRun` continuation carries a non-empty DeployState (implement->`Implement`, reactivation->`Triage`), so `DeployState==""` cleanly separates "done one-shot" from "between-turns lifecycle task". Cost: an await-approval park (DeployState=Conversation, phase=Succeeded) now holds its warm pod up to IdlePodReapAfter instead of cold-respawning on quick reactivation - bounded, not a leak, strictly better than instant-reap. NON-OBVIOUS: did NOT change `isTerminal` (task_controller.go:89, ~10 callers) - only the reaper's use of it; issue-derived pod naming (`podNameSuffix` -> `issue-<N>`) left as-is (the `tatara.dev/task-uid` label already disambiguates incarnations, so the shared name is not the kill trigger). FIX (2): the poll+reap ticker lived in `CallbackServer.Start` under `callbackRunnable` (NeedLeaderElection=false, correct for the HTTP callback+push-metrics handler which must serve on every replica), so `PollOnce`+`ReapOrphans` ran in EVERY replica (confirmed: two replicas logged the same reap 0.5s apart). Split the ticker into `CallbackServer.RunMaintenance(ctx)` registered as a NEW leader-only runnable `maintenanceRunnable` (NeedLeaderElection=true) in wire.go; Start keeps only the HTTP server + bounded shutdown. Leader-election-disabled (single replica) still runs it. Gate green: make generate manifests (NO CRD drift - no API change), lint (0), test (full envtest controller+manager suites), build. Design/plan: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-11-reaper-phase-race-pod-blip-design.md, docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-reaper-phase-race-pod-blip.md.

- 2026-07-11 (feat/task-kind-redesign, cross-flow hardening, UNCOMMITTED) **Six cross-flow interaction fixes** (PROGRESS-hardening.md "Cross-flow interaction findings"). Load-bearing decisions: (1) The tier-revert model floor is SCOPED to the terminal `tatara-helmfile` target repo (`modelForKindOnRepo`), NOT a blunt always-on floor on the 5 reasoning kinds. An always-on floor was rejected because it contradicts the existing override-wins model tests AND finding #2 (which hardens tier-revert for kind=review, so component-repo review IS tierable down normally). Scoping to tatara-helmfile protects the entire self-heal chain (the revert MR's own review/implement live there) while leaving per-component tiering intact - the sanctioned "bypass for tier-revert-originated Tasks", identified structurally by target repo (no origin marker; any helmfile change deserves opus). (2) Systemic sibling approval is enforced AUTHORITATIVELY at implement-time (prompt filter + writeback close-strip), NOT at group construction: the group is stamped once at lead-task creation before ANY approval is recorded, so construction-time approval-filtering would empty every group; construction only excludes maintainer-DECLINED siblings (a permanent label-visible no). Residual: a sibling approved AFTER lead creation is not retroactively added to the ledger role:closes group (proceeds as its own implement) - safe (never a wrong close). (3) Cross-source proposal dedup now normalizes titles and applies to BOTH brainstorm and incident, superseding the prior deliberate "incident never title-dedups" behavior (that test was updated). Gate green: make generate manifests (no new API fields - reused DeployState/DeployedVersion/ApprovedByMaintainer, no CRD drift) lint test(-race) build. Single writer.Merge egress (mergePRSquash) untouched.

- 2026-07-11 (feat/task-kind-redesign, Phase 3+4) **clarify kind reconcile + webhook routing.** clarify decomposes the issueLifecycle FRONT-half (Triage+Conversation) into a discrete kind: `reconcileClarify` (clarify.go) reuses the shared queue-drain preamble (`drainLifecycleQueues`, extracted from reconcileLifecycle - byte-identical metric semantics, both call it), the shared front-half agent-run driver (`handleFrontHalf`, extracted from handleTriage), and `handleConversation`; it dispatches ONLY ""->Triage / Triage / Conversation / terminal, and ERRORS on any deploy-half state (clarify never enters Implement/MRCI/etc). The implement-outcome terminal action is parameterized: `finishFrontHalf(onImplement)` (extracted from finishTriage) runs the shared close/discuss/guard/default arms; issueLifecycle passes `triageImplementAction` (approved label + setLifecycleState Implement, terminal=false so the shared resetAgentRun tail runs, same Task continues), clarify passes `clarifyImplementAction` (handoff.go: setLifecycleLabel(implementation) removes brainstorming/adds implementation preserving the one-of-4 invariant + populates Status.Handover warm-resume artifact + setLifecycleState Done, terminal=true so finishFrontHalf SKIPS the resetAgentRun tail that would resurrect the terminated Task). DECISION: clarify keeps Source + RepositoryRef (single source repo, like issueLifecycle today) NOT an empty-ref project umbrella - the reused triage machinery (buildTriagePrompt/setLifecycleLabel/resolveTriageReader) is keyed on RepositoryRef; the empty-ref umbrella + umbrella prompt bundle is Phase 8's job. clarify reuses buildTriagePrompt (triage-flavored prompt) as-is; a clarify-specific prompt is a Phase 8 nicety. Webhook (server.go): new issue -> clarify (was issueLifecycle entry=Implement); issue comment on untracked -> `createClarifyTask` (was createLifecycleTaskAtTriage, issueLifecycle entry=Triage); ANY PR/MR (bot OR human) -> review (dropped the bot-PR->issueLifecycle special case AND the "Closes #N" linked-issue dedup folding - reviews are per-PR GenerateName, no issue-slot fold). clarify uses the deterministic `issueLifecycleTaskName` hash (kept the helper name) + no lifecycle-entry annotation (reconcileClarify always inits Triage). findLifecycleTask/findReactivatableTask now match `isFrontHalfKind` (clarify OR issueLifecycle bridge) and taskListOpts dropped its single-value LabelSourceKind selector (can't match both kinds) for in-Go filtering. Removed maybeEnqueueDocumentation push trigger entirely (documentation is a Phase-5 cron). The trigger-label-on-Conversation->Implement webhook jump is restricted to issueLifecycle bridge tasks only (clarify has no Implement state). DEFERRED to later chunks (flagged, not silently dropped): (a) NO producer yet creates an `implement` Task off the tatara-implementation label - clarify flips the label + terminates but the label->implement-Task-creation is a Phase-6/scan concern, so the end-to-end handoff dead-ends until that lands; (b) cross-Task warm-resume FORK (a new implement Task forking clarify's ConversationObjectKey) is Phase 8 - for now Handover is populated on the clarify Task's status as the durable artifact; (c) kindProfiles triageIssue/issueLifecycle NOT dropped (bridge still in-flight; drop with Phase-6 bridge retirement). The issueLifecycle bridge is fully intact and drain-only. Gate green: make generate manifests test lint build (all ./... tests ok, lint 0). 3 new clarify_test + routing_redesign push-no-task test; webhook tests updated to clarify/review + removed-feature tests deleted.

- 2026-07-11 (feat/task-kind-redesign, Phase 1-2) **7-kind redesign scoping + per-kind model tiers.** implement/review/clarify are UNCONSTRAINED (in neither `repoScopedKinds` nor `projectScopedKinds`; new `unconstrainedKinds` set + `IsKnownKind` helper for the queuedevent validator) rather than the plan's "move into projectScopedKinds + writeback allowlist" - so `ValidateTaskSpec` accepts empty OR repo ref for them (back-compat for stored/in-flight implement-with-ref tasks; new webhook umbrellas use empty ref) and `IsProjectScopedKind(implement)=false` so the writeback fence never blocks implement's PRs (no allowlist needed). documentation is the ONE repo-scoped agent kind. `kindDefaultModel` map (opus: brainstorm/incident/clarify/implement/review; sonnet: documentation/refine) is the modelForKind fallback and OVERRIDES project-wide Agent.Model (design A, uniform - documentation already did this; brainstorm now defaults opus regardless of Agent.Model); project ModelByKind override still wins. `writeBackOpenChange` now handles empty RepositoryRef: derives the ordered repo set from `TaskReposInScope` intersect project repos (else all), opens one PR per repo, per-repo `derivePRTitle(scope=repo.Name)`. DEVIATION: skipped plan Step-2.10 refine->tatara-refine-backlog required-skill (non-contract, breaks intentional refine->empty test expectations; refine's TATARA_SKILL_PROFILE already scopes its skills).

- 2026-07-08 (fix/bot-comment-turn-taking-gate) **Bot comment turn-taking gate: mechanical comment egress now routes through `comment_gate.go` so tatara stops re-commenting on its own issues (#243 alert re-fire x9, #112/#126 terminal x15) and MRs.** New `decideCommentGate`: rule 2 (bot-authored PR -> `gateBotMR`, zero comments - drive path parks via label+Status only) short-circuits, else rule 1 turn-taking (`botHasLastWordAmong`: bot silent if it has a comment and no reporter/approver `commentSilenceBreakers` = EffectiveReporter u EffectiveMaintainer logins, empty=>any-non-bot, replied at-or-after it). `TaskReconciler.gatedComment` wraps it (metric `SCMWrite(provider,"comment","suppressed_last_word"|"suppressed_bot_mr")`, fail-open on every read error). Gated sites: alert re-fire (writeback_proposal.go), terminal diag (task_controller.go postTerminalComment), park (lifecycle.go parkWithComment). **DELIBERATELY NOT gated (not tech-debt): (1) writeback.go warn/result/Done + lifecycle_implement.go outcomes - one-shot WritebackPending-idempotent completion posts, NOT spam; gating them self-suppressed the "Done - opened PR:<links>" notice behind the same-turn warn. (2) writeBackReview verdicts - respond to head-SHA not comments; the RoleReviewed+HeadSHA scan ledger already dedups same-head re-review; gating regressed approve-after-push (esp. GitLab MR notes). (3) drain-loop (lifecycle.go PendingComments) + triage discuss - gated UPSTREAM by the scan task-creation gate / triage silence gate.** Edge decisions: `resolveBotMR` trusts the `Source.AuthorLogin` hint ONLY for provider==github (GitLab AuthorLogin is the webhook actor, not the PR author) else reads GetPRState; ties favour posting (`tBreak.Before(tBot)`, second-granularity created_at); zero-CreatedAt comments skipped. Reviewed by 2 opus reviewers; both blocking findings (writeBackReview regression + same-turn suppression) fixed by clean reverts (those 3 files byte-identical to main).

- 2026-07-07 (fix/memory-ready-debounce-gate) **Memory gate debounced: task/lifecycle/ingest spawn is now gated on `memoryStablyReady` (Phase==Ready AND ReadySince>=3min) instead of bare Phase==Ready, preventing herd-release on return-to-healthy or leader rotation.** Root cause: bare Phase==Ready check at 3 gate sites (task_controller.go, lifecycle.go, repository_controller.go) released ALL gated work at once when memory first transitioned to Ready, and a new leader reported memory Ready for ~3min before retrieval was confirmed. FIX: (1) `MemoryStatus.ReadySince *metav1.Time` field - set on Provisioning->Ready edge in `reconcileMemory`, cleared on leaving Ready, persists through Status().Update so it survives leader rotation. (2) `memoryStablyReady(p, now)` returns true only when Phase==Ready AND ReadySince!=nil AND age>=3min (mirrors the existing memoryRetrievalUnhealthyThreshold window). (3) All 3 gate sites now use memoryStablyReady. (4) BOY-SCOUT: generic gate in task_controller.go is now SPAWN-ONLY (task.Status.Phase=="" && !taskHasInflightTurn) - an in-flight turn keeps being supervised during a blip. Tests: ReadySince edge-set/clear, memoryStablyReady table, spawn-only bypass for in-flight turn, fresh-task still gated when within window. All test setups updated via stableMemStatus() helper.

- 2026-07-06 (feat/brainstorm-depth1-clone, Task 15) brainstorm switched from full to depth-1 clone (reads current default-branch code only; incident/refine keep full clone for cross-branch forensics/backlog history); controlled by the `task.Spec.Kind != "brainstorm"` guard in the `TATARA_WORKSPACE_FULL_CLONE` block in agent/pod.go.

- 2026-07-06 (refactor/remove-selfimprove WS2) selfImprove removed from all live code paths (kindProfiles, writeback, turnloop, task_controller, metrics); CRD enum entries intentionally kept in api/v1alpha1/task_types.go:161,258 and project_types.go XValidation rules (modelByKind/effortByKind/spawnCeilingByKind) to avoid rejecting stored terminal selfImprove CRs - removing enum values is a breaking CEL change.

- 2026-07-06 (issue #253) **The tool-surface probe now debounces a failing backend (3 consecutive unhealthy cycles) before metering, so the operator's own rollout churn no longer reports a still-serving chat backend as `unreachable` and trips the `> 0 for 15m` tool-surface alert.** Root cause: `updateToolSurfaceProbe` (project_tool_surface_probe.go) dials `chat /readyz` and the operator-write surface in-process on the 60s gauge cadence with a 3s timeout, and `probeToolSurfaceRoute` classifies ANY `httpc.Do` transport error as `unreachable`. During an operator self-deploy (deploy_timeout/cascade rerolls produced 3 ReplicaSet waves over ~1h), pods that were themselves starting up or shutting down emitted transient `unreachable` for a chat that was provably healthy the whole time (0 restarts, `/readyz` served 200 to the kubelet throughout, no chat error logs). The probe folds into no Project condition and gates no agent dispatch, so the ONLY impact was false-warning noise via the metric the alert reads directly. FIX (chose candidate direction #2 from the issue over #1 "suppress during startup/shutdown" and #3 "make the alert tolerant"): mirror the existing `memoryUnhealthyCycles`/`memoryRetrievalUnhealthyThreshold` debounce - new `toolSurfaceUnhealthyCycles map[string]int` (keyed by backend, serialised reconcile path, no mutex) + `toolSurfaceUnhealthyThreshold = 3`. `probeToolSurface`: a healthy result (ok/present) deletes the backend's run and meters immediately; an unhealthy result increments the run, is ALWAYS logged (now with a `consecutive` field), but is metered only once the run reaches 3. NON-OBVIOUS: unlike the memory sibling (which always meters raw and debounces only a CONDITION fold), the tool-surface metric IS the alert signal, so the debounce gates METRIC EMISSION itself (below threshold the cycle meters nothing - no ok, no failure - which the issue's "before metering unreachable" wording sanctions). Direction #1 was rejected as imprecise (detecting the pod's own outbound-startup window is fiddly; controller-runtime only reconciles post-cache-sync) and the debounce subsumes the shutdown case (a single ctx-cancel cycle is below threshold); direction #3 was rejected as masking rather than fixing signal fidelity. The debounce is per-backend so it also covers the `operator` self-probe. Metric labels unchanged, so the obs pre-seed (operator_metrics.go ~520) is untouched. Tests: 2 new (DebouncesTransientUnreachable via a closed httptest server -> unreachable; HealthyCycleResetsDebounce) + existing 4 unaffected (healthy results still meter cycle-1). build/vet/gofmt/golangci-lint + controller(full envtest)+obs suites green; -race NOT run locally (sandbox has no gcc; CI runs it). Branch tatara/fix-253-tool-surface-probe-reports-chat-backend.

- 2026-07-06 (feat/documentation-agent, operator half) **New `documentation` Task kind wired end-to-end (CRD, webhook trigger, pod per-kind table, writeback, reaper GC); ships inert.** Repo-scoped to a project's DOCS repo (not the merged component repo) - the inversion is deliberate so `writeBackOpenChange` opens the MR in the docs repo unchanged; the triggering component repo + before/after SHA ride as NEW Task annotations (`AnnSourceRepo`/`AnnSourceBaseSHA`/`AnnSourceHeadSHA`) rather than `Task.Spec.Source` (which is issue/PR-shaped, not push-shaped). Landed on top of the just-merged Batch-3 per-kind table simplification (pod.go `kindProfiles`/`branchKind`/`resolveByKind`), so this is one table row + a few kind-keyed switches, not new plumbing. THREE independent CEL gates on the same enum string (ModelByKind, EffortByKind, SpawnCeilingByKind) all needed `documentation` added to both their key-rule AND message, plus MaxProperties 9->10 each, plus the kubebuilder Task.Kind Enum separately - miss any one and the CRD rejects what Go accepts (verified via 2 new envtest CEL-acceptance tests). Model is LOCKED to claude-sonnet-5 for this kind specifically (modelForKind special-cases the fallback, not the project default) since a doc task is genuinely a Sonnet-tier job regardless of the project's general Model; ModelByKind can still override it. Documentation Tasks carry no Source (no issue/PR number) so the generic `TaskBranch`/`podNameSuffix`/`ConversationKey` SHA-key-vs-Number-key branches needed a `documentation`-specific arm (short source-head-SHA suffix) or every doc Task's pod/branch name would collide/genericize to `task-<name>`. scm.WebhookEvent gained `BaseSHA`/`HeadSHA` for push events (previously push parsed neither before/after SHA in either GitHub or GitLab payload parsers) - needed for the dedup key and the annotations. Webhook gate: `handlePush` now takes `*Project` (was just `projectName` string) so `maybeEnqueueDocumentation` can read `proj.Spec.Documentation` without a second Get; self-trigger guard compares the DOCS repo URL against the pushed repo via `scm.SameRemote`, and the docs repo must be an enrolled `Repository` CR (bot push access) or the event is silently dropped. Dedup key `doc-<sourceRepoName>-<headSHA>` so redelivery no-ops but a distinct merge always spawns. `derivePRTitle` and `branchKind` are independently-maintained switches (landmine, both updated: `docs(...)` title, `tatara/docs-<sha>` branch). Tests: 2 new files (`pod_documentation_test.go`, `documentation_test.go` in webhook) + additions across task_types/project_tiermap_validation/pod_profile/pr_title_derive/turnloop_skills/conversation_gc/scm github+gitlab tests. Awaiting: tatara-cli tool profile, tatara-agent-skills skill (both separate repos, unblocked to merge independently), then a tatara-helmfile MR to enroll the docs repo + flip `documentation.enabled` for the tatara Project. Design: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-documentation-agent-design.md (parent tatara repo) - note its line numbers were stale (predated the Batch-3 file splits), symbols were re-located by grep, not by line number.

- 2026-07-05 (issue #252) **The per-Project memory PrometheusRule now carries two postgres-LAYER alerts so the DB failure modes that cause the "Memory HTTP 5xx error ratio high" page are caught one hop upstream, before they surface as API 5xx.** Recurrence of the [[#238]]/#240 signature: write-path 503s on `/memories:bulk` (reads healthy) driven by a degraded `mem-tatara-pg` CNPG cluster - this time a standby (pg-3) permanently wedged after its replication slot was invalidated (`wal_removed`, the intended effect of #240's `max_slot_wal_keep_size`) then stuck on a diverged future timeline that CNPG would not re-clone. Root observability gap: the only memory alerts were on the tatara-memory API layer (`http_requests_total`/`up`), so every DB degradation was invisible until it became API 5xx - purely reactive. FIX (KISS, one file): `memoryAlertRules()` (internal/memory/monitoring.go) now takes `(cluster, namespace)` and appends `MemoryPostgresVolumeFilling` (a cnpg PVC - PGDATA or the dedicated 2Gi WAL volume - under 15% free for 5m; catches #238 disk exhaustion before WAL writes stall) and `MemoryPostgresInstanceRestarting` (a cnpg `container="postgres"` restarting >2x in 15m; catches the primary crash/failover thrash of #238/#240). NON-OBVIOUS - metric choice: both key off `kubelet_volume_stats_{available,capacity}_bytes` (kubelet) and `kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total` (kube-state-metrics), which are cluster-standard and VERIFIED present in Prometheus during triage, NOT `cnpg_*`. Reason: despite #238's `PGPodMonitor`, `list_prometheus_metric_names cnpg_.*` returned EMPTY and `up{namespace=tatara}` showed only chat/memory/operator jobs (no postgres target) - the cnpg scrape is not landing metrics (most likely the running operator predates #238; PodMonitor code is correct and wired at project_memory.go:109, so not churned). Alerting on absent `cnpg_*` series would be silently inert (hard rule 4), so replication-slot/streaming-standby alerting (the exact wedged-standby signature of #252) is DEFERRED to a follow-up gated on confirming cnpg_* metrics flow. Scoping: rules are per-Project, and several Projects' cnpg clusters (mem-tatara-pg, mem-infrastructure-pg) share namespace `tatara`, so each rule scopes series by `namespace=<ns>, {persistentvolumeclaim|pod}=~"<cluster>-.*"` (cluster = `mem-<proj>-pg`, pods/PVCs are `<cluster>-<n>[-wal]`). NOT DONE (out of repo/too risky, flagged as triage lever areas): auto-recovering a wedged invalidated-slot standby CNPG won't re-clone; retuning `max_slot_wal_keep_size` (= half WAL volume, ~1GB - aggressive on the 2Gi default but changing it re-opens the #240 disk-full tradeoff); tatara-memory API pods shipping no logs to Loki (separate repo). Tests: monitoring_test.go asserts both alerts present+warning and cluster/namespace-scoped exprs. Branch tatara/fix-252-memory-5xx-alert-recurs-mem-tatara-pg-st.

- 2026-07-05 (issue #236) **Infra-incident Tasks are now EXEMPT from the `Memory.Phase == Ready` admission gate, breaking the self-heal deadlock where an incident investigating the down memory stack was gated on that same stack.** Incident (same outage as [[#238]]): `mem-tatara-pg` filled its CephFS volumes with WAL -> memory stack below quorum -> project `tatara` memory phase not-Ready -> EVERY Task hard-gated at `task_controller.go` `Memory.Phase == Ready`. The operator DID fire the alert and DID create incident Task `incident-qe-qjbxl`, but that incident sat in Planning ~4h gated on the same down memory, so the self-heal agent never ran / never opened an issue. Circular dependency: infra-outage self-heal needs the subsystem that is down. FIX (option (a) from the issue, KISS): NEW `InfraIncidentExempt(spec)` + `AlertTargetsCoreInfra(alertRule)` in `api/v1alpha1/task_types.go` - an incident-kind Task whose `spec.AlertRule` (the alertname label) case-insensitively contains any of `infraIncidentKeywords` {memory, lightrag, postgres, cnpg, neo4j, pvc, cephfs, quorum, wal} bypasses the gate. Wired into the generic-path gate in `task_controller.go` (incident is project-scoped, NOT issueLifecycle, so it hits that gate, not the lifecycle.go:613 one): when memory is not Ready AND `InfraIncidentExempt`, log `action=task_memory_gate_bypass` + `Metrics.MemoryGateBypass(project,kind)` and fall through to spawn; ELSE gate as before. NEW counter `operator_memory_gate_bypass_total{project,kind}` (obs/operator_metrics.go) - nonzero rate = memory degraded + self-heal running through the bypass, worth alerting on. NON-OBVIOUS: the BuildPod call site (`task_controller.go`) dereferenced `project.Status.Memory.Endpoint` unconditionally, which the gate previously guaranteed non-nil; on the bypass path Memory can be nil/degraded, so it now derives `memEndpoint` guarded (empty when Memory==nil - fine, incident agents investigate via Grafana MCP, not the memory graph, so an unreachable/empty memory endpoint is acceptable). Only incident kind qualifies; all normal work keeps the gate. OUT OF SCOPE (issue "Also", separate repos): memory-independent human-notify escalation when `operator_memory_stacks{phase!=Ready}` persists, and the PVC-near-full/stack-stuck-not-ready root-cause alerts (already added in tatara-observability). Tests: api InfraIncidentExempt table (infra/non-infra/case-insensitive/non-incident/empty), controller envtest (infra incident reaches pollRequeue spawn path + counter==1 with memory NOT Ready; non-infra incident stays memGateRequeue + counter==0), obs metric. build/vet/golangci-lint(0) + api+obs+controller(envtest) green. Branch tatara/fix-236-incident-self-heal-deadlocks-infra-incid.

- 2026-07-05 (issue #238) **The per-Project CNPG postgres cluster now provisions a dedicated WAL volume and a PodMonitor, closing the disk-exhaustion write-outage and the DB observability blind spot behind the "Memory HTTP 5xx error ratio high" alert.** Incident: `mem-tatara-pg` PVCs (10Gi, single shared volume) filled to 100% (pg-1 99.6%, pg-2 100%) between ~07:30-08:50Z; a full data/WAL volume stopped Postgres writing WAL -> primary crash/restart loop + failovers -> memory API returned 503 "upstream temporarily unavailable" on the WRITE path `/memories:bulk` (~94% of bulk writes) while reads on replicas kept working. Asymmetric per-instance growth (pg-1/pg-2 high, pg-3 low) pointed at WAL accumulation on the shared PGDATA volume. Root cause in THIS repo: `internal/memory/pg.go` `PGCluster()` set only `StorageConfiguration.Size` - no separate `walStorage`, and no `spec.monitoring`, so there were ZERO `cnpg_*` metrics in Prometheus (disk/replication saturation invisible until the API 5xx'd). FIX (3 parts): (1) NEW `spec.memory.pgWalStorage` CRD field (`+kubebuilder:default="2Gi"`, api/v1alpha1/project_types.go) + `pgWalStorage(p)` helper + `defaultPgWalStorage="2Gi"` const (internal/memory/memory.go), wired to `ClusterSpec.WalStorage` so WAL lives on its OWN PVC isolated from PGDATA and is resized independently. (2) NEW `PGPodMonitor` builder (internal/memory/monitoring.go) selecting cnpg's per-pod label `cnpg.io/cluster=<cluster>`, scraping the container port named `metrics` (9187) `/metrics`, gated behind `cfg.MonitorEnabled` alongside the existing MemoryServiceMonitor/PrometheusRule in project_memory.go applyMemoryStack. NON-OBVIOUS: cnpg's built-in `spec.monitoring.enablePodMonitor` is DEPRECATED in cnpg v1.29.1 ("create a PodMonitor manually"), so a native PodMonitor is built instead to avoid tech-debt (rule 4); cnpg pods serve /metrics on 9187 regardless of spec.monitoring, so a manual PodMonitor works standalone. jobLabel left default (no alert keys off the cnpg `job` label; the memory alerts query `http_requests_total`, not `cnpg_*`). (3) CRD crd-bases regenerated with controller-gen v0.18.0 (templates/crds.yaml globs crd-bases at template time, no separate edit; deepcopy unchanged - string field). VERIFIED SAFE on the EXISTING cluster: the cnpg v1.29.1 webhook `validateWalStorageChange` returns nil when `old.Spec.WalStorage==nil` - adding walStorage to a cluster that never had it is permitted; only disabling/shrinking it later is rejected. OUT OF SCOPE (flagged on the issue, separate repo tatara-helmfile not in workspace): (a) the live mem-tatara PVCs were manually expanded ~10Gi->20Gi out-of-band ~12:00Z; helmfile's project-tatara `pgStorage` still declares 10Gi and cnpg REJECTS size decreases, so that value must be reconciled to 20Gi to avoid a reconcile conflict, and `pgWalStorage` optionally set explicitly for mem-tatara. (b) Optional hardening: cap WAL retained for replication slots via `postgresql.parameters.max_slot_wal_keep_size` so a stuck standby cannot fill the WAL volume unbounded (deferred - changes replica-recovery semantics, would force a stalled standby to re-clone). GOTCHA: applying walStorage to the existing degraded cluster triggers cnpg to create the WAL PVC and roll the pg pods to move pg_wal onto it - a brief rolling operation when the operator picks up the change. build/vet/gofmt/golangci-lint(0) + memory+api+controller(full envtest) suites green; the ONLY full-suite failure is the pre-existing `internal/config` `TestLoad_S3DefaultsOff` which the agent container's own `S3_*` runtime env vars contaminate (passes with those cleared; untouched by this change). Branch tatara/fix-238-memory-http-5xx-alert-mem-tatara-postgre.

- 2026-07-05 (usage-gate activation) **The account-usage poller can now run on a self-refreshing interactive-login OAuth token, because the shared fleet `setup-token` lacks the `user:profile` scope `/api/oauth/usage` requires.** SPIKE (2026-07-05): `GET /api/oauth/usage` with the fleet `sk-ant-oat` setup-token returns `403 permission_error: OAuth token does not meet scope requirement user:profile`; an INTERACTIVE `claude login` token (scopes `user:file_upload,user:inference,user:mcp_servers,user:profile,user:sessions:claude_code`) returns `200` with the real window schema (five_hour/seven_day/seven_day_opus/seven_day_sonnet + extra_usage + limits[] + spend). `setup-token` scope is fixed (no flag adds `user:profile`), so the poller MUST use an interactive-login token - which expires ~hourly and rotates its refresh token. NEW (`internal/accountusage/oauthrefresh.go` + `refreshtoken.go`): `RefreshOAuth` (pure OAuth `refresh_token` grant) + `NewRefreshingTokenSource` (Load bundle -> return access while `now < ExpiresAt-Margin`, else refresh + persist rotated bundle; on refresh fail with still-valid access, fall back to it; only errors when access is hard-expired). wire.go: when `UsageSecretName` set, the poller's TokenSource is the refreshing source over that Secret (keys `oauth-access-token`/`oauth-refresh-token`/`oauth-expires-at` unix-secs), read via uncached API reader + written back via `mgr.GetClient().Update` (single leader polls; secrets RBAC already has update). Config (all env-overridable so a provider change is a helmfile flip, NOT a code change): `USAGE_SECRET_NAME`, `USAGE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` (default `9d1c250a-e61b-44d9-88ed-5944d1962f5e`), `USAGE_TOKEN_URL` (default `https://platform.claude.com/v1/oauth/token`), `USAGE_REFRESH_MARGIN` (5m). Also FIXED `normalizeUtil`: the spike confirms utilization is already 0..100 (2.0, 36.0), so the old `v<=1 -> v*100` coercion was a latent bug (a real 0.42% read as 42%); now passthrough clamped [0,100]. TECH-DEBT / FRAGILITY (rule 4, accepted): the refresh endpoint + client_id + the `/api/oauth/usage` contract are ALL undocumented/reverse-engineered and "may change without notice"; refresh tokens are single-use; the refresh call CANNOT be safely tested without burning a live token (rotation would break whoever holds the chain). MITIGATION: endpoint/client_id are config; the wrapper OTel reactive-429 floor + token-conservation maxCT/tier controls remain the real spend governor; poll-health gauge + `tatara_account_usage_poll_failures_total` make a broken refresh VISIBLE; separate `claude login` = independent refresh chain (confirmed) so a dedicated poller login never disturbs the human's Claude Code. ACTIVATION (still owed, needs the human): a dedicated `claude login` -> seed the usage Secret (sops) with the bundle -> tatara-helmfile MR (`USAGE_ENABLED=true` + `USAGE_SECRET_NAME` + per-Project `spawnCeilingByKind`), staged one-project-first. build/vet/golangci-lint(0) + accountusage(8 new tests)+config green; controller envtest not run locally. Branch feat/usage-poller-oauth-refresh. See [[claude-subscription-usage-gate]] context in parent tatara ROADMAP.

- 2026-07-04 (handoff-continuation, component 5) **The refine pre-scan barrier now fires on the brainstorm cron tick, unconditionally, replacing the ClosedLookbackDays>0 opt-in - refine and brainstorm are now ONE merged schedule, and refine no longer gates mrScan/issueScan/healthCheck.** Old runScans() ran a single barrier block BEFORE mrScan (opt-in via `cronSpec.Refine.ClosedLookbackDays>0`, computing the earliest due-base across mrScan/issueScan/brainstorm/healthCheck) that deferred ALL scans until a terminal refine Task existed. New: the barrier logic moved INTO the `cronSpec.Brainstorm.Enabled` block, keyed on `activityDue(proj,"brainstorm")`'s own base - it gates ONLY brainstorm (create refine Task if none in-flight, `consider(now.Add(requeueRefineBarrier))` to poll at 30s, skip running brainstorm this pass; on a terminal refine - Succeeded/Failed/Done/Stopped/Parked via existing `TaskTerminal` - stamp LastRefine and fall through to `r.brainstorm(...)`). mrScan/issueScan/healthCheck run on their own schedules unconditionally now, never waiting on refine. NON-OBVIOUS: `ClosedLookbackDays` stays as the refine lookback INPUT (default 30) but its role as the enable-GATE moved to brainstorm-enabled - a project with `Brainstorm.Enabled=true` and NO ClosedLookbackDays set still pays the refine barrier on every brainstorm cycle (this broke `TestBrainstormEnqueuesDespiteQueuedAutonomousCount`, fixed by stamping `LastRefine` in that fixture since it targets the budget gate, not the barrier). refine.GoalProject and brainstormGoalProject also gained handoff instructions (refine: list_handoffs+delete_handoff for stale/done; brainstorm: list_handoffs+get_handoff to propose continuing live ones BEFORE fresh ideas, under the same MaxOpenProposals cap) per docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-04-handoff-continuation-design.md Component 5. Tests: projectscan_refine_test.go rewritten around the brainstorm-tick trigger (due-tick creates refine+holds, terminal releases into brainstorm, Failed still releases, mrScan/issueScan unaffected, dedup, LastRefine-already-current skips straight to brainstorm); goal_test.go / projectscan_brainstorm_goal_test.go cover the handoff instructions. build/vet/gofmt + controller+refine+api suites green. Branch feat/handoff-refine-brainstorm.
- 2026-06-27 (#181) **Per-repo deterministic scan jitter spreads mrScan/issueScan fires across the cron interval, killing the synchronized top-of-hour fan-out that re-trips the "queue depth backlog" alert.** Root cause (live telemetry): all 5 infra repos x {issueScan,mrScan} (+brainstorm) share `0 * * * *`, so ~11 normal-class events land at `:00` on one FIFO pool (cap 5), depth jumps ~9->19 and stays >10 for ~30m, satisfying the rule's `for:30m`. A capacity bump (helmfile #72, 3->5) did NOT clear it - the lever is fan-out shape, owned by this repo. Fix (projectscan.go): `scanOffset(project,repo,activity,period)` = fnv32a hash mod period -> a stable per-(project,repo,activity) phase offset in [0,period); `cronPeriod` = interval between two consecutive cron fires (1h for hourly); `repoNextFire(sched,off,after)` = `sched.Next(after-off)+off` (phase-shifted next fire). New `reposDueForScan` returns the repos whose shifted fire is in the `(lastStamp, now]` window plus the soonest upcoming per-repo fire. NON-OBVIOUS / why this is stateless: it REUSES the existing project-level Last*Scan stamp - the stamp advances to `now` on every per-repo fire, so the (stamp, now] window slides and covers each repo's slot exactly once per period WITHOUT any new per-(repo) status field (the rejected alternative). runScans' mrScan/issueScan blocks now fire only `dueRepos` and requeue to the soonest slot. recoverOrphans + backstopSweep are gated behind `periodDue` (the OLD project-level activityDue boundary, computed pre-stamp) so per-repo jitter does NOT multiply the once-per-cycle recovery sweeps. brainstorm/healthCheck unchanged (per-project single events, not the herd). Empty-repo fallback: soonest = unshifted sched.Next(now) to avoid a 0-duration busy-loop requeue. activityDue refactored to share `activityScheduleAndLast`. Config stays a plain `0 * * * *`; offset is pure-hash so it is identical across operator restarts/pods (no rand, no wall clock). Options B (split review pool), C (per-project admitted metric label), D (burst-aware alert) from the issue are deliberately OUT of scope. Tests: projectscan_jitter_test.go (offset determinism+range+spread, cronPeriod hourly, repoNextFire shift, end-to-end simulation = each repo fires exactly once per steady-state hour AND >=2 distinct fire times, cross-run determinism, empty-repo requeue, disabled/bad-cron ok=false). go build + golangci-lint (0 issues) + full controller envtest suite green. Branch tatara/fix-181-operator-normal-pool-backlog-re-trips-qu.
- 2026-06-27 (#189) **Token-budget admission gate: pause proactive work at 50% / incidents at 80% of token usage within a reset window; off until enabled.** New `internal/budget` leaf package (pure decision engine, no k8s types, unit-tested in isolation) consumed by both the dispatcher (gate) and the turn-complete callback (accumulation). KEY MAPPING (non-obvious): the issue's two thresholds map onto the queue's EXISTING two pools, not a new taxonomy - the normal pool (QueueClassNormal: brainstorm/implement/review/issueLifecycle/...) is held at `ProactivePercent`, the alert pool (QueueClassAlert: kind=incident) only at the higher `EmergencyPercent`, evaluated independently so incidents keep flowing while proactive work is paused. `EmergencyBlocked` implies `ProactiveBlocked` because ResolvePercents orders emergency>=proactive. Two modes: customWindow (operator meters its own per-turn tokens - inputTotal incl. cache-read + output, the same delta behind operator_task_tokens_total - against an absolute TokenLimit) and claudeSubscription (gates on wrapper-reported Claude 5h/weekly percent). WINDOW SEMANTICS (non-obvious): the reset is driven PURELY by `resetSchedule` (a 5-field robfig cron, same parser as the scan crons) - `CurrentWindowStart` is the most-recent fire <= now; `windowDuration` does NOT drive the reset, it only BOUNDS the reset-boundary search to [now-2*duration, now] so a frequent cron isn't an unbounded scan (require it non-zero in customWindow). The accumulator (WindowStart+WindowTokens) lives on Project.Status.TokenBudget and `Roll` resets it to 0 when now crosses a new boundary, then adds. SUBSCRIPTION mode is INERT until the wrapper reports a snapshot whose reset is KNOWN and FUTURE-dated (`active = !reset.IsZero() && reset.After(now)`) - a zero/past reset is ignored so the gate can never get permanently stuck on a snapshot it can't expire (and the whole mode stays off until the cross-repo wrapper PR lands; the 5h/weekly limits are a Claude OAuth subscription feature, NOT the per-minute anthropic-ratelimit-* API headers). PER-TURN ACCUMULATION: recordUsage's signature changed to return (delta, recorded, err) so handleTurnComplete can call updateProjectBudget guarded by the SAME `recorded` flag as the per-Task accounting (a stale/duplicate callback never double-counts the window); one RetryOnConflict status write carries both the window roll and the subscription snapshot, behind a changed-guard (mirrors recordConversation). TRADEOFF ACCEPTED: ProjectReconciler has no GenerationChangedPredicate, so per-turn Project.Status writes DO trigger a Project reconcile - tolerated because turn-completes are low-frequency and the feature is opt-in; Project.Status is the home because the dispatcher already Gets the Project at admit time and reads the accumulator there. Config layers per-Project spec.tokenBudget over operator-wide TOKEN_BUDGET_* env defaults (a nil block inherits verbatim; a present block's `enabled` is authoritative); BudgetDefaults().Validate() fails Load fast on a bad enabled fleet default. Gauge operator_token_budget_used_ratio{scope=used|proactive|emergency} emitted ONLY when enabled (no series for disabled projects); counter operator_admission_blocked_total{class,reason="token_budget"}; budget-held pools requeue near the next cron boundary (budgetRequeueAfter: time-to-next-fire +1s, capped 5m; 60s fallback for no/bad schedule). Off until enabled (zero config disabled = admit path byte-for-byte unchanged, the [[#114]] backwards-compatible pattern). RBAC unchanged - projects/status update was already granted. Tests: budget unit (10), CRD accessor (5), config (5), obs metrics (3), turncallback envtest (window accumulate/roll/stale/snapshot/disabled), dispatcher envtest (direct-decision gate, blocked-emits-metric, real window-evaluation table, budgetRequeueAfter). Branch tatara/fix-189-tatara-operator-aware-of-token-budget; awaiting deploy + sister tatara-claude-code-wrapper PR for claudeSubscription.
- 2026-06-27 (#128) **The synthetic memory probe is now an AUTHENTICATED FUNCTIONAL check, not an unauthenticated route-presence ping - it closes the post-routing/auth blind spot that let MemoryReady stay green while every agent read 404'd.** Before, `probeMemoryRoute` sent no token and classified `404->absent`, transport-fail`->error`, and EVERY other status incl. `401` `->present`/healthy, so the whole failure class AFTER routing+auth (a memory-audience/OIDC misconfig that 401s every real token; a LightRAG/Neo4j backend that 5xxs real queries; a 200-with-broken-body after a bad re-ingest) was invisible - and this cycle `POST /queries`+`GET /code-graph/stats` DID 404 end-to-end for a research pod while the operator reported MemoryReady=true. Maintainer-approved set (issue decomposition) SP1=B/SP2=A/SP3=A/SP4=A. (SP2) New `MemoryToken func(ctx)(string,error)` on ProjectReconciler, wired in wire.go to an `auth.NewTokenSource` (client-credentials, the shared `OperatorOIDCClientID/Secret`, audience `tatara-memory`) mirroring the existing `wrapperTokens`; minted ONCE per 60s cycle (one audience for all projects; the source caches). (SP1) `probeMemoryRoute(ctx,httpc,method,url,body,token)` now sends `Authorization: Bearer` + (for POST) a JSON body and asserts HTTP 2xx + well-formed non-null JSON. The two routes carry a fixed canary: `POST /queries` body `{"mode":"naive","text":"tatara-operator synthetic memory probe","top_k":1}` and `GET /code-graph/stats?repo=__tatara_operator_probe__`. NON-OBVIOUS mode choice: `naive` (NOT hybrid/local/global) - tatara-memory's `Service.Query` sends `OnlyNeedContext:true` so `/queries` is retrieval-only (no response LLM), and naive further skips the keyword-extraction LLM the graph modes make, so MemoryReady asserts the exact minimal contract every agent retrieval needs (auth->handler->query-embedding->vector store->JSON) WITHOUT coupling the health gate to response-LLM provider uptime or a per-probe LLM cost; the embedding dependency it DOES keep is correct (no retrieval works without it). KNOWN GAP (deferred, like the issue's SP1-C canary-match): Neo4j graph-traversal liveness is NOT asserted (needs a graph mode); the code-graph/stats route covers the Postgres code-graph path instead. `__tatara_operator_probe__` works because tatara-memory answers an unknown repo with a zero-count 200 (`SELECT count(*) WHERE repo=$1`), so it exercises auth+handler+Postgres without needing real repo names; a DB-down stats maps to 5xx->degraded. (SP3) `operator_memory_retrieval_probe_total{route,result}` KEPT present/absent/error and ADDED `unauthorized` (401/403 = a valid token rejected -> auth/contract drift) and `degraded` (auth ok but 5xx OR malformed/empty/non-JSON body); only `present` (2xx+well-formed JSON) is healthy, all of absent/error/unauthorized/degraded count unhealthy. Pre-seed in `obs.NewOperatorMetrics` widened to the 5 results so the alert (#68) keying on this series lights up for the new classes for free. (SP4) UNCHANGED: 60s cadence + 3-consecutive-unhealthy (~3 min) MemoryReady fold at project_memory.go:250. Token-mint failure is metered `error` (unhealthy) and short-circuits the HTTP call (a token path the operator cannot use is, from here, indistinguishable from memory being unreachable; the 3-cycle fold rides out a transient mint blip). Nil `MemoryToken` (tests / unconfigured) sends no token -> memory 401 -> `unauthorized`/unhealthy by design (an operator that cannot authenticate to memory has no basis to report Ready - surface, do not hide). SCOPED OUT: #119 operator-write/chat breadth, any tatara-memory API change, eval/recall scoring (#41/#46), Alertmanager routing, SP1-C canary seeding. Tests: project_memory_probe_test rewritten - classifier table (2xx+JSON->present, empty/garbage/null->degraded, 401/403->unauthorized, 404->absent, 5xx/400->degraded), token+body attach, transport->error, unhealthy-increments/healthy-clears toggle, 401-for-everyone unhealthy, mint-failure unhealthy+server-not-hit, skip-non-Ready; fold test unchanged. go build/vet ./... + controller(envtest)+obs tests green. Branch tatara/fix-128-make-the-synthetic-memory-probe-an-authe. CI-FLAKE FIX (PR #196 `ci/test` job): the controller envtest suite intermittently panicked at boot with `timeout waiting for process etcd to start successfully (... stopped unexpectedly before becoming ready)` - the `waitDone` (process-exited) branch, NOT the `timedOut` branch, so a longer `ControlPlaneStartTimeout` would NOT help; etcd EXITS before ready on a busy ARC runner and controller-runtime's own 5 control-plane-start retries fire back-to-back with no pause, so a multi-second resource blip outlasts all 5 and fails the whole suite before any test runs (pure infra flake, unrelated to the probe diff - suite passes locally). FIX: `suite_test.go` TestMain now wraps `testEnv.Start()` in a bounded retry (`envtestStartAttempts=3` x `envtestStartBackoff=3s`, fresh `envtest.Environment` per attempt, nil-safe `Stop()` between tries) so a brief blip is ridden out; zero added latency on the happy path (Start succeeds first try).
- 2026-06-27 (#188) **Scan-time bot-last-word gate stops the constant agent loop on issues AND PRs/MRs: tatara no longer re-schedules when it posted the most recent comment and no human has replied.** Generalizes the [[#105]] human-activity gate, which only covered issue candidates WHEN a matching terminal/adoptable Task existed (`humanCommentAfter` in adoption + fresh-creation + isDeduped gates) - it never covered (a) the PR/MR scheduling path, where `mrScan`'s bot-PR branch gated only on `hasLiveLifecycleTaskForIssue` + `priorTerminalAttempts<maxRecoveryAttempts(3)` then `closeExhaustedPR`, so a bot PR that PARKED-with-comment (e.g. MRCI deadline) was re-created every cron cycle 3x then WRONGLY CLOSED; nor (b) the issue path when the prior Task was GC'd (no Task to gate -> fresh triage loop). NEW BACKSTOP: `botHadLastWord(ctx, reader, candidate, botLogin)` (projectscan.go) reads the candidate's conversation timeline and returns true when the newest comment (by CreatedAt, robust to SCM list order) is the bot's, via shared `botIsLastCommenter([]IssueComment, botLogin)` (lifecycle.go's `botHasLastWord` refactored onto it). Wired into `issueScan` (after adoption/fresh-creation gates, before `createScanTask`) and `mrScan`'s bot-PR branch (before `createScanTask`); new metric outcome `tatara_scan_items_total{outcome="skipped_bot_last_word"}` + INFO log. GITLAB MR FIX: `SCMReader.ListIssueComments` hits `/issues/:iid/notes`, WRONG for an MR (separate IID namespace) - added optional `scm.PRCommentLister{ListPRComments}` implemented by GitHub (delegates to ListIssueComments; a PR IS an issue) and GitLab (`/merge_requests/:iid/notes` via new shared `glListNotes(resource,...)` helper). NON-OBVIOUS - chose an OPTIONAL interface (type-assert + fall back to ListIssueComments, the GitHub-correct default) over adding to SCMReader, to avoid touching ~18 test fakes; production readers always implement it (compile-time `var _ PRCommentLister` asserts on both). Fail-open (return false = do NOT suppress) on read error / empty botLogin / nil reader / unsplittable repo, matching humanCommentAfter and preserving missed-webhook recovery. KEY SAFETY (why this can't stall autonomous merge): the MRCI->Merge->MainCI flow is a SINGLE LIVE Task polling every 30s (`RequeueAfter=pollRequeue`), guarded by `hasLiveLifecycleTaskForIssue`; the gate only blocks RE-CREATION of an already-TERMINAL Task, never an in-flight one. MRCI posts a comment only on park/terminal (not on pending/success), so suppressing re-creation of a parked-with-comment bot PR ALSO avoids the bogus closeExhaustedPR-after-3 - the parked Task stays and a HUMAN comment reactivates it via the webhook (findReactivatableTask). Left the `mrScan` REVIEW path (human PRs) untouched: it dedups on head-SHA so a new commit re-reviews and a bot-last-word gate there would wrongly suppress that. Tests: scm pr_comments_test (GitLab ListPRComments hits merge_requests endpoint + filters system notes + oldest-first, GitHub delegates to issues endpoint, PRCommentLister satisfied); controller scan_bot_last_word_test (botIsLastCommenter table; botHadLastWord PR->ListPRComments vs issue->ListIssueComments routing, no-PRCommentLister fallback, empty-bot/nil/unsplittable/read-error fail-open; issueScan + mrScan skip-on-bot-last-word -> 0 QE + metric, create-on-human-last -> 1 QE). go build/vet ./internal/... + golangci-lint ./internal/scm ./internal/controller (0 issues) + scm/controller(envtest) suites green. Branch tatara/fix-188-constant-issue-mr-agents.

- 2026-06-27 (#138) **TataraIngestJobFailing now alerts on a live current-state gauge with a `for:`, not the monotonic `operator_ingest_job_total` counter - it no longer mis-narrates a self-healed ingest burst for an hour.** The [[#135]] fix (mode={incremental|full} label + `increase(operator_ingest_job_total{result="failure",mode="full"}[1h])>0`) stopped *incremental* blips paging, but the alert was still recovery-BLIND: any full-ingest failure (or the incremental->full self-heal fallback) kept it firing for a full hour after ingest had already recovered, because `increase(counter[1h])` can't tell "currently broken" from "blipped and self-healed", and it had no `for:`/`repo` label. Maintainer (szymonrychu) approved the current-state-gauge direction. Fix (all operator-side): (1) new per-repo GaugeVec `operator_repository_ingest_failing{repo}` (obs/operator_metrics.go) = 1 when a Repository is currently failing, derived from status (Phase=="Failed" OR IngestFailureCount>0), plus `operator_repository_last_ingest_timestamp_seconds{repo}` from Status.LastIngestTime (staleness computed in PromQL as `time() - gauge`, the standard idiom - a snapshot timestamp doesn't go stale between sparse reconciles the way a baked age would). (2) `RepositoryReconciler.publishIngestHealth` (repository_controller.go) sets both every reconcile right after the Get, BEFORE the IngestEnabled guard, so a disabled repo reports failing=0 (never pages) and a recovered repo clears the instant handleFinishedJob resets Phase=Ingested/count=0 -> next reconcile sets gauge 0. (3) alert `TataraIngestJobFailing` re-expr'd to `operator_repository_ingest_failing > 0` `for: 15m` (15m comfortably clears the ~minutes-long incremental-retry+full-fallback self-heal window; consistent with TataraMemoryStackFailed's Phase=Failed gauge alert), annotation reworded to name `{{ $labels.repo }}` and stop asserting "cron not landing" on a recovered blip. (4) NEW companion alert `TataraIngestStale` = `(time() - operator_repository_last_ingest_timestamp_seconds) > {{ .Values.prometheusRule.ingestStaleAfterSeconds }}` `for: 30m` catches the silent "cron not landing, nothing actively failing" case the failing-gauge misses; threshold is a new chart value `prometheusRule.ingestStaleAfterSeconds` (default 93600s=26h, > the daily 0 6 * * * reingest cron; cluster-agnostic scalar per rule 6/14). The monotonic counter + its mode label STAY (still emitted; still feed the tatara-loop.json success-rate panel) - only the ALERT moved off it. BOY-SCOUT: the `repo` label resolves [[#135]]'s DEFERRED "name the stuck repo" item. CAVEAT (same as [[#135]]): the firing rule is the Grafana-managed mirror UID ffq4pikvg0q2ob; after this chart merges the Grafana copy must be re-synced from the PrometheusRule (infra/helmfile) for the new expr + the new TataraIngestStale rule to take effect. OUT OF SCOPE (maintainer-confirmed): root-causing the 3x transient incremental failures behind the original page. Tests: obs TestSetRepositoryIngestFailing (set/clear, no latch) + TestSetRepositoryLastIngestTimestamp + names-stable extended; controller TestPublishIngestHealth (Failed->1, mid-retry count>0->1, healthy->0+ts, recovery clears, disabled->0). go build + obs/controller(envtest) tests + golangci-lint (0 issues) + helm lint/template (both alerts render, $labels/$value escaping correct) green. Branch tatara/fix-138-tataraingestjobfailing-fires-for-an-hour.

- 2026-06-27 (#129) **Push-metrics prefix allowlist was silently dropping 100% of the wrapper's families and the ingester's non-`ingest_` families; widened to the REAL pushed prefixes + added a regression-guard alert + a WARN-once receiver log.** Root cause (builds on the configurable-allowlist work in [[#46]]): the chart default `pushMetricsAllowedPrefixes` was `"wrapper_,agent_,memory_,ingest_"` - written from ASSUMED names. The wrapper pods actually push `ccw_*` (+ one `tatara_wrapper_internal_issue_total`) and the repo ingester pushes `ingest_*` plus `analyzer_*/semantic_*/scip_*/llm_*`; the receiver filters per metric family on prefix (`parseAndStamp` -> `hasAllowedPrefix`, receiver.go) and drops any non-match into `operator_push_series_dropped_total{reason="reserved_name"}`. So every `ccw_*`/`tatara_wrapper_*` family (turn outcomes, cost/tokens, clone/commit/push/auth failures) and every `analyzer_*/semantic_*/scip_*/llm_*` family never reached Prometheus, and `memory_` matched NOTHING (the memory service is scraped, not pushed - it emits `lightrag_*/code_graph_*/tatara_memory_*`). Maintainer-approved fix set A/A/A (issue decomposition): (1) widened chart default to `"wrapper_,agent_,ccw_,tatara_wrapper_,ingest_,analyzer_,semantic_,scip_,llm_"` (dropped dead `memory_`; kept `wrapper_/agent_` as reserved-for-future) - rule-6 camelCase scalar -> `PUSH_METRICS_ALLOWED_PREFIXES` ConfigMap key -> `getCSVList`; AND realigned the in-code fallback `pushmetrics.DefaultAllowedPrefixes` (was `{wrapper_,agent_}`) to the SAME 9 so the env-unset path matches the shipped contract. (2) New PrometheusRule `TataraPushMetricsDropped` on `increase(operator_push_series_dropped_total{reason="reserved_name"}[15m]) > 0` (mirrors `TataraPushMetricsRejected`, which watches a DIFFERENT series `operator_push_receive_total{result="rejected"}` and did NOT cover this) so future prefix drift can't silently regress. (3) Cheap add: `parseAndStamp` now logs one WARN per affected push (`action="push_series_dropped"`, lists dropped family names + the active allowlist) via `slog` default (set in main) so an operator can fix the allowlist without reproducing the push. NON-OBVIOUS: chose to WIDEN the allowlist (option A) over RENAMING the wrapper/ingester metrics to allowed prefixes (option B) - rename is a breaking 2-repo change spanning dashboards/PromQL; widening is one cluster-agnostic scalar and unblocks all pushers immediately. The drop counter still feeds the alert as the runtime guard against the next new family. Tests: pushmetrics `TestDefaultPrefixesAdmitPushedFamilies` (each real pushed family passes under the default allowlist, not counted reserved), updated `TestDefaultPrefixesRejectMemory` comment (memory_ intentionally excluded). go build/vet ./... + golangci-lint ./internal/pushmetrics (0 issues) + helm lint/template (ConfigMap value + new alert render) green. Branch tatara/fix-129-push-metrics-prefix-allowlist-silently-d.

- 2026-06-27 (#168) **Wrapper HTTP 409 "session busy" on SubmitTurn is now transient backpressure, not a hard dispatch failure - stops the retry storm and the false TaskTurnSubmitFailureRatio alert.** Builds directly on [[#164]]'s `operator_turn_submit_total{kind,result,outcome}` split (result in ok|error|transient, granular outcome). #164 DELIBERATELY excluded 409 from the transient path on the assumption that "the wrapper flips Busy->Ready before firing its turn-complete callback, so a 409 on submit is unexpected"; production telemetry disproved it - ~94% of turn-submit failures were 409 (the operator's annCurrentTurn view races the wrapper's session release: callback-driven transition, recordTurn failing to persist annCurrentTurn, or an operator restart between SubmitTurn and recordTurn), so every 409 hit result="error" AND returned a hard `fmt.Errorf("submit ... turn")` -> controller-runtime backoff re-submitted -> tight 409 loop (e.g. one task 13x in 30s). Fix: new `agent.IsSessionBusy(err)` (session.go) matches `*HTTPError{Status:409}`; `handleTurnSubmitFailure` (task_controller.go) gains a 409 branch BEFORE handleTransientWrapper that requeues on `busyRequeue=15s` with NO reconcile error and counts result="transient" (outcome stays http_409). NON-OBVIOUS: 409 is NOT routed through handleTransientWrapper (the 503/425/UnreachableError path) on purpose - that path's annAgentUnreachableSince + agentBootDeadline(5m) termination is for a Ready-but-never-accepting-turns pod; a busy session is legitimately RUNNING a prior turn (could exceed 5m) and must not be killed for it. A session stuck busy forever is still bounded WITHOUT a new deadline: a turn-0 desync (annCurrentTurn=="") is failed by the existing planning-stall watchdog (PollOnce/expireStalledPlanning, planningStallDeadline=20m), an in-flight turn by the turn-timeout backstop. KNOWN LIMITATION (issue suggestion #3, NOT implemented - would need a new wrapper API to report its in-flight turn id, cross-repo): the turn-0 desync self-heals only by re-running turn-0 once the orphaned in-flight turn completes - that turn's callback hits resolveTaskByTurn's "unknown turn" 404 (turncallback.go:170, empty annCurrentTurn is skipped) and is dropped, losing its result; acceptable vs the storm, flagged for a follow-up that adopts the in-flight turn. New counter operator_agent_session_busy_requeue_total (mirrors operator_agent_boot_race_requeue_total; the requeue is reconcile-success, else invisible); turn_submit Help + the stale session.go 409 comment updated. Alert needs NO rule change - reclassifying 409 out of result="error" removes the false positive. Suggestion #4 (exclude UnreachableError boot-race from result="error") was ALREADY done by #164. Tests: agent TestIsSessionBusy (409/wrapped-409 true; 503/425/500/unreachable/nil/other false); controller TestTaskReconcile_WrapperHTTP409_RequeuesWithoutError (mirrors the 503 test: RequeueAfter==busyRequeue, no error, no turn recorded, busy-requeue counter==1, result=transient/outcome=http_409 >=1, result=error==0). go build ./... + golangci-lint (0 issues) + agent/obs/controller(envtest) tests green. Branch tatara/fix-168-operator-misclassifies-wrapper-409-sessi.

- 2026-06-27 (#161) **GitHub remove_label SCM-write failures are now observable AND the burst that drove the SCM-write-failure-ratio alert (0.69) self-heals via rate-limit-aware retry.** Two-part fix in internal/scm/github.go, both intentionally scoped to the scm package (controller untouched -> no envtest needed, see [[#154]]). (1) OBSERVABILITY: GitHub.RemoveLabel's non-404 error branch now emits `slog.WarnContext("github: remove label failed", provider, issue_ref, label, status=ErrorStatus(err), err)`. This failure is best-effort sibling cleanup that setLifecycleLabel (controller/labels.go) tolerates, so before this its ONLY trace was the operator_scm_writes_total{result="error"} increment - finding #6 of the issue: ~58 errors, ZERO queryable log lines. KEY DESIGN (mirrors [[#149]]): logged at WARN via slog (the scm package uses slog directly, which HAS a WARN level, unlike controller logr-over-slog which only has Info/Error) - NOT at ERROR, so it stays out of the `level="ERROR"` log-burst alert while being visible to the on-call `level=~"ERROR|WARN"` query. No new metric: status is ALREADY machine-readable via operator_scm_request_errors_by_status_total{provider,verb,status} (added in [[#163]]/c76a19c), and adding an http_status label to operator_scm_writes_total would change the write-failure-ratio alert math. (2) RESILIENCE: ghDo (the shared GitHub write path, so add_label + remove_label + create_issue etc. all benefit) now retries rate-limit rejections - 429, or 403 carrying Retry-After / X-RateLimit-Remaining:0 / a "rate limit" body - honoring Retry-After secs, else X-RateLimit-Reset epoch, else exp backoff 1s<<attempt + jitter (math/rand/v2 rand.N, de-correlates a concurrent burst). Bounded: ghMaxRetries=3, and a wait > ghMaxBackoff(16s) FAILS FAST (return error -> controller-runtime requeue) rather than blocking a worker goroutine. SAFETY: only rate-limit responses are retried because GitHub rejects them UNPROCESSED (side-effect-free re-send even for non-idempotent POSTs); 5xx is deliberately NOT retried (may have applied server-side -> could duplicate create_issue). Plain 403/401/404/422 carry no rate-limit signal so they still surface immediately (existing TestGitHubRemoveLabelRealErrorSurfaces with body "nope" unchanged). ghRetrySleep is a package var stubbed in tests for instant runs. DIAGNOSIS NOTE: the burst cause (secondary rate limit vs other) was UNCONFIRMED from telemetry (the whole point of the observability gap); the now-present status metric + WARN log will confirm it on recurrence. Did NOT add proactive global write-spacing/throttle (recommendation #3) - unconfirmed cause + KISS; reactive backoff is GitHub's documented mitigation and self-heals the burst. Tests: internal/scm/github_ratelimit_test.go (retry-then-succeed on Retry-After 403, 429 exhaust+surface, plain-403 no-retry, 5xx no-retry, long-Retry-After fail-fast, RemoveLabel end-to-end retry, RemoveLabel WARN-on-non-404 + silent-on-404). go build/vet ./... + golangci-lint ./internal/scm (0 issues) + scm/obs tests green. Branch tatara/fix-161-github-remove-label-scm-writes-fail-in-a.

- 2026-06-27 (#149) **Reaper conversation-GC no longer amplifies a store-wide object-store outage into an ERROR-log burst; it short-circuits the pass on a connection-level failure.** Root cause (see [[#114]] conv-gc): `gcConversations` (reaper.go) probes every key of every fully-closed batch with `ConvStore.Exists` (HeadObject) and, on ANY error, did `l.Error + Metrics.ConversationGC("error") + continue` - so a TCP dial-refused / retry-quota-exhausted RGW blip became one ERROR per backlog key (dozens) every reaper cycle and tripped the Grafana `>20 ERROR/5m` log-burst alert even though the operator was healthy and the dependency was the thing that failed (alert fired 2026-06-25, 57 ERRORs/5m, 100% `connection refused`). Fix: new pure classifier `objstore.IsUnavailable(err)` (internal/objstore/unavailable.go) detects store-wide/connection-level failures - `ratelimit.QuotaExceededError` (wrapped by the SDK's "failed to get rate limit token, %w"), `*net.DNSError`, dial errnos ECONNREFUSED/ECONNRESET/EHOSTUNREACH/ENETUNREACH via `errors.Is`, and any `net.Error` with `Timeout()` - while genuine per-object errors (NotFound/AccessDenied/single 5xx) return false so they still surface individually. gcConversations now wraps the batch loop with a `batchLoop:` label; on an `IsUnavailable` error from Exists OR Delete it calls `skipUnreachable(err)` (one log line + `Metrics.ConversationGC("unavailable")`) and `break batchLoop` - so one outage yields ONE line per pass, not dozens, and the next cycle retries once the store recovers. NON-OBVIOUS DECISION: the issue asked for a "single WARN", but controller code logs via logr-over-slog (`log.FromContext`, bridged in cmd/manager/logr.go) which has NO WARN level (logr only emits Info[+V] or Error; the package imports no slog - confirmed), so the skip is logged at INFO via `l.Info` (non-ERROR is the load-bearing property - it keeps the line out of the `level="ERROR"` burst query); the machine-readable signal for a dedicated/quieter alert is the NEW `operator_conversation_gc_total{result="unavailable"}` label (additive, separate from "error"; metric doc comment updated). No backoff added (KISS - one INFO/pass is cheap and self-heals; a persistently-down store just re-skips next cycle). Tests: objstore TestIsUnavailable (refused/reset/unreachable/quota/dns/timeout -> true; NotFound/AccessDenied/generic -> false) + controller TestGCConversations_SkipsPassWhenStoreUnreachable (unreachableConvGC returns ECONNREFUSED; asserts existsCalls==1 short-circuit, zero deletes, result="unavailable"==1, result="error"==0 via a registry-gather helper). go build/vet, golangci-lint (0 issues), objstore+controller(envtest)+obs tests green. Branch tatara/fix-149-reaper-conversation-gc-amplifies-a-trans.

- 2026-06-25 (#154) **GitHub.RemoveLabel now swallows a 404 "Label does not exist" so benign best-effort sibling-label removals stop counting as SCM write errors - fixes the false-positive TataraSCMWriteFailureRatioHigh alert from [[#133]].** setLifecycleLabel (controller/labels.go) enforces "exactly one of 6 managed phase labels" by removing the other 5 best-effort; its doc comment already promises RemoveLabel "tolerates the label being absent", but GitHub.RemoveLabel (scm/github.go) issued DELETE .../labels/{name} and surfaced GitHub's `404 {"message":"Label does not exist"}` as a hard error. recordSCM(provider,"remove_label",err) then logged result="error" for every absent sibling (~5/reconcile), driving operator_scm_writes_total{verb="remove_label",result="error"} to dominate the ratio (~0.82) while every functionally meaningful write (add_label/comment/create_issue/approve/close_issue) succeeded - the alert was firing on healthy state AND blind to a real outage. Fix (the robust option #1 of three the issue listed): RemoveLabel now `errors.As(err,&he) && he.Status==http.StatusNotFound -> return nil`, mirroring GitLab.RemoveLabel's idempotent `PUT ...?remove_labels=` (which never 404s) and the existing 404-tolerant patterns in gitlab.go:537/547. Real failures (401/403/5xx/network) still return and still count. SCOPED OUT (#3 in the issue, the controller `known`-guard reliability fix so known-absent siblings aren't blindly DELETEd each reconcile): the load-bearing fix is #1 (the closed-issue / list-error fallback path relies on it regardless), and a behavioral change to the reconcile path could NOT be verified in the pod (controller envtest needs /usr/local/kubebuilder/bin/etcd which is absent; `make test` provisions it via setup-envtest in CI). Tests (scm/github_writeback_test.go): RemoveLabel happy-path DELETE, absent-label 404 -> nil (with tatara/approved URL-escape assertion), and 401/403/500 still surface as *HTTPError. go test ./internal/scm + vet + golangci-lint green; controller package compiles (TestMain envtest panic is the missing-etcd harness limit, not a regression).

- 2026-06-24 (#135) **operator_ingest_job_total gained a `mode={incremental|full}` label so the TataraIngestJobFailing alert stops paging on the benign incremental->full self-heal.** A repo whose incremental (`--since`) ingest fails is retried up to incrementalFallbackThreshold=3 (each a BackoffLimit=0 Job that increments `result="failure"`) then falls back to a full ingest that succeeds; the old `{result}`-only counter could not tell that self-heal from a stuck repo, so a single fallback burst tripped the `increase(...{result="failure"}[1h])>0` warning. Fix: BuildJob stamps Job label `tatara.dev/ingest-mode` (ingest.LabelIngestMode; incremental when since!="", else full) on both Job + Pod template; handleFinishedJob reads it back and passes mode to IngestJobResult(result, mode) on BOTH success and failure paths; empty/absent label (a Job in-flight across the rollout) defaults to `full` = fail-loud (page rather than silently drop an unclassifiable failure). Alert expr re-targeted to `increase(operator_ingest_job_total{result="failure", mode="full"}[1h]) > 0` - a failed *full* ingest (first/scheduled full, or the self-heal fallback) is the genuine "graph going stale" signal; transient incremental failures no longer page. Zero-init loop now seeds result x mode (4 series). DEFERRED (not in scope, noted for maintainer): a bounded `repo` label so the alert names the stuck repo (held to keep zero-init clean; Loki runbook already surfaces it), and preserving the last failed ingest Job past its 600s TTL for forensics. CAVEAT: the firing alert is the Grafana-managed mirror (UID ffq4pikvg0q2ob); after this chart merges the Grafana copy must be re-synced from the PrometheusRule (infra/helmfile) for the new expr to take effect. Dashboard success-rate panel left as-is (its unfiltered sum() aggregates correctly over the new label). Tests: job_test ingest-mode label (incremental/full, Job+Pod), operator_metrics_test mode-attributed counts; go test obs/ingest/controller (envtest) + golangci-lint green.

- 2026-06-24 (fix/agent-self-report-platform-problems) **Agents must call report_internal_issue when blocked by platform/tooling failures, NOT file help-issues.** incident/goal.go previously told agents to still file an issue when grafana MCP was unreachable (root cause of #133 wrong-route). Fixed: platformProblemGuidance const appended at four seams (planTurnText, lifecyclePhaseGuidance, brainstormGoalProject, healthCheckGoalProject) + local duplicate in incident/goal.go (avoids import cycle). Deployed via helmfile after tatara-helmfile MR (BOTH chart + image.tag bump required). Part 2 (Grafana Loki alert rule repair, terraform infra) is a separate follow-up.

- 2026-06-24 (#133) **grafana-mcp 401: root cause = image 0.11.4 predates GRAFANA_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN_FILE support (landed v0.16.0); fix = helmfile pin 0.11.4->0.17.0 + operator ValidateImage() guard.** ValidateImage rejects parseable semver < v0.16.0 at reconcile time (slog ERROR, fail-open on latest/digest); wired in reconcileGrafanaMCP before SSA-apply. Token, wiring, and file-mount are all correct; no secret change needed. helmfile #61 image bump ships the fix.
