Now pinned by `TestReconcile_CapsSuppressionDoesNotCoverTheTurnBudget` (kind=review, committed Review outcome at stageEnteredAt, live Ready pod so no-outcome cannot fire, turns >= max -> must still fail turn-budget-exhausted). (3) `handoffCondition`'s fail-closed on nil `StageEnteredAt` was unpinned - inverting it to fail OPEN kept the suite green; no test built a committed-outcome Task with no stage stamp. Now pinned by `TestHandoffCondition_FailsClosedWithNoStageStamp`. **TRAP - the re-claim's `LastTransitionTime` refresh rests on an accident:** the orphan re-claim refreshes the lease clock ONLY because `setCondition` is a WHOLE-STRUCT overwrite. `meta.SetStatusCondition` would NOT refresh it (Status goes True->True, and it only re-stamps on a Status CHANGE), minting a lease born already expired - the next retry 1s later reads it as orphaned, re-claims, and duplicates every side effect, **reachable on a single-version single-pod cluster with no race at all**. The existing "past the TTL" test asserted only 200 + stage + `Reason=="Clarify"`, all of which `commit()` overwrites, so the claim's own timestamp went unobserved. Pinned by `TestOutcome_ReclaimOfAnOrphanedStubRefreshesTheLeaseClock`, which parks a handler inside `CreateIssue` (the existing `blockingCreateIssueForge`) to read the condition in the window between re-claim and commit; `setCondition` now carries a DO-NOT-tidy-into-`meta.SetStatusCondition` warning. Also pinned `release()` on the unknown-kind `default:` arm (reachable: `status.agentKind` is an unvalidated plain string and the gate only checks `env.Kind == task.Status.AgentKind`, so a bogus agentKind sails through on a matching bogus kind). Deleted three VACUOUS tests that asserted constants against themselves (`TestOutcomeClaimTTLAndHandoffDeadline`, `TestOutcomeReasonFor`'s `"" -> OutcomeReasonClaimed` entry, `TestHandoffStalledIsInTheF5ClosedSet`'s literal restatement); KEPT `TestOutcomeHandlerBudgetIsUnderTheClaimTTL`, which pins a real invariant. **Rationale inconsistency resolved:** this branch deleted `mrScan` on the grounds that pruning makes removal safe, while `healthCheck` was kept "inert for stored-CR back-compat" - one had to be wrong. The mrScan rationale is the correct one (`TestProjectCR_StaleMRScanBlockIsPrunedNotRejected` proves pruning drops a stale block silently), so healthCheck's back-compat claim is false; it survives only as scope. Comments corrected at all four sites, deletion queued in ROADMAP.md. `deploy-samples`' stale `mrScan` blocks removed; `issueScan.maxPerCycle` (not a `CronActivity` field at all) is PRE-EXISTING rot, left alone and noted in ROADMAP.md.

2026-07-18 (fix-stream H, the `operator_unpark_declined_total` burst, #368) 29,756 declines over ~4h, peak 3.14/s, wildly disproportionate to the handful of active tasks. Root cause was NOT one stuck task: `project.Status.Memory.Phase` was pinned at `Provisioning` for the whole window, which forces `reconcileMemory` to return `RequeueAfter=10s` EVERY pass (`project_memory.go:480-483`) - and that floor drives `Reconcile()`'s ENTIRE `ctrl.Result`, not just the memory subsystem. Loki shows Project reconciling even faster than that 10s timer within some windows (three `reconciled project` lines in 0.6s) - filed separately as #367 (suspect: `applyMemoryStack` re-patching `.Owns()`-watched objects every Provisioning pass, re-triggering through the watch on top of the timer; not fixed here, deliberately out of scope). `driveUnparks` had NO pacing of its own: it re-swept the FULL parked-Task backlog (58 system-wide at burst time, most days old - "in-flight" active-task counts are a different, much smaller number) on every single pass, whatever cadence that happened to be, and `kind="rule"` (normal steady-state non-eligible re-entry, per the metric's own doc comment) accounted for ~100% of the volume. **Confirmed NOT a gap: `operator_unpark_declined_total` already carries both `stageReason` and `kind` labels** (added #352/3d35a17, predates this investigation) - the original hypothesis that the metric lacked a reason label was wrong; dropped from scope once verified live against Prometheus. Fix: `driveUnparksPaced` (`unpark.go`) floors `driveUnparks` to `UnparkDriveInterval` (30s constant, `defaultUnparkDriveInterval`), **keyed per-project via `lastDriveUnparks map[string]time.Time`** - deliberately NOT a single scalar clock like `lastGaugeRecompute`, because gauge recompute really is cluster-wide by design (see its own doc comment) while `driveUnparks` is per-Project; a scalar here would have made two live Projects throttle each other. The residual wait folds into `soonestRequeue` so a genuinely-eligible F.6 re-entry is never starved past the floor once nothing else forces frequent reconciles. `driveCommentUnpark` (webhook comment-driven F.6 promptness path) is untouched - different call site, no pacing needed there by design.

2026-07-17 (issue #324, the residue after #347's mint fix - `refining -> refining`) The #347 mint fix closed the CACHED `status.stage == ""` race one edge too early: `reconcileTriaging` fires on a CACHED `status.stage == triaging`, computes `next` from `triageTarget(spec.kind)` (deterministic on Spec, so every reconcile of a stale-cached Task recomputes the SAME target), and calls `r.enter(next)`. `objbudget.FitTask`'s in-write `Get` re-reads through the SAME cached `r.Client` but later in the SAME reconcile - `mintIssueCRs`'s own I/O is enough wall-clock time for the informer to have caught up on our OWN prior reconcile's `triaging -> refining` write - so it sees the Task already at `refining` and refuses `refining -> refining`. Audited every `r.enter` caller for the same shape (cached-derived target, no live check): `reconcileClocks` (both the handoff-stalled Parked exit and the budget-elapsed exit) and `reconcileCaps` (turn/pod-recreation/no-outcome exits) share it identically - each does its own I/O (`ownedMergeRequests`, `podGone`) before `enter`, and each recomputes its edge from a `task` struct that is never re-Get'd, so a stale cached copy yields the SAME edge.To on repeat, genuinely reachable the same way, just rarer than triaging (which fires on every single new Task). Fixed ONCE, generally: new `refreshTaskFromAPI` (task_controller.go, APIReader live Get, nil-safe for unit tests) called in `reconcileStage` right after the terminal check and before `reconcileClocks`/`reconcileCaps`/the stage switch, so every one of those branches is covered by a single choke point instead of three bespoke ones. Rejected AGAIN (twice now): a self-edge in the `Transitions` table - `stage.Enter` re-stamps `StageEnteredAt` and zeroes `PodStartedAt`/`StageWorkStartedAt`/`PodRecreations` even when from==to, so a self-edge would silently reset a running Task's clocks and crash budget. TDD caught its own mint precedent's known failure mode: the new `TestReconcileStage_TriagingIsIdempotentAgainstAStaleCache` was written to production's real shape (ONE client backing both `Client`/`APIReader` holding the LIVE `refining` Task, a SEPARATE stale in-memory `triaging` struct as the `task` arg) FIRST, proven to fail with the exact production error string when the guard is stubbed out, then proven to pass once restored.

2026-07-18 (issue #355, memory-provisioning stream D - bounded Degraded timeout + rollout convergence + pre-SubmitTurn gate) Live incident: `mem-tatara-lightrag` sat Running-but-never-Ready ~7h+, `status.memory.phase` stuck `Provisioning` indefinitely (no bound existed at all - `memoryPhase` only ever returns "Ready"/"Provisioning"), old+new API pods coexisted (no rollout-convergence check in `memoryStackHealth` - `AvailableReplicas` alone can't distinguish a converged rollout from a stale-generation pod still Available), LightRAG call error ratio hit 72-84%. Consistent with issue #327 (closed, different root cause - node-isolation SPOF, no anti-affinity; tracked separately as #365, out of this stream's scope). Three fixes, all operator-repo-only: **(a) bounded timeout -> Degraded.** New `Project.Status.Memory.ProvisioningSince` (mirrors `ReadySince`'s edge-set/clear shape but in the opposite direction: set on any Ready/Failed/""->Provisioning edge, PRESERVED across a Provisioning<->Degraded episode so the clock does not reset every 10s poll, cleared on reaching Ready). New chart-configurable `memoryProvisioningTimeoutMinutes` (default 45m, `MEMORY_PROVISIONING_TIMEOUT_MINUTES` env, same clamp-floor/zero-disables shape as `idlePodReapMinutes`/`IDLE_POD_REAP_MINUTES`). `reconcileMemory` flips phase to `"Degraded"` (NOT `"Failed"` - Failed stays reserved for hard apply/password errors) once `now - ProvisioningSince >= timeout`, with `MemoryReady=False/reason=ProvisioningTimeout`. New `TataraMemoryStackDegraded` alert (charts/tatara-operator/templates/prometheusrule.yaml, sibling of the existing `TataraMemoryStackFailed`, `for: 5m`) - deliberately NOT in internal/memory/monitoring.go's per-project rule, because `operator_memory_stacks` is the OPERATOR's own cluster-wide gauge (scraped via the operator's chart ServiceMonitor), not a per-project tatara-memory-scraped series; a per-project PrometheusRule would emit one identical duplicate alert per project. **(b) rollout convergence.** `deploymentRolloutConverged` (project_memory.go) gates `lightragAvail`/`memoryAvail` on `ObservedGeneration==Generation && UpdatedReplicas==Replicas==AvailableReplicas`, not `AvailableReplicas` alone - closes the exact old+new-pod-coexisting gap the incident showed. **(c) pre-SubmitTurn memory gate.** The existing admission gate (task_controller.go, `task.Status.PodStartedAt == nil && !memoryStablyReady`) only fires ONCE per pod-lifecycle, before ensureStagePod; it does NOT re-check between a pod becoming Ready (PodWatchReconciler stamps `PodStartedAt`/arms CLOCK 3) and the actual `SubmitTurn` call in `reconcilePodStage`, a gap spanning scheduling+image-pull+the G.10 handshake during which memory can flip unhealthy. New re-check immediately before `SubmitTurn` (same `memoryStablyReady`/`InfraIncidentExempt` semantics, `memGateRequeue` cadence) closes it for every turn0 path uniformly (first pod, `respawnLostPod`, `ttlStop` rotation - all funnel through the one `SubmitTurn` call site). Held-turn surfacing mirrors the existing WS3-I5 `enqueueDeployTimeoutComment` pattern exactly: ONE operator comment per owned open issue on first hold, deduped via new `Issue.Status.LastMemoryGateCommentAt` (DISTINCT marker from `LastDeployTimeoutCommentAt`/`LastRefireCommentAt`, same one-shot shape). New `operator_memory_gate_hold_total{project}` counter (sibling of the existing `operator_memory_gate_bypass_total`). **TRAP found while fixing:** `tsProject` (task_stage_test.go, ~40 callers) deliberately carries `Phase=Ready` with NO `ReadySince`, so `memoryStablyReady` reads false and the OLD admission gate accidentally protects every `PodStartedAt==nil` fixture from ever reaching `ensureStagePod`'s `ValidatePodSecretRefs` (none of these fixtures wire `AnthropicSecretName`). Backdating `ReadySince` in `tsProject` itself (the first attempt) broke `TestSteadyStateQueuedTaskDoesNotTerminate`/`TestReconcilerAppliesTheThreeClocks` by removing that incidental protection. Fixed with a SEPARATE `tsStablyReadyProject` opt-in used only by the two tests that actually need `SubmitTurn` to fire. Also: `deploymentRolloutConverged`'s stricter gate broke `fakeStackUnhealthy` (memory_debounce_test.go's `TestReadySince_TransitionEdges`) because it zeroed `Replicas` but left a stale `UpdatedReplicas=1` from a prior `fakeStackHealthy` call - envtest's k8s 1.33 API server validates `status.updatedReplicas <= status.replicas` on Deployment status subresource writes; fixed by also zeroing `UpdatedReplicas` in the test helper. Anti-affinity/topologySpreadConstraints (issue #327's other recommendation) tracked separately as #365, filed but not implemented here.

2026-07-17 (issue #324, fix pass on the above) The guard ADOPTS the live object (`*task = live`) instead of bailing, and the four decisions behind that are the ones worth keeping. (1) **ADOPTION, not a requeue.** The first cut paid for a live GET, DISCARDED it, and returned `RequeueAfter: 1s` so the NEXT reconcile could redo the work from a cache it merely hoped had caught up. Adoption is strictly better: the live object is FRESHER than the cache the requeue was waiting for, so there is nothing left to wait for. It also removes a real LIVELOCK - a wedged watch or a dropped event drifts EVERY reconcile, and the bail turned that into a 1s requeue reporting `ReconcileResult("Task","success")` forever with zero progress and nothing firing (the default 10h `SyncPeriod` is nowhere near close enough to rescue it). What makes adoption safe is that nothing downstream needs the cached object: `EnterStage`/`FitTask` re-Get and re-mutate under `RetryOnConflict` anyway. **`StageTerminal` is re-checked on the FRESH object** - a Task that went terminal between the cached and live reads must take the reaper's early return, not fall through into clocks/caps. `mintedAlready` still BAILS and that asymmetry is deliberate: there is no live object to adopt AS at the mint, the whole question there is whether our own Create edge landed. NotFound is deliberately NOT a bail either (proceed; the reaper owns it, or the enter surfaces a real NotFound). (2) **COST, accepted knowingly:** one live GET - an etcd QUORUM read via `mgr.GetAPIReader()` - on EVERY non-terminal reconcile. `delivered`/`approved`/`merging`/`deploying` pay it while entering nothing from this reconciler; `delivered` alone requeues at 30s for 48h, ~5760 quorum reads per Task. Narrowing the guard to just the entering stages was CONSIDERED AND REJECTED for now: a single choke point that is obviously right beats three call-site checks that drift apart, and the whole reason this branch exists is that the last narrow fix (#347, mint-only) left a residue one edge later. Only ever narrow it with the pod-stage test below in place. (3) **The window is NARROWED, not CLOSED** - the pre-fix comment claiming a live read "closes the window completely" was FALSE generalized and is corrected. It is true only for the MINT, whose sole writer is this reconciler. `status.stage` has other writers, none serialised against this one: the `/outcome` REST handler (runs on ALL 3 replicas, not just the leader), `queue_controller`'s `admitTicket` (a DIFFERENT controller - `MaxConcurrentReconciles: 1` is PER-CONTROLLER, so it runs concurrently), and `StageDriver`. A write from any of them between the live GET and `objbudget`'s in-write Get reopens it; that residue is what transition.go's post-write `IllegalStageTransition` emit catches. (4) **New `operator_stage_drift_total{stage}`** (obs/stage_metrics.go, labelled by the CACHED/stale stage, next to `IllegalStageTransitionTotal` as its early-warning sibling) plus an INFO log with `cached_stage`/`live_stage`: adoption is self-healing, and self-healing silently is how a wedged watch stays invisible. A trickle is normal informer lag; a sustained rate on one stage is a wedge. **THE TEST THAT MATTERS is the POD-STAGE one**, and the widening was entirely unpinned without it: both triaging tests use a Task with no `StageEnteredAt`, so `ArmedClock` returns `ClockNone` (stage.go:572) and `BudgetExit` returns nothing for a podless stage - between them they execute ZERO lines of clocks/caps edge derivation, and anyone narrowing the guard back into `reconcileTriaging` keeps the suite green while clocks/caps drift reopens, surfacing only as `{from="failed",to="failed"}` in prod. `TestReconcileStage_PodStageCapsAreIdempotentAgainstAStaleCache` (implementing, all three stamps set, `PodRecreations` over budget, no Pod so `podGone`, live copy already `failed`) is mutation-proven: stubbing the guard fails it with `illegal stage transition failed -> failed`.

2026-07-18 (fix/mr-mirror-selfheal, issues #380/#381) **Review Task permanently orphaned from its MergeRequest mirror when a mint is interrupted.** Live: review Task for PR #373 finished but submit_outcome 400'd `this task owns no open MR` on every retry; scm_read(kind=mr) listed #361-378 but not #373/374/376. The MR CR EXISTED but as an unbound STUB - spec present, status carried only `headSHA` (a webhook stampMRHead from a later push) + a misleading MirrorSynced condition (hourly cadence thread-sync), NO state/body/headBranch/author, NO controller owner; the Task carried no mrRefs. Root cause: `MintReviewTask` (intake.go) mints in three non-atomic etcd steps - createTaskRaceSafe (Task create), bindMRToTask (SyncMergeRequest full-populate + ownMergeRequest), stampMintStatus (mrRefs). A restart between step 1 and completing step 2 (the v1.8.1->v1.8.2 rollout this afternoon) left the Task created but the MR a bare stub. Every later attempt (hourly sweep PRReview path AND webhook re-mint) hit `createTaskRaceSafe` returning created=false and returned early WITHOUT re-binding; the MergeRequestReconciler cadence only re-syncs the comment thread (syncMergeRequestThread), never re-runs full SyncMergeRequest, so the stub's state/ownership were never repaired - permanent. The 400's proximate cause is `ownedMRs` (handlers_v2.go) filtering by controller-owner==task.Name (empty -> no-open-mr); scm_read hiding #373 is handlers_v2.go filtering `state != open` (stub state empty). Fix: made the created=false backstop SELF-HEALING - createTaskRaceSafe now returns the live twin, and MintReviewTask/MintIssueTask run `repairMRBinding`/`repairIssueBinding` against it when the artifact is an unbound stub (or absent). Repair NEVER steals: a CR already controller-owned (by the twin - idempotent - or, defensively, anyone) is left untouched, so it is safe on every hourly pass and race-safe (ownMergeRequest's RetryOnConflict + never-steal is the real arbiter; the Get is a cheap skip). ClassifyPR already routes an UNOWNED stub to PRReview, so live stubs #373/374/376 self-repair within one sweep cycle (<=1h) after deploy, no manual mutation. **Adjacent, filed NOT fixed (#381):** `report_internal_issue` is a stdout-only no-op (turncallback.go re-logs and returns 204, no CR) - agents believe they file platform reports that reach no durable sink; the #373 review agent's tool_error report left no trace but its own handoff Task notes. semver: patch (bugfix, no API/CRD change).
2026-07-18 (fix/metric-wiring-audit, issue #370: post-redesign dead-metric audit) Live Prometheus (Grafana MCP, 7-day history across pod generations) proved several I1/K.1 metrics were NOT dead-code residue despite reading zero in the raw 48h evidence that opened this stream - they were correctly wired and had fired in PRIOR pod generations (`operator_review_outcome_total`, `operator_review_post_total`, `operator_open_proposals`); the flat-zero window was pod-restart-recency + genuinely low event frequency, not a bug. Do not "fix" a live-zero metric without checking longer history first. Real findings: (1) **`operator_task_parked_total` undercounted, root cause found:** `internal/restapi/outcome.go`'s own `stage.Enter(...StageParked...)` sites (awaiting-human, identity-unverified, ...) bypass `transition.go`'s `EnterStage` - the only other caller of `TaskParked()` - the same choke-point gap D1 already closed for `operator_task_terminal_total` via `commit()`'s `TaskTerminalEntry` call, never extended to `TaskParked`. Fixed by adding the identical `if to == StageParked && from != ""` check to `commit()`. (2) **`operator_sweep_errors_total` and `operator_stage_drift_total`** were CounterVecs with zero pre-warm (no `seedLabels`, unlike every other closed-set CounterVec in this codebase) - a healthy zero-error steady state produced literally no series, not a zero baseline. Pre-seeded both. (3) **`tatara_scan_items_total`/`tatara_scan_tasks_created_total`/`tatara_scan_duration_seconds` pruned** (dead-per-redesign, tatara-observability already treats them as such) - but the operator's OWN chart (`templates/prometheusrule.yaml`'s `TataraLoopStalled` deadman + `dashboards/tatara-loop.json`) still queried two of them; pruning them without fixing the chart would have made `TataraLoopStalled`'s PromQL `and` against a nonexistent metric evaluate to an empty vector FOREVER (the alert would never fire again, silently). Repointed the alert onto its own already-documented primary term (`operator_tasks_minted_per_sweep_count`) and the dashboard panel onto `operator_tasks_minted_per_sweep_sum`/`operator_sweep_mint_cap_hit_total`; added `obs.SweepLastSuccessTimestamp{activity=brainstorm|documentation|issueScan}` (new label values on the EXISTING sweep heartbeat gauge, set from `stampScan` on success) as the successor signal for a stalled scan cron, but deliberately did NOT fold it into `TataraLoopStalled`'s expression - opt-in-disabled activities (brainstorm/documentation) would need `absent_over_time`-aware gating to avoid false-firing on a project that never enabled them, which is real alerting design, not a mechanical repoint; a dedicated per-activity alert is a ROADMAP follow-up. `Makefile`'s `chart-lint` metric-presence allowlist updated to match (had the OLD dead names hardcoded, which would have masked exactly this class of break). (4) **Real unreported hygiene bug found while auditing item 6 (stage-age gauge staleness):** the stage/stage-age gauges self-heal fine (Reset+recompute every 60s, verified sound, not a bug) - but `DeleteTaskSeries` (`operator_task_tokens_total`/`operator_task_turns_total` per-issue counters) had ZERO production callers, only a unit test. Wired into `deleteReapedTask` (reaper.go); switched its matching from exact `DeleteLabelValues(...,model,type)` to `DeletePartialMatch(project,repo,kind,issue)` so a Task whose `Status.ResolvedModel` changed mid-life (respawn/stage change) gets ALL its model-labelled series cleared, not just the final model's. (5) `operator_orphan_adopted_total` confirmed a legitimate zero (both webhook- and sweep-driven mint counts were 0 in the same 7-day window - the backlog is fully covered by webhook-primary reactivity, not silently dropped). Companion tatara-observability PR (allowlist entries for `operator_review_post_total`/`operator_stage_drift_total`) tracked separately per stream E's concurrent allowlist rewrite there.

- 2026-07-19: issue #381 bug A - mrOpen hardcoded head="task/"+task.Name
  (never existed on the forge, every MR open 502d "scm write failed" with
  the real 422 masked). Fixed to agent.TaskBranch(task), the same function
  that seeds the pod's TASK_BRANCH env; ourMR/AdoptPR/taskForBranch migrated
  in lockstep, TaskBranchPrefix ("task/") deleted - it had zero remaining
  callers. mrOpen's error path now passes a forge 4xx through with the real
  status + trimmed body instead of a blanket 502. Review follow-up: accepted
  taskForBranch's O(project-tasks)-per-PR cost as-is - it is bounded by the
  Task-list field index, not an unindexed scan - revisit only if a project's
  live task count grows large enough to make that cost visible.
- 2026-07-19: issue #381 bug B part 2 - a Source-bearing Task that ends up
  owning zero MergeRequest/Issue CRs (an interrupted two-phase mint write,
  live proof mt-r-tatara-cli-87) now gets a 5-minute-grace backstop that
  parks it under the EXISTING stage.ReasonAwaitingHuman reason rather than
  a new reason - the reason set is CEL-validated and load-bearing (see the
  2026-07-13 entry). No new CRD field for the notification's rate limit:
  the Task this backstop targets owns no Issue/MR CR to stamp a cooldown on
  by construction, so the stage-transition guard itself (parked->parked is
  not a legal F.3 edge) is the rate limit instead. Comment delivery reuses
  Task.Spec.Source.IssueRef + a new parsePRURL/parseSourceURL pair
  (parseIssueURL alone only recognizes issue URLs, not PR/MR ones) rather
  than resolving a Repository CR - review-kind Tasks carry neither
  Spec.RepositoryRef nor Spec.Source.URL, only Spec.Source.IssueRef (a PR
  web URL despite the field name). Review follow-up (BLOCKER): the backstop
  now gates its park on `stage.Legal(task.Status.Stage, StageParked)` before
  calling `r.enter` - triaging and delivered have no legal ->parked edge in
  the F.3 table and fall through untouched (triaging's own triage-stalled
  clock, and delivered's reaper age-out, own those cases instead), closing
  the illegal-transition infinite-requeue crash-loop the un-gated version hit.
- 2026-07-19 (#398): `incidentDedupKey` dropped its CommonLabels hashing
  entirely - key is now sha256(project + rule name) only, no denylist. Root
  cause: Grafana's CommonLabels is the intersection of whatever instances
  co-fire in one evaluation, so the member set (and therefore the label
  intersection) churns run to run even though the rule itself hasn't
  changed; the #320/#328 "stable labels" denylist still let that churn
  change the key and bypass the open tracker, re-spawning full
  investigations 4x ~35min apart on one rule. Trade-off accepted: same rule
  + different co-firing workload now collapses onto ONE tracker (a coarser
  key than #320/#328's), so workload distinction is no longer in the dedup
  key - it now rests on the refire comment (`labelSummary`, full
  CommonLabels, replaces the old volatile-stripped `stableLabelSummary`),
  the escalation valve (refire threshold 10 / stale age 48h re-admits a
  fresh investigation), and cross-rule group linking (`incidentGroupKey`,
  untouched, still project+correlation-labels). Removed as dead:
  `defaultVolatileDenylist`/`denylistSet` (grafana.go),
  `Server.dedupDenylist` (server.go), `Config.IncidentDedupVolatileLabels` +
  `INCIDENT_DEDUP_VOLATILE_LABELS` (config.go + webhook.Config +
  cmd/manager/wire.go wiring). `correlationSet`/`incidentGroupKey` (the
  separate GROUP key) are untouched.
- 2026-07-19 (#386): `obs.SweepLastSuccessTimestamp` is process-local and
  resets to unset on every pod redeploy, while `Status.LastIssueScan` /
  `LastBrainstorm` / `LastDocumentation` are etcd-backed and survive it. The
  issueScan cron (`0 */4 * * *`) is slower than the push-CD redeploy cadence,
  so a fresh pod almost never re-stamps before the next replacement and
  `TataraLoopStalled`'s NoData-is-failure alert false-positived. Fix:
  `runScans` (projectscan.go) now rehydrates the issueScan/sweep/brainstorm/
  documentation gauges from the persisted `Status.Last*` stamps at the top of
  every reconcile, before any due-check - success-only stamping (`stampScan`)
  was a false liveness proxy across redeploys, it advances the gauge between
  rehydrates but was never the only way it gets set. A never-scanned project
  (nil stamp) is correctly left unset - that is true NoData, not a bug.
- 2026-07-19: `internal/controller`'s `suite_test.go` shares ONE envtest
  namespace/etcd across every test in the package with no per-test cleanup -
  a package test that lists-all (like the WP9 backstop's `EnqueuePending`,
  unscoped by design across every Project) must tolerate suite-wide
  pollution from every earlier test that left state behind, not just its own
  fixtures. `TestDispatcherEnqueuePending_NoWatchTrigger` hung deterministically
  (not a rare race - reproduced twice in a row) on a buffer-4 test channel
  once accumulated Queued-state QueuedEvents from ~20 earlier tests exceeded
  it and the blocking send never unblocked (`ctx=context.Background()`,
  never cancelled). Fixed by sizing the test channel to match production's
  real 256 (`wire.go:426`) and reading back by searching the drained batch
  for the expected event instead of assuming it is first in List order.
- 2026-07-19 (#392): `docBatchingConfigured` (docbatch.go) hand-duplicates the
  `MintDocBatch` gate predicate in `projectscan.go` (`cronSpec.Documentation.
  Schedule != "" && doc != nil && doc.Enabled && doc.Repo != ""`) - it exists
  so `needsDocumenting`'s exemption (d) can tell "documentation configured but
  never mints a batch" apart from "will eventually mint and cover this Task",
  since a project with `documentation.enabled` but no cron schedule
  structurally never fires MintDocBatch and would otherwise pin every
  delivered Task on it in the reaper's doc_reference GC gate forever. The two
  predicates are NOT unified behind one shared function (would require
  threading projectscan.go's cron-tick context into docbatch.go's reaper-gate
  context, a bigger refactor than this fix's scope) - keep them in sync by
  hand on any `DocumentationSpec`/`ScmCron` schema change.
- 2026-07-19 (feat/mr-ownership) MergeRequest gains status.ownership (tatara|external) + lastBotHeadSHA. Merge gate moved from Kind=="review" to per-MR ownership=="tatara". ReconcileOwnership (one function, two drivers: leader MergeRequestReconciler on the webhook-stamped HeadSHA bump + cron sweep) backfills by author and flips tatara->external on unattributable head drift; external->tatara is ONLY the gated /scm/mr-takeover endpoint (comment must exist in mirror, author IsTrustedAuthor && !IsBot && != botLogin - agent judgment never flips state, PR-Agent lesson). Chose a NEW `takeover` Task kind (not reuse clarify): needed for a collision-free deterministic name (issues and MRs share a number space on GitHub but NOT GitLab) and for a clean kind=takeover observability label; ripple is bounded (enum + unconstrainedKinds + branchKind + Create->approved edge) because the running agent profile is stage-derived (implement), not Spec.Kind. Takeover Task mints into `approved` (admission-gated; the maintainer's gated comment IS the approval), pushes to the MR head branch via the new AnnTakeoverHeadBranch (TASK_BRANCH override, NOT the read-only review CHECKOUT_BRANCH). Announcements posted by an idempotent DrainOwnershipAnnouncement keyed on a <!-- tatara-ownership --> marker, decoupled from the flip write (forge-outage resilient, review-post pattern). lastBotHeadSHA recorded LIVE at implement-outcome accept (never agent-reported) and stamped fast by a verified bot-push webhook to close the push-before-outcome race. General comment->task invariant: every human comment yields a Task update or creation - the issue arm already minted via the webhook funnel + driveCommentUnpark, so added the MR arm EnsureTaskForMRComment, killing the F5-1 orphan-MR-comment drop (server.go:741-745); minting is a race-safe create (allowed in the webhook goroutine, same as issue/MR-opened), the sweep is the convergence backstop, and takeover is now a CONSUMER of this one pipeline (comment -> ensure review task -> agent judges -> mr_takeover_request -> flip + mint/unpark takeover task), not a bespoke path. semver:minor.
- 2026-07-19 (OP9) The takeover flip (external->tatara) is 3 non-atomic client.Update calls (ownership flag, owner-ref move, lastBotHeadSHA stamp) rather than one transaction - accepted per the platform's convergent-model contract, not a gap: a crash mid-sequence self-heals on the next sweep pass instead of needing a rollback path.
- 2026-07-20 (OP9 whole-branch review, 2 fixes) (1) Takeover authz was gated on IsTrustedAuthor, which also accepts a listed REPORTER - too weak: takeover grants the bot push+merge agency over a human's own MR, a PRIVILEGE grant, not intake trust. Swapped to IsMaintainer (api/v1alpha1/logins.go, closed-by-default, bot structurally excluded); reporters are now intentionally insufficient to take over an MR, only listed maintainers can. (2) The initial /scm/mr-takeover implementation set Ownership/OwnershipReason/OwnershipChangedAt on flip but NOT LastBotHeadSHA, leaving it at its prior (often empty, for a never-bot-pushed Renovate PR) value - ReconcileOwnership's drift check (ownership==tatara && liveHead != LastBotHeadSHA) then fired on the very next reconcile and flipped the just-taken-over MR straight back to external, parking the takeover Task before its agent ever ran. Fixed by stamping LastBotHeadSHA to the current head in the same FitMergeRequest closure as the ownership flag. Neither gap was caught by the endpoint's own unit tests (comment-mirror-only harness, no ReconcileOwnership call) - added a crossing test (TestMRTakeover_SurvivesNextReconcileOwnership) that runs the real endpoint then the real ReconcileOwnership to close that seam.
- 2026-07-19 (OP5) Closed F.5's reason-set coupling: adding `ReasonOwnershipLost` to F.3's transition table alone is not enough - a new park reason must also join the F.5 `Reasons` slice in `internal/stage/stage.go` or `stage.Enter` rejects it at runtime (the same class of doc/code drift Task 22 found already). Any future park reason follows this same two-place-edit pattern.
- 2026-07-20 (#33 review-task external-terminal MR) A kind=review Task in reviewing whose owned PR a human merges/closes had no legal terminal: submit_outcome 400'd "no open MR", the claim released, the idle pod was reaped and respawned, re-reviewing the terminal PR until the 4h budget parked it. Fix: two reviewing edges (delivered/mr-merged-externally when all owned MRs merged; rejected/mr-closed-externally when all terminal with >=1 closed), a shared terminalMREdge (internal/controller/reviewpost.go) driving an UNCONDITIONAL finalize in reconcileClocks (fires even with no handoffCondition - the #33 shape) + a pre-dispatch guard in ensureStagePod, Owns(&MergeRequest{}) for prompt requeue, and a 2xx no-op (not 400) at both submit_outcome review rejection sites. REMINDER: a new stage Reason is a TWO-PLACE edit - the const AND the Reasons F.5 slice in internal/stage/stage.go, or stage.Enter rejects it at runtime. LegalFor gates reviewing->delivered ONLY, never reviewing->rejected (that pair is shared with the issue-closed stop, which enters with mrs==nil); the mr-closed terminal predicate lives in terminalMREdge, its sole caller. MR-state helpers (MRTerminal/AllMRsTerminal/AllMRsMerged) are exported because controller.terminalMREdge consumes them cross-package.
- 2026-07-20 (issue batch #400-#408) Seven fixes in one branch (fix/issue-batch-jul20), root causes all verified against 3f654fd before design. Non-obvious decisions: (#402) QueuedEvent priority was dead code - nothing in production ever set Spec.Priority, so EffectiveQueuePriority's nil-default 2 applied to every class including alert; fix stamps it explicitly in EnqueueEvent (Alert->0, Normal->2) and ensureTicket passes WithPriority(0) for kind=incident so an incident's DOWNSTREAM stages (class=Normal clarify/implement/review tickets) also outrank sweep backlog - the downstream leg, not the alert pool itself, was the real starvation site. (#403) EnterStage now no-ops X->X at TWO points: the top-of-function from==to early-out AND inside the IllegalTransitionError handler (the FitTask fresh-read can see a stage another writer already committed - that inner site is the production parked->parked source). Self-edges in F.3 stay rejected (4th time): stage.Enter's clock resets (StageEnteredAt/PodStartedAt/PodRecreations) are not idempotent. (#404) 401/403 from Merge maps to scm.ErrAuthFailed at both providers (GitLab AND GitHub - only GitLab had fired but parity was mandatory) -> parked(merge-auth-refused), a two-place F.3+F.5 edit, deliberately NO F.6 unpark re-entry (same disposition as review-post-refused: token rotation needs a human). No dedicated counter - RecordSCM already labels status=401 and terminal dashboards key off stage_reason. (#406) The investigating->implementing escalation came through Unpark's ReasonNoOutcome case, which unconditionally targeted Implementing for any non-review kind; guard is ParkedFromStage in {implementing, reviewing} - parking BEFORE the C.6 approval gate must never escalate. Second hole: incidentComment's SCM-error branch returned without o.release(), leaving a bare OutcomeAccepted claim that suppressed nothing. (#408) fold/reap handovers were already atomic via own.HandOverController; the real two-Update window was DemoteMRController (standalone demote Update) + ownMergeRequest's later mint Update, reached from reMintReviewOwner (post-reap) and mrTakeover. Fixed by threading expectFrom (variadic) into ownMergeRequest/bindMRToTask/MintReviewTask/MintOrUnparkTakeoverTask and doing demote+promote in ownMergeRequest's single Update; DemoteMRController deleted (no callers left). (#401) The brainstorm heartbeat freeze was the refine pre-scan barrier holding proceed=false with zero log/metric while its gating refine Task never terminated - all state etcd-backed, so redeploys never cleared it; barrier now logs+counts every held tick (SweepErrorsTotal brainstorm/refine_barrier_held) and force-releases after requeueRefineBarrierMaxHold=2h (refine_barrier_timeout). (#400) Investigation-comment cooldown mirrors the refire-comment pattern with its OWN marker fields (LastInvestigationCommentAt/SuppressedInvestigationCount, never shared with LastRefireCommentAt per the LastDeployTimeoutCommentAt precedent); suppressed appends still terminate the Task rejected(tracked-elsewhere) - only the forge write is bounded; env INCIDENT_INVESTIGATION_COMMENT_COOLDOWN_MINUTES, default 30m.
- 2026-07-23 (operator#74) generic `mcpServers` field lands per operator#74; reserved-name enforcement is deliberately the wrapper's, not the operator's.
- 2026-07-24: `Project.spec.agent` gains `skillSources` ([]AgentSkillSource:
  name/url/ref/subdir, mirrors AgentMCPServer) + `promptAppendByKind`
  (map[string]string, `*` wildcard appended before the kind entry via
  `AgentSpec.PromptAppendFor`, consumed by `assignmentFor` after the built-in
  per-kind `agentJob` text). `skillSources` serializes to the
  `TATARA_EXTRA_SKILL_SOURCES` pod env (JSON array), mirroring
  `TATARA_EXTRA_MCP_SERVERS`. Skill-source auth reuses the project's existing
  `scmSecretRef` token via the wrapper's global `GIT_TOKEN` credential
  helper - no new secret path needed. `assignmentFor` gained a third `*Project`
  arg; all call sites (task_stage.go, assignment_test.go,
  assignment_goal_test.go) updated.
- 2026-07-24: Moved the locked opus tier from `claude-opus-4-8` to
  `claude-opus-5` across `kindDefaultModel` (pod.go), the tier-revert goal
  text (incident/goal.go) and the CRD doc comment; `claude-sonnet-5` and the
  `documentation`/`refine` entries are unchanged. No enum constrains a model
  ID value anywhere in the CRD - the only guard is the CEL rule
  `self.all(k, self[k].startsWith('claude-') && self[k].size() <= 64)` at
  `api/v1alpha1/project_types.go`, which only rejects non-`claude-` values,
  so `internal/controller/project_tiermap_validation_test.go` deliberately
  keeps its `claude-opus-4-8` literals (it tests that guard, not the tier)
  and is the sole remaining occurrence of the old ID in the repo. The tests
  that had to move (not just cosmetic renames) are the ones asserting the
  LOCKED per-kind default itself: `modelForKind` resolves
  `kindDefaultModel[kind]` ahead of `Spec.Agent.Model` whenever the kind has
  an entry, so any project-wide `Model` literal left at the old value in a
  test would have been silently shadowed rather than exercised.
- 2026-07-25 (fix/sweep-heartbeat-and-alert-false-positives, #441/#446) **The
  sweep heartbeat gauge had three writers and one series.**
  `operator_sweep_last_success_timestamp_seconds` was declared
  `[]string{"activity"}` only while `runScans` rehydrates it from EVERY
  Project's persisted status on every reconcile - measured on a single scrape
  target, `issueScan` cycled through exactly three values within 8 seconds and
  then held for four hours (#446 Q3), so the alert paged critical and
  self-resolved eight minutes later with no sweep having run, and a Project
  whose cron was genuinely dead was masked by a healthy sibling's write. Fixed
  by COMPLETING THE LABEL SET (`project` first on both
  `SweepLastSuccessTimestamp` and `SweepErrorsTotal`), not by detecting
  backward movement: that is the confirmed practice (temporalio/temporal#9600
  was fixed the same way) and no source supports alerting on non-monotonicity.
  Metric NAMES are unchanged, preserving the naming-stability discipline of the
  2026-07-18 #325 entry. (1) `SweepErrorsTotal`'s `init()` pre-seeding could
  not survive - project names are unknown at process start - so it moved to
  `obs.SeedSweepErrorsForProject`, called from `ProjectReconciler.Reconcile` on
  every pass (idempotent; `WithLabelValues` returns the existing child),
  keeping `increase(operator_sweep_errors_total[1h])` well-defined from a
  Project's first reconcile rather than its first error. (2) New gauge
  `operator_sweep_next_expected_timestamp_seconds{project,activity}` moves
  cadence knowledge into the PRODUCER (the kube-state-metrics
  `kube_cronjob_next_schedule_time` pattern), not the alert rule: the obvious
  alternative, a `group_left` companion-interval join in the alert expression
  itself, ships in no mainstream OSS project found in review and carries two
  sharp runtime failure modes (`multiple matches for labels` when the join is
  not exactly one-to-one, and a missing series on either side silently killing
  the whole alert) - the interim flat 6h threshold was breached for ~18h of
  every 24 by the `0 3 * * *` / `0 6 * * *` nightly crons. It is published only
  for activities that are ACTUALLY ENABLED (`SweepEnabled`,
  `Brainstorm.Enabled`, `Documentation.Enabled && .Repo != ""`), not merely
  scheduled - a next-expected-run for a run that will never happen is the same
  false page one level down. Publish is `defer`red from the top of `runScans`
  (not called eagerly) because `stampScan` mutates `proj.Status.Last*` in place
  partway through the same function - publishing at the top would leave the
  gauge stale for up to `maxScheduleRequeue` = 6h, longer than the consuming
  alert's 3h grace. (3) `invalid_cron` is now metered in exactly ONE place,
  `publishNextExpected`; the three per-activity branches in `runScans` keep
  their logs and lost their increments, closing the gaps where a bad cron on a
  disabled-feature path was never metered at all. (4)
  `agent_internal_issue_total{category,severity}` added at the
  `action=agent_internal_issue` log site so tatara-observability#63 can move
  that alert off a `pattern | line_format | json` LogQL pipeline that took
  ~9.6s over ~50 lines and paged on its own query timeout; the free-text
  `description` stays a log field, deliberately not a label. Not pre-seeded -
  the category enum lives in the wrapper and the consuming rule is `> 0` with
  no `or vector(0)`, so an absent series is correctly NoData -> OK. (5)
  issueScan/sweep do NOT publish the raw cron boundary: `reposDueForScan`
  phase-shifts each enrolled repo by `fnv32a(project\0repo\0activity) mod
  period` (issue #181) so per-repo fires spread across the interval instead of
  bunching at one boundary, and the raw `sched.Next(base)` the gauge first
  shipped with was a deterministic false page for any low-repo-count project -
  the true earliest fire can land up to a full period later. Fixed with
  `earliestIssueScanFire`, which reuses
  `cronPeriod`/`scanOffset`/`repoNextFire` verbatim (the exact helpers
  `reposDueForScan` itself calls) rather than reimplementing the arithmetic, so
  the two paths cannot re-diverge; `brainstorm`/`documentation` run through the
  unshifted `activityDue` and are correctly unshifted as-is - do not unify the
  two formulas without re-deriving which runtime path each activity actually
  uses (a test literal that agreed with the wrong formula is exactly how this
  shipped once already). (6) The deferred publish threads `repos` and a
  `reposLoaded bool` through a closure (both predeclared before the `defer`,
  since `repos` does not exist yet at defer-statement time); a `scanReader`
  failure (dead/renamed `scmSecretRef`) or a repo-list failure both leave
  `repos` at its nil zero value - indistinguishable from a Project that
  genuinely enrolled zero repos - and `runScans` returns a NIL error on the
  `scanReader` path, so no other metric catches it either. `reposLoaded`, set
  true only after `projectReposForScan` succeeds, disambiguates the two:
  not-loaded leaves the previously published series INTACT (retracting it would
  silence the alert exactly when the sweep is broken), loaded-and-empty still
  retracts (a genuinely repo-less Project is a config state, not a broken one).
  (7) A `prometheus.GaugeVec` child, once created via `WithLabelValues`, is
  exported at its last `.Set()` value for the life of the process with no
  implicit expiry, so `ProjectReconciler.Reconcile`'s `IsNotFound` branch now
  calls
  `obs.SweepNextExpectedTimestamp.DeletePartialMatch(prometheus.Labels{"project":
  req.Name})` before returning - the only place a deleted Project's per-project
  gauge children CAN be retracted, since nothing else in the reconcile graph
  runs for an object that no longer exists. Known remaining gap:
  `SweepLastSuccessTimestamp` and `SweepErrorsTotal` are torn down NOWHERE on
  Project deletion and leak a frozen child per deleted Project until pod
  restart; only `SweepNextExpectedTimestamp` got teardown here, because only it
  feeds a `time() - gauge` alert rule that would page - the other two should be
  swept up together in one follow-up rather than patched one at a time as each
  gets independently noticed. The companion alert-rule changes are
  tatara-observability `fix/sweep-heartbeat-and-alert-false-positives`, which
  MUST merge after this.

  **Final-review fixup (same branch):** point (7)'s teardown had one hole
  left: `runScans`' `Spec.Scm == nil || Spec.Scm.Cron == nil` guard (both
  `+optional`) returns BEFORE the `publishNextExpected` defer is registered,
  so a Project whose `spec.scm.cron` is cleared after publishing series never
  retracts them. Fixed by an explicit
  `SweepNextExpectedTimestamp.DeletePartialMatch` in that early return, NOT by
  nil-guarding `publishNextExpected` and moving the defer above the guard:
  `publishNextExpected` unconditionally dereferences `proj.Spec.Scm.Cron` on
  its first line, so that route needs its own rewrite for no added safety over
  mirroring the already-established `IsNotFound` precedent one function up.
  Also: the operator's OWN chart (`prometheusrule.yaml`'s `TataraSweepStalled`)
  still read `max by (activity)` post-relabel - the exact masking bug #441
  fixed everywhere else, missed in the one place shipped in this repo's own
  release; regrouped to `(project, activity)` and the stale "zero-error"
  description corrected to the #325 liveness semantics. `nightlySweep` (dead
  since #325, zero producers) dropped from `SeedSweepErrorsForProject`'s
  seed list: 13 of 44 seeded series per Project were permanently-zero
  fiction, now 13 of 31.
- 2026-07-25 (#428) Upstream LightRAG retries Postgres ~10x on boot but exits
  fatally (no retry) if Neo4j is unreachable, so a readiness probe on
  `internal/memory/lightrag.go`'s container cannot help - the process is
  already dead by the time a probe would fail, not merely unready. Fixed with
  a `wait-for-neo4j` initContainer (reuses `cfg.LightragImage`, which already
  ships python3, rather than a new tool image) that TCP-polls
  `bolt://<neo4j>:7687` and exits 1 after ~5 minutes if it never comes up,
  which surfaces as a bounded, diagnosable Init:CrashLoopBackOff instead of a
  silent hang. Postgres is deliberately NOT gated - the incident's own Loki
  evidence shows the upstream 10x retry already working in practice, so
  gating it too would duplicate behaviour that isn't broken. The sibling
  `tatara-memory` container (`memory_builders.go:76-80`) solved the analogous
  slow-dependency-boot problem with a StartupProbe instead, because its own
  failure mode is different: it blocks in `waitForDB` but never exits, so
  liveness (not a dead process) was the risk there.
- 2026-07-26 (#432) `Cluster.spec.bootstrap` is WRITE-ONCE and fails SILENTLY.
  No webhook rejects a change to it and no condition or event records one, but
  cnpg reads it only inside `createPrimaryInstance`, which the cluster
  controller calls solely when `Status.Instances == 0` (cnpg v1.29.1
  `internal/controller/cluster_controller.go:1063`) - so adding
  `bootstrap.recovery` to a LIVE cluster is accepted by the API server and then
  ignored forever. Consequence for the WAL-archiving work: the steady-state
  `PGCluster()` render MUST keep `bootstrap.initdb`. Swapping in a recovery
  stanza would be a no-op on all three existing clusters AND would become
  active on the next FRESH Project, where recovery would fail against an empty
  archive instead of running InitDB. Restore therefore lives in a separate
  builder (`PGClusterFromBackup`) with no automatic caller, driven by a human
  after the PVCs are gone. That builder also has to give the recovered cluster
  a DIFFERENT `backup.barmanObjectStore.serverName` from the
  `externalClusters[]` source, or the fresh cluster writes its own timeline
  into the very archive folder it is restoring from.
- 2026-07-26 (#432) Turning on WAL archiving CREATES a new disk-full risk that
  none of the existing alerts can see, which is why the alerting shipped in the
  same change rather than as a follow-up. When `archive_command` fails,
  PostgreSQL retains every un-shipped segment in `pg_wal` indefinitely and the
  cnpg Cluster PHASE STAYS Healthy throughout - only the `ContinuousArchiving`
  CONDITION flips - so a phase-keyed rule is structurally blind to it. Left
  alone it reproduces the ~3.5h #240 disk-full write outage on all three
  clusters at once, and `max_slot_wal_keep_size` (added for #240) does NOT
  help: it bounds only what a REPLICATION SLOT may pin, not what an un-archived
  segment pins. Two rules, both metric names live-verified against this
  cluster's Prometheus: `MemoryPostgresWALArchiveFailing`
  (`cnpg_pg_stat_archiver_last_failed_time > ..._last_archived_time`, epoch
  seconds with -1 as the never-happened sentinel, primary-scoped by cnpg's own
  `predicate_query`) for an archive command that ERRORS, and
  `MemoryPostgresWALArchiveBacklog`
  (`cnpg_collector_pg_wal_archive_status{value="ready"} > 100`) for one that
  HANGS or is merely slower than WAL is generated - the second is not
  redundant, because a hang records no failure at all and leaves both
  timestamps frozen, keeping the first rule quiet forever.
- 2026-07-26 (#432) Two cnpg API traps worth remembering, both verified against
  the vendored source rather than docs. (1) The barman compression enums are
  NOT symmetric: `wal.compression` accepts bzip2|gzip|lz4|snappy|xz|zstd but
  `data.compression` accepts bzip2|gzip|snappy ONLY (barman-cloud v0.5.0
  `pkg/api/config.go`), so lz4/xz/zstd on `data` is a CRD rejection at apply
  time and nothing catches it before the cluster. Proven empirically by
  temporarily setting zstd and watching envtest reject it against the vendored
  CRD; `postgresql.cnpg.io_scheduledbackups.yaml` was vendored beside the
  existing Cluster CRD so the ScheduledBackup path gets the same real-schema
  validation. gzip is used on both sides: the only value legal in both enums,
  and certain to exist in the operand image (a missing codec would fail
  `archive_command`, i.e. cause the exact outage this feature guards against).
  (2) `ScheduledBackup.spec.schedule` is robfig/cron with a leading SECONDS
  field - SIX fields, not the five-field Kubernetes CronJob form. A five-field
  value is accepted and silently means something else ("0 2 * * *" becomes
  hourly), so `pgBackupSchedule` rejects any non-six-field value and falls back
  to the default with a warning. `backupOwnerReference` likewise defaults to
  `none`, which orphans every Backup object produced; set to `cluster`.
- 2026-07-26 (#432) `deleteScheduledBackup` must tolerate a NO-KIND-MATCH as
  well as NotFound. On a cluster whose cnpg install lacks the scheduledbackups
  CRD, a Delete returns "no matches for kind" rather than NotFound, and since
  the delete runs on every reconcile where backups are off (the default), a
  strict NotFound-only check took the ENTIRE memory stack down for a feature
  nobody enabled. Caught by envtest, which installs only the vendored Cluster
  CRD - that red was a real production failure mode, not a test artefact.
- 2026-07-26 (#443) Task names are now GenerateName-minted
  (`queue.BuildTaskFromQueuedEvent`), which fixes the branch collision - a
  project-scoped dedup key ("brainstorm-<project>") produced ONE constant
  QueuedEvent name, ONE constant Task name and therefore ONE constant
  `tatara/task-*` branch per project+kind, forever, so every cycle collided
  with the last cycle's abandoned branch. The non-obvious part is the
  REPLACEMENT it forced: `admit` used `Create` returning `AlreadyExists` as
  BOTH its idempotency and its self-heal primitive, and a GenerateName mint
  can never collide, so a failure between the `Create` and the Admitted status
  patch would have minted a SECOND Task (second pod, second branch, second
  token spend) on the next pass. `DispatcherReconciler.mintedTask` replaces
  it: adopt-by-label before minting. DO NOT DELETE IT AS REDUNDANT - it looks
  redundant precisely because it is doing the job `AlreadyExists` used to.
  Two details are load-bearing: the label is `tatara.dev/minted-by`, NOT
  `tatara.dev/queued-event` (which `admitTicket` rewrites onto the current
  per-stage ticket at the Task's first stage step), and its value is the
  event's UID, NOT its name (`QueuedEventName` repeats every cycle, so a name
  match would let a new cycle adopt - and, if terminal, DELETE - the previous
  cycle's Task). The read goes through `APIReader` for the same cache-lag
  reason as fix M28. Also: `*scm.GitHub`/`*scm.GitLab` finally implement
  `DeleteBranch`, so the reaper's B.6 step-4 branch delete stopped being a
  logged no-op; GitHub answers a missing ref with 422 "Reference does not
  exist" on that endpoint alone, normalized to 404 in the adapter because the
  operator's permanent-gone vocabulary is 404/410. The delete is gated on
  `liveTaskPushingTo`, which asks `agent.PushBranch` and NOT
  `agent.TaskBranch`: a takeover Task pushes to the abandoned MR's existing
  head branch (derived from a DIFFERENT Task's name), so a TaskBranch scan
  answers "nobody pushes here" for the one case where deleting destroys live
  work - and parked counts as LIVE there, because a parked(ownership-lost)
  takeover Task is unparked straight back into approved.
- 2026-07-26 (#442, #425) CNPG's own remediation cannot see a diverged standby,
  because its decision is gated on reading that instance's instance-manager HTTP
  endpoint (`:8000/pg/status`) - which is exactly what is NOT up when the
  container is crash-looping. Same trap one level down: the whole `cnpg_pg_*`
  metric family is produced by CNPG's user-query collector, so those series
  vanish whenever the collector's query fails, independent of whether the
  instance is up and scrapeable. Anything meant to DETECT a dead or diverged
  member must therefore key off a signal that survives the member being dead:
  `Cluster.Status.{CurrentPrimary,ReadyInstances,InstancesStatus,DanglingPVC}`
  (written by the operator, not the sick pod) on the control-plane side, or
  `up{}` / the surviving PRIMARY's view of its slots on the metrics side. Never
  off the sick instance's own status or its own `cnpg_pg_*` series.
  `internal/controller/project_memory.go` reads the Cluster-status set and
  surfaces it as `MemoryReady=False/PostgresDegraded` with the phase left
  Ready - deliberately NOT a phase gate: #215 and #355 exist precisely because
  tightening the quorum gate flaps the whole fleet, and a degraded-but-quorate
  cluster is still serving. `status.memory.notReady` names which of
  postgres/neo4j/lightrag/memory-api is holding a Provisioning stack, whose
  absence is what made #425 undiagnosable from the Project alone.

- 2026-07-27 (handoff scope inheritance) **The project brainstorm goal named NINE tools that do not exist**, and the test suite pinned two of them. `list_handoffs`/`get_handoff` were specced by the 2026-07-04 handoff-continuation design and never built (the platform pivoted to free-text `task_note(kind="handoff")`); `propose_issue`/`skip_research` are in tatara-cli's own `TestReapedToolsAreGone`; `code_related`/`code_important`/`code_cross_repo`/`code_bridges`/`code_communities` were folded into `code_context(rel=)` and `code_graph(op=)`. Agents never reported any of it - they silently fell back to `task_list`, which returns every live Task's notes unfiltered, and project mtg inherited a single-deck scope through four consecutive cycles. Fix is in two halves. (a) `internal/promptguidance/toolnames.go` holds a hand-maintained mirror of tatara-cli's agent-visible tool names plus `UnknownToolNames(prose)`, and every goal builder is pinned to it (`TestGoalBuildersNameOnlyRealTools` in controller/incident/refine). Extraction is backticked-span + a strict shape (`^[a-z][a-z0-9]*(_[a-z0-9]+)+$`, call-args stripped) chosen so `decks/`, `%ct`, `values.yaml`, `decision=close` and `tatara-council-brainstorm` can never be false positives: a checker with false positives grows an allow-list, and an allow-list is how this bug survived. (b) The goal paragraph now puts the MANDATE FIRST, frames a handoff as EVIDENCE that never narrows scope, requires re-deriving the target every cycle, and adds WIDEN ON REPEAT - because mtg's agents re-derived correctly every time and stayed stuck, so re-derivation alone does not break a wedge. Paired with `tatara-agent-skills` teaching the WRITE side (`handoff`, `tatara-council-brainstorm` step 7). **Known limit, deliberate:** the check only sees BACKTICKED mentions, so `internal/refine/goal.go` - which instructs `list_issues`, `list_commits`, `close_issue`, `edit_issue`, `submit_turn`, `exit_plan_mode`, none of which exist, and never names `submit_outcome` at all - passes it vacuously. Not fixed here: it needs a decision about which real call shapes replace them. `internal/promptguidance/toolnames_test.go` asserts the limit so it stays a decision. `issueGoal` and `takeoverGoal` are excluded from the check on purpose: they render third-party issue/comment text verbatim, so their backticks are user data. Addendum: `documentationGoal` was extracted and pinned by this change, but its only caller `documentationScan` has NO production callers (see the comment at projectscan.go recording that `MintDocBatch` replaced it), so the live documentation goal is `docBatchGoal` in docbatch.go, now pinned alongside it; the dead path is left in place deliberately, for the maintainer to decide on separately. Also verified during this change: `tatara-agent-skills`' `validate_tool_calls.py` now resolves a REAL tool manifest reporting 21 tools, exactly matching `AgentVisibleTools`, so that repo's MEMORY.md note about it 404ing and passing vacuously is stale.

- 2026-07-27 The identity-unverified unpark was a ONE-SHOT and it stalled a Task for a day with the approval label visible on the forge (gitlab helmfile#26). Three fixes, not one: Task.status.approvalVerdict makes the grammar pass durable, driveUnparks stops excluding the reason and re-derives the rest from live state, and loadOwnedIssues reads through APIReader. The backstop is the fix; the uncached read is the escape hatch.
- 2026-07-27 conversing is a 16th stage rather than a warm pod kept alive through parked, because parked is in terminalStages AND podlessStages, so a warm parked pod would be reaped by the reaper and run off the MaxConcurrentAgents books. Pod-bearing and non-terminal costs zero carve-outs in the reaper, the accountant and the transition table; each carve-out avoided maps to a named 2026-06/2026-07 production incident.
- 2026-07-27 The AgentPodTTLSeconds vs conversation-idle conflict is resolved by two clocks with different jobs, NOT by touching the wrapper: agent.PodTTLSeconds says when ONE POD rotates (the wrapper 410s past podStart plus the env it stamps), Task.status.conversationLastEventAt says when the CONVERSATION ends. ttlStop leaves the stage unchanged, so a conversing pod rotates and the conversation continues (D6). The idle clock deliberately does NOT use stageWorkStartedAt: ttlStop nils it and podwatch re-stamps it, which would reset the idle timer on every rotation and make an idle conversation immortal.
- 2026-07-27 conversing runs the clarify agent kind. An eighth agent kind would need matching tool and skill profiles in tatara-cli, tatara-agent-skills and the wrapper's installer; the clarify agent is already the conversational one and its outcome vocabulary is the where-does-this-go decision.
- 2026-07-27 `/v1/interject` is deliberately NOT restored for mid-conversation delivery. The wrapper's own MEMORY.md records why it was deleted: mid-turn PTY injection races the Stop hook and the tailer. Turn-boundary delivery via `<events>` on the next queued turn is the correct mechanism instead, and it needed no wrapper change at all - only the operator side (the conversing stage, its follow-up SubmitTurn call site) had to exist.
- 2026-07-27 The first comment into a new conversation still pays a cold spawn, on purpose: `EnterStage` tears down the previous stage's pod on every entry, including the parked/clarifying/reviewing -> conversing edge, and no carve-out was permitted (`internal/controller/conversing.go`) - a surviving pod is per-TASK named, so reusing one across a stage change would silently run the wrong kind/model/skills. The conversing pod's turn-0 bundle carries the whole mirror thread, notes journal and `<events>`, so nothing is lost; warmth applies only to the SECOND and later comments in that conversation.
- 2026-07-27 Maintainer calls, taken with the downside stated rather than silently dropped: no acknowledgement layer on a delivered follow-up comment, no absolute pod lifetime ceiling while conversing (only the per-rotation PodTTLSeconds and the idle clock bound it), and no ping-pong cap between two agent kinds - `BotRounds` is counted and exposed on `operator_bot_rounds` but read by nothing that acts on it.
- 2026-07-28 (security review correction) The safety of entering conversing from `parked(identity-unverified)` does NOT come from `LegalFor`'s GUARD 1 - that guard keys on `Task.Spec.Kind == "review"` (`internal/stage/stage.go` LegalFor), not on the agent kind, and a non-review Task is never blocked by it on this edge. The real guarantee is `verifyApprovalScope` (`internal/restapi/outcome.go:1277,1332`), which re-runs the live C.6 grammar over every owned Issue on every `submit_outcome` and never reads `ApprovalVerdict`. A clarify agent CAN move a Task toward code execution via `decision=implement` -> `approved` -> admission, exactly like clarifying can; only the DIRECT `conversing -> implementing/merging/approved` edge is blocked by GUARD 1, which IMPORTANT 3 widened during this review to cover the conversing path explicitly (`stage.go` GUARD 1 comment). An earlier version of this branch had the wrong reasoning (crediting the kind guard alone) written into several test comments; those were corrected in the same review pass.
- 2026-07-28 (security review, three real defects from one missing-field pattern) `UnparkInput` has three production builders in this package (`ApplyUnpark`, `reverifyParked` in `internal/webhook/pending_events.go`, and `unparkFires` in `internal/controller/reaper.go`) that must stay in lockstep on every field, and nothing enforced that. Three separate defects on this branch each came from one builder silently leaving a field at its zero value: `GrammarPassed` (finding 3, could reap a Task the driver was about to re-enter on its very next pass), then `ConversingHasRoom` (CRITICAL 1, same reap-out-from-under-the-driver class for the new conversing edge), then `MaxTurnsPerTask` (NEW-1, PRE-EXISTING and live before this branch: every `parked(no-outcome)` Task's turn count read as satisfied for any value, so `unparkFires` always declined it and `reapParked` deleted it once `ParkRetention` elapsed regardless of real turns), and separately `MRs` in `reverifyParked` (NEW-2, made the kind=review already-merged guard structurally inert on that one path; bounded to pod waste, not a security bypass, because GUARD 1 still blocks review-kind admission). A field-by-field audit table against all three builders now lives as `unparkFires`' doc comment in `internal/controller/reaper.go`.
- 2026-07-28 `MintStage` gained a trusted-human-author clause (after tatara-parked, before webhook-marker/humanHasLastWord - order irrelevant, all three return the same result) so a maintainer's/reporter's issue mints ACTIVE even when webhook delivery was lost. Narrowed from the literal `IsTrustedAuthor(...)` plan to `iss.Author != botLoginOf(proj) && IsTrustedAuthor(...)`: `IsTrustedAuthor` treats the project's own bot login as a trusted insider (`api/v1alpha1/logins.go:61`), so the literal clause would have minted every bot-authored orphan issue too - same precedent as `ClassifyPR` clause 2 (`internal/controller/sweep.go:513-516`) on the PR side. `MintStage` gained a `*Repository` param (call sites `intake.go:142`, `sweep.go`'s `sweepIssues`, both already had `repo` in scope). MEASURED BLAST RADIUS (fix round 1): 6 orphan open Issue CRs cluster-wide (tatara 4, infrastructure 1, mtg 1) are sweep-eligible; 153 Tasks parked, 27 stage-reason backlog-sweep and retaining a controller owner so they do NOT re-mint until reaped; per-pass exposure further bounded by `maxNewTasksPerSweep`/`maxOpenTasks`. Pinned by `TestMintStage/a_BOT-authored_orphan_issue_is_NOT_a_trusted_human_and_does_not_mint_ACTIVE` and `TestSweepCutoverBacklogFromATrustedMaintainerMintsActiveInstead`.
- 2026-07-28 Brainstorm refill now also chains on Task COMPLETION (`Owns(&Task{})` + `brainstormCycleFinishedPredicate`, reusing `TaskDone`), not only Issue verdicts, since a SKIP writes no Issue and `Watches(&Issue{})` never fired for it. CORRECTED (fix round): the original claim that this waited out the 15m `brainstormResyncInterval` (5x for project mtg) is FALSE - `driveUnparksPaced` self-requeues every ~30s (`defaultUnparkDriveInterval`) regardless, so a skipped cycle was already re-evaluated within that floor. Real win: an immediate wake plus bypassing the workqueue rate limiter during Project reconcile error backoff. It also does NOT rescue the five-consecutive-skips symptom: `brainstormRefillDecision`'s skip breaker trips for `trigger==TriggerEvent` at `consecutiveSkips >= maxSkips` (3) - the exact trigger this edge routes through - so from skip 3 on, this wake is suppressed by the same breaker; only the CRON tick resets it. 15m resync stays as the correctness floor (level-based, not edge-based). HISTORY (M5 fix round): the skip breaker and the 15m CRON tick described in this paragraph are both GONE, retired by `c6a4b51` and `c0a50f9` respectively, same as the line-4 disclaimer covers - this entry predates both and was written in present tense with no marker of its own.
- 2026-07-28 `ClearWebhookOriginated` (`internal/controller/sweep.go`) changed from documented-idempotent (missing CR = no-op) to erroring after `webhookMarkerBackoff` exhausts (~3.1s / 5 attempts) when the Issue CR is genuinely gone, now retrying a `NotFound` like it always retried a `Conflict` (a cached `Get` can lose the same read-after-write race `MarkWebhookOriginated` lost on a CR this same request may have just created; mtg-decks#9's sibling defect). Semantic change to a SHARED function across four call sites (`internal/webhook/server.go:669`, `server.go:797`, `mirror_refresh.go:183`, `sweep.go`'s wrapper) - all already log non-fatally so no caller needed to change, but a future caller assuming "missing CR is always a no-op" is now wrong.
- 2026-07-28 The reaper's comment -> label -> release ordering (`internal/controller/reaper.go:885-893`, which blocks the reap on any non-404/410 label error) is now the SOLE guard against `MintStage`'s trusted-author clause re-minting a reaped issue ACTIVE. Author standing is immutable, unlike `humanHasLastWord` which self-extinguishes once the bot has the last word, so `tatara-parked` landing before the Issue is released is that clause's only off-switch.
- 2026-07-28 (reachability fix) The `exhausted` brainstorm action landed correct on the `internal/restapi/outcome.go` handler but unreachable: no agent-facing text taught it. Fixed the two brainstorm prompt strings only (`internal/controller/assignment.go`'s `agentJob`, `internal/controller/projectscan.go`'s EARLY EXIT paragraph) to teach all three actions and the skip-vs-exhausted distinction (skip = nothing this cycle, costs one more session soon; exhausted = nothing until the project moves, pauses the project, one report is enough). `tatara-cli`'s schema/validator and `tatara-agent-skills`' guardrails skill fixed in lockstep, separate repos. No Go logic touched; no existing test asserted the old wording, so nothing else needed updating.
- 2026-07-28 (agent-judged approval gate, step A) The webhook's bespoke `reverifyParked` limb is DELETED, along with `verdictFrom` and `Server.loadOwnedIssues` (`internal/webhook/pending_events.go`). `parked(identity-unverified)` now joins awaiting-human and backlog-sweep on the ONE shared `driveCommentUnpark` -> `ApplyUnpark`, which is strictly better on the axis the limb existed for: its in-loop `Get` uses the uncached `APIReader`, the exact read that lost the 2026-07-27 cache race. Two consequences worth knowing. (1) The `UnparkInput` builder count in the 2026-07-28 three-builder entry above is now TWO (`ApplyUnpark`, `unparkFires`); the field-by-field audit in `unparkFires`' doc comment was rewritten to match, and every "NOT set by `reverifyParked`" caveat is gone because the builder is. (2) NOTHING in production writes `Task.status.approvalVerdict` any more, so `grammarPassedFor` is permanently false for every Task in the cluster between step A and step F. For a Task with a human comment on it that means CONVERSING is the only reachable target, never `implementing`. For the `AutoApproveTataraProposals` carve-out it is WORSE than that and worth stating separately: such a proposal has NO re-entry at all. `autoApproveApplies`/`autoApprovalEvidence` sit inside `verifyOneIssue`, whose one surviving PRODUCTION caller is the method `GrammarVerifier.VerifyApproval` (`approval_grammar.go:363-378`), reached from restapi's `submit_outcome` scope check (`internal/restapi/outcome.go:1339`) - which needs a live pod, and a parked Task has none. (CORRECTED 2026-07-28, step B: the first version of this entry, and the matching carve-out paragraph in `internal/controller/unpark.go`, both said the live path was `VerifyApprovalDetailed`. It is not - `GrammarVerifier.VerifyApproval` calls `verifyOneIssue` directly and never touches `VerifyApprovalDetailed`, which has NO production caller at all: its only callers are the package-level `VerifyApproval` (`:395`) and `ReVerifyParkedDetailed` (`:565`), both test-only since step A. The conclusion is unchanged - a parked Task has no live pod, so it cannot reach the carve-out either way.) Every other exit from `parked(identity-unverified)` requires `hasNonBotEvent`, i.e. a human comment, and the carve-out's defining condition is ZERO maintainer comments. So an auto-approvable proposal parked at identity-unverified strands until `ParkRetention` reaps it. All of this is a DELIBERATE intermediate state of the A-F deletion sequence, not a regression to chase - the wordlist itself (`MatchesApprovalPhrase`) is deliberately untouched here and dies in step F. (CORRECTED 2026-07-29, step G: TWO errors in that clause. It died in step E.2, not F - see the E.2 entry below. And step F restored NOTHING: there is no verdict writer at HEAD and there was never going to be one. The durable `Task.status.approvalVerdict` was deleted outright and the grant moved to `restapi.verifyApprovalScope`, which decides per REQUEST and persists only `Issue.status.approval`. Anyone reading this line as a promise that the record comes back will go looking for a writer that does not exist.) Three code comments carry the same warning inline: `internal/controller/project_controller.go` (the F.6 driver call site), `internal/controller/unpark.go` (`driveUnparks` and the carve-out paragraph in the file header) and `internal/controller/reaper.go` (the `unparkFires` field audit).
- 2026-07-28 (decision D2) The on-demand issue mirror sync moved OFF the identity-unverified limb and ONTO the plain webhook comment path (`syncOwnedIssueThread`), scoped by the newly exported `controller.TaskOwnsIssue`, and it now runs for ANY live or parked Task rather than only `parked(identity-unverified)`. `Issue.Status.Comments` is the single field BOTH halves of the approval gate read - the agent reads the `external_id` it cites out of the turn-0 bundle rendered from it, and the operator re-verifies that citation against it - and nothing else on the comment path writes it (`mirror_refresh.go` touches Body/Title only; the parked cadence is daily). Leaving the sync on the old limb would have bought the forge read for the one stage that renders no bundles and skipped it for `clarifying`, so every clarify would cite ids the mirror does not have and refuse forever. Failure is best-effort but LOUD: `pending_event_issue_sync_failed` at ERROR plus `operator_mirror_write_dropped_total{site="issue_sync"}`.
- 2026-07-28 (agent-judged approval gate, step B) The DURABLE grammar verdict is gone from the un-park path. `grammarPassedFor` (`internal/controller/unpark.go`) is DELETED, both `stage.UnparkInput` builders in this package (`ApplyUnpark`, `unparkFires`) stopped setting `GrammarPassed`, and `ApplyUnpark` lost the parameter every caller already passed as a literal `false` (arity 10 -> 9). `UnparkInput.GrammarPassed` itself deliberately STAYS for now and dies in step C: **delete writers before readers** - a struct literal that OMITS a field still compiles, one that SETS a removed field does not, so reversing that order breaks the tree mid-sequence. Consequence, and it is the point of the step: `parked(identity-unverified)` can now only reach CONVERSING (room permitting) or decline; `implementing` is unreachable from either builder, and the approval decision moves to restapi's `verifyApprovalScope` on a live `submit_outcome(decision=implement)`. The `unparkFires` field-by-field audit comment (`internal/controller/reaper.go`) was REWRITTEN rather than trimmed - it now reads "NINE set by both / ONE set by neither" and names `GrammarPassed` as the one - because a stale audit there has caused probe-vs-driver divergence three times (findings 3, CRITICAL 1, NEW-1) and it is the only artefact keeping the two builders in lockstep. Five verdict-driven tests in `unpark_backstop_test.go` died with the behaviour they pinned; the replacement `TestApplyUnpark_VerdictIsNotConsulted` was fault-injection-verified non-vacuous (re-setting `GrammarPassed` from the verdict makes it fail with `target = implementing`). The approval wordlist (`MatchesApprovalPhrase`) is still INTACT and untouched - it dies in step 6/E.2.
- 2026-07-28 (agent-judged approval gate, step C) `stage.UnparkInput.GrammarPassed` is DELETED and with it the whole implementing tail of `stage.go`'s `ReasonIdentityUnverified` arm (`openIssues`/`allApproved`/`enter(StageImplementing)`). `internal/stage` now does NO approval reasoning of any kind: the arm's only job is responsiveness - a non-bot pendingEvent enters `conversing` when the project is under its conversing ceiling, and declines otherwise - so `parked(identity-unverified) -> implementing` is not merely unreachable, the edge does not exist. #294 holds by construction: this flips the DECISION at the chokepoint (`restapi.verifyApprovalScope`) and adds no EDGE; `TestTransitionTable` stays green with `clarifying -> implementing` ILLEGAL. **D1 (owner decision, Option A):** the no-room case gets its own closed-vocabulary code `DeclineNoConversingRoom = "no-conversing-room"`, mirrored in `internal/controller`, replacing the `grammar-not-passed` label it had worn since step A - a misnomer for a capacity refusal. Option B (falling through to `enter(StageClarifying)`) was REJECTED: it would route around the very conversing ceiling being reported by spawning a clarifying pod instead, and it would drop a `kind=review` Task past the three guards (merged-MR, round cap, round increment) that live only in the conversing branch. `DeclineGrammarNotPassed` and `DeclineNotAllApproved` left the vocabulary with the branches that produced them. Observability (hard rule 13): `no-conversing-room` is the ONE rule decline `driveUnparks` logs at INFO (`action=unpark_declined`) rather than counting silently - it means a human commented and the operator refused to answer them purely on capacity, which is operator-actionable, and the counter alone cannot say WHICH Task went unanswered; bounded by `driveUnparksPaced`'s floor. The `unparkFires` field-by-field audit (`internal/controller/reaper.go`) was REWRITTEN again, to "NINE set by both / NONE set by neither" - the strongest form of the lockstep property, since no `UnparkInput` field is now left at its zero value by either builder. (The header first said TEN, carrying step B's "nine set by both + one set by neither" arithmetic forward as if the tenth had been promoted to set-by-both when it was in fact deleted from the struct; caught in review. `UnparkInput` has exactly NINE fields and the audit's own bullet list enumerates nine - a header that disagrees with its own list is precisely the unreliable-audit failure mode this artefact exists to prevent.) `TestUnpark_IdentityUnverifiedNeverConverses` was DELETED (its premise inverted) and `wiring_fixes_test.go`'s identity-unverified driveUnparks test was folded into `unpark_backstop_test.go`'s same-shaped step-B twin rather than kept as a second copy.
- 2026-07-28 (agent-judged approval gate, step D) `Task.status.approvalVerdict` and the `ApprovalVerdict` API type are DELETED, along with `api/v1alpha1/approval_verdict_test.go`. The field had no production reader or writer left after steps A-C, so this is the last of it, not a behaviour change. WHY IT IS NOT A BREAKING CRD CHANGE: `apiextensions.k8s.io/v1` forces structural schemas with pruning always on, there are ZERO `preserveUnknownFields` / `x-kubernetes-preserve-unknown-fields` anywhere in this repo, `v1alpha1` is the single version so there is no storage-version change and no conversion webhook, and no `+kubebuilder:printcolumn` referenced the field (task_types.go's printcolumns are stage, stageReason, agentKind, spec.kind, projectRef, stats.turns, shortDescription). Live Tasks keep the stored bytes until their next status write and the apiserver then prunes them silently - the same mechanism `TestProjectCR_StaleMRScanBlockIsPrunedNotRejected` already proved for the 2026-07-17 mrScan removal. **ROLLBACK ASYMMETRY, the one operational trap here:** the CRD change is safe forwards but NOT symmetric. Once the new operator has pruned `approvalVerdict` off every live Task, rolling the IMAGE back finds no verdict anywhere, so every `parked(identity-unverified)` Task declines until a human re-comments. That is DEGRADATION, not corruption - no data is wrong, a stalled Task just needs one new comment. Apply the CRD and the image TOGETHER; CRD-first is safe (the old image tolerates a missing optional field), image-rollback-after-prune is what costs you the re-comment.
