Two test consequences worth knowing. `TestApplyUnpark_VerdictIsNotConsulted` is renamed `TestApplyUnpark_IdentityUnverifiedNeverReachesImplementing` and no longer seeds a verdict: with the type gone, the only assertion it can still make is the one that mattered, that an approved owned Issue plus a human comment lands on conversing and never implementing. `TestOutcome_Conversing_ApprovalVerdictIsNeverConsulted` (`internal/restapi`) is replaced by `TestOutcome_Conversing_ImplementRefusedWhenLiveGrammarFails`, which keeps its load-bearing half - it was the ONLY test anywhere building a Task at `StageConversing`, i.e. the sole certification that the Task 9 conversing widening is safe, and deleting it outright would have left that widening uncovered. Its three verdict shapes (absent / zero-value / stale-dated) collapsed to one case because "the stored verdict is never consulted" is now a property of the type system rather than of the handler. Also corrected en route: the generated CRD carried a STALE `approvalVerdict` description ("written by the webhook fast path ... read by the periodic driveUnparks backstop") that step B (`9691ea3`) had falsified in the Go doc comment without re-running the generator, so `make generate manifests` had been dirty on this branch since that commit - it is idempotent and clean again now. Worth a habit: a doc-comment-only edit to a `+kubebuilder`-annotated type IS a manifest change.
- 2026-07-28 (agent-judged approval gate, step E.1) The operator no longer READS a maintainer comment; it verifies the agent's CITATION of one. New API type `ApprovalCitation{ID,Quote}` (deliberately NOT a CRD field - it crosses the `/outcome` wire and the restapi -> controller seam and is verified in the same request; the durable record of a PASS is `ApprovalEvidence`, whose existing `Phrase` now stores the agent's verbatim quote rather than a wordlist entry, so no new evidence field was added). `verifyOneIssue` gained `citations` and lost `phrases`; `mostRecentMaintainerComment` is DELETED and replaced by the `isMaintainerComment` predicate (it is unexported, so leaving it unreferenced would fail `golangci-lint unused` at this step's own checkpoint). Refusal vocabulary is now five: `no-maintainer-comment`, `no-citation`, `citation-not-maintainer`, `quote-not-in-comment`, `evidence-replayed`. **THERE IS NO RECENCY CLAUSE, and that is the deliberate amendment.** Requiring the newest maintainer comment deadlocks an ordinary thread - "go ahead, I approve!" followed by "thanks - ping me when the PR is up" leaves consent unambiguous but nothing citable, so the agent would submit `discuss` every turn and the Task would park at `awaiting-human` forever with no signal. The withdrawal veto moved to the AGENT (it submits `discuss`), and the accepted cost is that the operator's backstop against a MISJUDGING agent is weaker than the wordlist's was: a stale "go ahead" can out-vote a fresh "hold off" if the agent misreads. The citation is likewise NOT checked against `Task.status.stageEnteredAt` - `stage.Enter` re-stamps that on EVERY transition, so park -> human comments -> un-park to conversing (re-stamp) -> agent cites the comment that caused the un-park would refuse itself forever. **RESIDUAL RISK, stated rather than hidden:** the verbatim-quote check closes FABRICATION (the operator re-reads the body it holds itself) but NOT SELECTIVE QUOTING - "go ahead" really does occur in "do not go ahead until CI is green". Closing that would require reading intent, which is exactly what the operator no longer does. **ORDERING, deliberate and fail-closed:** the FIRST citable comment in thread order is the one verified; clauses (c)/(d) then grant or refuse on it with no fall-through to the next citation, so an agent citing several comments gets a verdict on the first one it named that exists, not the most convenient one. `TestVerifyApprovalSingleUseEvidence` pins this. **A missing or failing citation is a 200 REFUSAL + park at `identity-unverified`, never a 400** (`internal/restapi/outcome.go`): the `autoApproveTataraProposals` carve-out has NO comment to cite, so `approvalCitations` cannot be unconditionally required, and a mis-instructed agent must park ONE Task rather than deadlock the fleet. Only malformed JSON, a missing/invalid decision and a blank reason stay 4xx (`DisallowUnknownFields` is on at both decode stages and applies to nested citation objects too). Two boy-scout fixes rode along: the clarify writer assigned `Status.Approval = ev` UNGUARDED where `ev` could be nil, which would write `status=approved` with NO approver - defeating the idempotence short-circuit, defeating the single-use replay guard, and projecting `tatara-approved` to the forge with nobody behind it (the controller's own writer already guarded this); and `obs.AutoApproveTotal` was missing the `m == nil` guard all its siblings have. New counter `operator_approval_refused_total{reason}` (hard rule 13) plus the existing `action=approval_refused` INFO log on every refusal path, wired through `GrammarVerifier.Metrics` at `cmd/manager/wire.go`. **DEVIATION FROM THE STEP PLAN, worth knowing:** the plan said pass `nil` citations from `VerifyApprovalDetailed`; it is threaded instead. That function applies ONE citation set to every live owned Issue, which is exactly what restapi's `verifyApprovalScope` does, so the parameter is the same semantic and not an invented one - and passing `nil` would have hard-coded "this path can never grant a human approval", a lie in code that Task 7 has not deleted yet. The wordlist (`MatchesApprovalPhrase`, `EffectiveApprovalPhrases`) is still INTACT and now unreferenced-but-exported, so `unused` does not flag it; it dies in step E.2. `ApprovalRefusedComment` DID have to change here rather than in E.2 - it switched on the deleted `ApprovalRefusedNoPhrase` - and it lost its `phrases` parameter along with the sentence telling humans to type a magic phrase.
- 2026-07-28 (generator hygiene, repo-level) **A doc-comment-only edit to a `+kubebuilder`-annotated type STILL changes generated output.** controller-gen copies Go doc comments into the CRD's `description` fields, so an edit that touches no field, no tag and no signature is still a manifest change. Step B of the agent-judged approval gate (`9691ea3`) rewrote the `ApprovalVerdict` doc comment without re-running the generator and left `make generate manifests` DIRTY for two commits, until step D (Task 4) deleted the type and noticed. The check is `make generate manifests && git diff --exit-code` on a clean tree, and it belongs after ANY edit under `api/`, comment-only ones included.

- 2026-07-28 (agent-judged approval gate, step E.2) **The wordlist is GONE.** `MatchesApprovalPhrase` and its whole normalisation stack (`normalizeApprovalLines`, `stripApprovalEmphasis`, `isApprovalWordRune`, `trimApprovalTrailer`, `isApprovalEmojiRune`, `approvalFenceRe`/`approvalQuoteRe`/`approvalShortcodeRe`), plus `ApprovalRefusedComment` (zero production callers - the park comment it rendered was never posted), `DefaultApprovalPhrases`, `EffectiveApprovalPhrases` and the CRD field `Project.spec.scm.approvalPhrases` are all DELETED. The unused `regexp` and `unicode` imports were the ordering canary: a partial deletion fails to compile. NOT a breaking CRD change, by the same analysis as step D - `apiextensions.k8s.io/v1` forces structural schemas with pruning always on, ZERO `preserveUnknownFields` / `x-kubernetes-preserve-unknown-fields` in the repo, `v1alpha1` is the single version so no storage-version change and no conversion webhook, and no printcolumn named the field. A Project CR that still sets `approvalPhrases` is silently pruned on its next write; nothing reads it, so pruning it changes no behaviour. **THE PART THAT WAS ALMOST SHIPPED DEAD:** the operator's OWN clarify prompt (`agentJob`, `internal/controller/assignment.go`) still said "you do NOT decide whether the approval is valid: the operator re-verifies every approval against the issue thread (the C.6 grammar)" and never mentioned `approvalCitations` at all. An agent following it would have been refused `no-citation` on every thread where a maintainer had spoken - the feature would have been live and unusable. The arm now teaches the real contract: the agent JUDGES intent, cites `{id, quote}` per live owned Issue with the `external_id` already carried on the `<comment>` element of its turn-0 bundle (so it must not re-crawl the forge), the operator verifies only WHO and WHAT WAS SAID, and **the withdrawal veto is the AGENT'S** because the operator checks no recency. The wording is deliberately aligned with `tatara-agent-skills` `skills/clarify/tatara-clarify-conversation` and `skills/mcp/tatara-mcp-outcome`, which teach the same contract from the agent's side - those two skills and this prompt must be edited together or they drift. One test rewritten rather than deleted: `TestVerifyApprovalRefusalNamesWhatWasMissing` pinned the deleted renderer, and is now `TestVerifyApprovalRefusalReasonsAreDistinct`, pinning the property that still matters (the five reasons are the `reason` label on `operator_approval_refused_total`, so a collision would merge two failure modes into one series). Watch out for the prompt-prose guard: `TestGoalBuildersNameOnlyRealTools` reads any backticked snake_case token as an MCP tool name, so a backticked `external_id` fails it - name it inside the `<comment external_id="...">` span instead.
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step E.2 review fixes) **THE BUNDLE ESCAPES AND THE OPERATOR DID NOT UNESCAPE - the worst defect this branch produced, and it would have shipped a feature that refused ordinary English.** `internal/prompt/escape.go` replaces `& < > " '` with their XML entities in EVERY comment body rendered into the turn-0 bundle (contract E.1, `bundle.go:259`), and `verifyOneIssue` matched the agent's quote against the RAW mirror body. The clarify prompt and both agent skills tell the agent to copy a VERBATIM substring, and what it can see is the escaped form - so a maintainer writing "let's ship it, go ahead" produced a bundle reading `let&apos;s ship it`, the agent cited that exactly as instructed, `strings.Contains` failed, the Task parked at `identity-unverified` with nothing telling the human why, and the next turn plausibly cited the same escaped span and looped. Apostrophes and ampersands are ordinary in approving comments ("that's fine", "go ahead & ship"), so this was the COMMON case, not an edge. **FIXED ON THE OPERATOR SIDE ON PURPOSE**, in the one place that compares (`quoteOccursIn`), never by instructing the agent: an instruction repeated across a prompt and two skills drifts, a single comparison cannot. It tries the quote AS SUBMITTED first and then `html.UnescapeString`'d - BOTH, in that order, because a maintainer who literally typed `&amp;` is matched only by the raw form (unescaping yields `&`, which is not what the body holds) while the bundle form is matched only by the unescaped one. Neither is a weakening: each is a literal containment test against the operator's own body, so a fabricated quote fails both. `html.UnescapeString` is used rather than hand-rolling `escapeXML`'s inverse, which would be a second definition free to drift. `ApprovalEvidence.Phrase` now stores the form that ACTUALLY OCCURS in the body rather than the submitted one, because a human reads it in `kubectl get issue -o yaml` and entity soup there is unreadable. The test builds its escaped inputs by calling `prompt.EscapeText` rather than writing `&apos;` by hand, so it cannot drift from `escape.go`; fault-injected (removing the unescape branch fails 5 rows).

- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step E.2 review fixes) **`verifyApprovalScope` had a gate hole a HUMAN could walk through: closing the issue RELEASED the work.** `internal/restapi/outcome.go`. `ownedIssues` returns every owned Issue whatever its state; `GrammarVerifier.VerifyApproval` answers `(iss.Status.Approval, true)` for an out-of-scope one - correct in isolation, since a closed thread is not pending approval and must not block the others, but that stored approval is routinely nil; and `verifyApprovalScope` refused only on `len(issues)==0`. So a Task whose ONLY Issue a human had CLOSED produced `granted=true` over an all-nil map, Task 5's new nil guard correctly skipped each Issue write, and control STILL fell through to `stage.Enter(StageApproved)` - `decision=implement` reached approved and on to implementing with no citation, no maintainer comment and no evidence at all. Closing the issue is the strongest veto a human has and it was the thing that released the work. The controller-side twin has refused this since it was written (`ApprovalPassed`, "THE EMPTY SET IS NOT A LICENCE"); this path had no equivalent. Fixed with BOTH halves the review named: out-of-scope Issues are FILTERED out of the scope loop (a filter, not a requirement, so closing ONE issue of a multi-issue Task does not strand the rest) and an empty remainder refuses; and an in-scope Issue that grants with NIL evidence refuses too. `approvalInScope` is now EXPORTED as `controller.ApprovalInScope` and CALLED from restapi rather than restated there - one definition of "a live Issue", the same reason `TaskOwnsIssue` is exported. **The lesson worth keeping is about the TEST, not the code:** `TestOutcome_Clarify_GrantedWithNilEvidenceIsNotWritten` asserted only that the ISSUE write was skipped, which made a half-fix look complete for a whole task - it now also asserts the TASK does not reach `StageApproved`. And `fakeApproval` now mirrors the production seam's out-of-scope arm; a fake that refused where production grants would have certified a gate production does not have.

- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step E.2 review fixes) Doc corrections that were each falsified by an earlier commit in this same sequence, all in the "how does approval work" path a future reader actually consults: the `ApprovalRefused*` const block still said the reasons were "the text the operator's park comment reports back to the human" after that renderer was deleted (they are the metric label and the `action=approval_refused` log field, and **nothing renders them to a human at all** - the refusal parks the Task silently); `internal/controller/labels.go`'s path-(a) note still described "the MOST RECENT maintainer comment CONSISTS OF an approval phrase"; four `stage.Edge.Trigger` strings on the `clarifying` and `conversing` arms and `SyncIssueOnDemand`'s header still named "the C.6 grammar". The `unpark.go:33` mention is deliberately KEPT - it is historical narrative about what the reasoning WAS, not a claim about today. Also added to the clarify prompt: one sentence carrying the skills' strongest anti-pattern (never cite a comment that declines; a scope note is an approval, a condition is not), which had no counterpart while withdrawal got a whole capitalized paragraph.
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step E.2 review round 2) **A scope filter has to be applied at EVERY loop that reads its output, not just the one that builds it.** Adding the out-of-scope filter to `verifyApprovalScope` (round 1) made the evidence map deliberately incomplete - it contains no entry for a closed Issue - but the WRITE loop below it still iterated ALL owned Issues and looked each one up. So the ordinary success case, one closed Issue alongside one live approved one, hit the `ev == nil` arm and logged `approval granted with nil evidence; refusing to write an approver-less approval` at **ERROR**, once per closed Issue, on every approval that WORKED. Nothing was mis-written and nothing advanced wrongly, so the gate was fine - but an ERROR naming a security failure that did not happen is a triage trap and breaks hard rule 12's level discipline. The branch's own comment ("Defensive: granted==true means every issue produced evidence") had silently become false by construction: it means every LIVE issue. Fixed with the same `if !controller.ApprovalInScope(iss) { continue }` at the top of the write loop, which also restores the `ev == nil` arm to being genuinely defensive rather than routinely hit. `TestOutcome_Clarify_ClosedIssueDoesNotBlockALiveOne` now asserts the log too (`v2Opts.logger` + a `bytes.Buffer` JSON handler - there was already precedent in this suite): no `approver-less approval` text and no `"level":"ERROR"` at all on the happy path. Fault-injected - reverting only `outcome.go` reproduces the exact ERROR line. Also made `GrammarVerifier.VerifyApproval`'s doc say where the safety actually lives: its out-of-scope arm is PERMISSIVE, not fail-closed, and the caller-side filter plus the nil check are what make it safe - relying on the arm alone was the hole.
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step F - the cleanup decisions) **There is now exactly ONE place an approval can be granted, because there were two and only one of them ran.** Deleted, all with zero production callers: the Task-level `VerifyApproval`/`VerifyApprovalDetailed` (loop over every owned Issue + write the evidence), `applyApprovalStage` (the `clarifying <-> approved` edge), `ApprovalPassed`, and `ReVerifyParked`/`ReVerifyParkedDetailed` (the un-park re-verify path, callerless since step A). The surviving entry point is `GrammarVerifier.VerifyApproval`, a per-Issue PURE READER; the scope loop, the evidence write and the `stage.Enter(approved)` all belong to `restapi.verifyApprovalScope` + the `submit_outcome` writer, which is also the only place the agent's claim exists. A second gate that nothing calls is not redundancy, it is a spec free to drift from the one that runs - and it had already drifted (it had no scope filter on its own writer's twin, and its "empty set is not a licence" rule existed nowhere else). **CONSEQUENCE, stated not hidden: "approval is NOT sticky" is GONE.** `applyApprovalStage` was the only thing that bounced an `approved` Task back to `clarifying` when it ACQUIRED an Issue after the gate, and it has had no production caller for the whole redesign - so this deletes a behaviour that was already dead, not a live guard. Nothing in production re-runs a Task-level gate after `approved`. **IF IT IS EVER RE-IMPLEMENTED, THE HOME IS `internal/controller/queue_controller.go:610-615`, NOT restapi.** restapi CANNOT host it: `verifyApprovalScope` runs at `submit_outcome` time and the property is about an Issue acquired AFTER that moment - the later Issue does not exist when restapi looks. `queue_controller.go` is the single application of the `approved -> implementing` edge and therefore the instant the approval is actually SPENT; it already holds the Task and a client, so re-asserting "every live owned Issue carries evidence" fits there race-free, and it subsumes shapes the old bounce missed (an Issue REOPENED, evidence cleared) rather than only the acquire case. Owner's call whether to do it at all; this line only records where it goes. **The tests for the deleted twin went with it rather than being retargeted** (the step-5 reviewer's call): a scope/ordering assertion against a function nothing calls certifies nothing. Every clause they covered is still pinned - the per-Issue clauses in `approval_citation_test.go` (`verifyOneIssue`, exhaustive), the scope/empty-set/closed-issue rules in `TestOutcome_Clarify_*` (`internal/restapi`), the on-demand sync in `TestSyncIssueOnDemand`. (CORRECTED 2026-07-29, step G: that test was cited here for a "sync BEFORE verify" ORDERING, and no such ordering exists any more - nothing verifies an approval at un-park time. What the sync is still load-bearing for is putting the triggering comment into the mirror that `verifyApprovalScope` later checks the agent's citation against, at `submit_outcome` time on a live pod. The test's own doc comment was corrected to match.) `approval_grammar_test.go` keeps only what is still live: the auto-approve fail-closed matrix, the seam's two arms, and the refusal-reason distinctness. At this layer a refusal reason is asserted THROUGH `operator_approval_refused_total{reason}`, because the seam returns `(nil,false)` and the label is the only place the reason survives - which pins hard rule 13 in the same assertion.
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step F) **`SyncIssueOnDemand` was RE-WIRED, not deleted, when `ReVerifyParked` went (owner decision D2).** Its caller is now the webhook comment path (`webhook.syncOwnedIssueThread`, scoped by `TaskOwnsIssue`). It is mandatory, not an optimisation: `Issue.Status.Comments` is the ONLY field BOTH halves of the gate read - the agent copies `external_id` out of the turn-0 bundle rendered from it, and the operator re-derives the citation against it with single-use enforced on `Comment.ExternalID`, which a `TaskEvent` does not carry. Nothing else on that path writes it (`mirror_refresh.go` touches Body/Title only) and the full-thread cadence for a parked Task is DAILY, so without the sync the agent has no id to cite and could not be verified if it invented one. Related and already landed in step C: `driveCommentUnpark`'s conversing-room List is gated on `controller.NeedsConversingRoom`, NOT on `== ReasonAwaitingHuman` - `identity-unverified` consumes `ConversingHasRoom` too, and gating on awaiting-human alone leaves the conversing edge structurally inert on the webhook fast path (same class as the MRs-omission bug at MEMORY.md:1051).
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step F) **RESIDUAL RISK, ACCEPTED AND NAMED.** The gate no longer matches text: the agent judges what a maintainer comment MEANS and cites it (`{id, quote}`); the operator re-derives who/which/verbatim against its own mirror. Indirect prompt injection in an issue body or a NON-maintainer comment could steer the agent into citing a genuine maintainer comment whose text actually declines. The structural checks stop a non-maintainer from BEING the approver; they cannot stop a misread, and quote-and-recheck closes FABRICATION, not SELECTIVE QUOTING. A negation-adjacency regex near the quoted span was REJECTED: it resurrects string matching in exactly the form being deleted and false-positives on "no problem, go ahead". A second LLM adjudicator was REJECTED too (JudgeDeceiver, arXiv 2505.13348 - an LLM judge is itself an injection target, so it moves the trust boundary rather than closing it). Blast radius is NOT one unwanted PR: push-CD auto-merges bot PRs on green, tags, publishes, and helmfile applies. See OWASP LLM Top 10 2025 LLM06, NIST AI 100-2e2025, CVE-2025-59041 / GHSA-j4h9-wv2m-wrf7.
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step F - carry-forward items, taken) Four smaller deferrals closed in the same pass. (1) **`isMaintainerComment`'s `Author == botLogin` disjunct had NO independent test** - every bot row in the matrix set `IsBot:true` and short-circuited at the first disjunct, so a live arm of a security check was unpinned. Two rows added with `IsBot:false, Author:"bot-1"` and `bot-1` in `maintainerLogins` (reachable for real: the mirror computes `IsBot` from the project's `botLogin` at mirror time, so a botLogin added or corrected LATER leaves stale unflagged rows), one asserting clause (b) refuses it and one asserting clause (a) does not count it as "a maintainer has spoken". (2) **`ApprovalEvidence`'s single-use doc was softened to match the code**: the guard compares against the LAST STORED evidence, not a history, so after two approve-then-leave-approved cycles the first comment is citable again. Not reachable today; a list of consumed ids is the fix if a path appears, and the doc now says which of the two it is instead of over-claiming. (3) **`o.ok("implement", "issues", ...)` reported ALL owned Issues** including out-of-scope ones the write loop skips; now `len(evidence)`, which is exactly what the outcome approved. (4) **`fakeApproval` did not model `verifyOneIssue`'s already-approved short-circuit**, so `needCitation` tests were STRICTER than production for an Issue already carrying evidence - a fake that refuses where production grants certifies a gate that does not exist. The stale name "C.6 grammar" was renamed to "C.6 citation check" at the five test sites the E.2 pass left behind; the `GrammarVerifier` TYPE keeps its name deliberately (it is the wired `restapi.ApprovalVerifier` seam and renaming it is churn across `cmd/manager/wire.go` and the restapi seam docs for no behaviour change).
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step G - contract bump + doc sweep) `agent.ContractVersion` goes **2 -> 3**, and the same constant is at 3 in `tatara-cli` (`internal/mcp/contract.go`) and `tatara-claude-code-wrapper` (`internal/version/version.go`). The three move in ONE shot on purpose: `submit_outcome(decision=implement)` gained `approvalCitations`, so an old cli refuses to start its MCP server and an old wrapper fails `agent.AssertContractVersion` at pod-ready (`internal/controller/podwatch.go`), BEFORE turn 0 - a loud pre-work failure rather than a silent mid-turn 400. The outage window until the new wrapper image lands is accepted. `TestContractVersionIsThree` (`internal/agent/contract_test.go`) pins the value; the `AssertContractVersion` table shifted with it (matching=3, old wrapper=2, future=4) and `podenv_test.go` now expects `TATARA_CONTRACT_VERSION="3"`. **DOC SWEEP, the non-obvious part:** the A-F sequence renamed the mechanism but 20-odd doc comments outside `approval_grammar.go` still called it "the approval grammar" in the PRESENT tense - which reads as "the operator matches text", the exact claim the redesign falsified. They now say "the C.6 approval citation check", and the two stale clause numbers went with them (`mirror.go` said "clause 3a", `scm/scm.go` "clause 3d"; `verifyOneIssue` documents clauses (a)-(d)). The `GrammarVerifier` TYPE and the `approval_grammar.go` filename keep their names deliberately (step F's call - it is the wired `restapi.ApprovalVerifier` seam and renaming it is churn across `cmd/manager/wire.go` for no behaviour change), so the terminology is deliberately split: the SEAM is Grammar*, the MECHANISM is the citation check. Past-tense narrative naming the wordlist, `grammarPassedFor`, `UnparkInput.GrammarPassed`, `VerifyApprovalDetailed`, `applyApprovalStage` and `ReVerifyParked` was KEPT everywhere - it explains why the code looks the way it does. **ONE BEHAVIOURAL DOC LIE FIXED, not the behaviour:** `stage.go`'s `approved -> clarifying` edge Trigger still read "Approval is not sticky (fix H9)" after step F deleted `applyApprovalStage`, its only production caller. The edge is still LEGAL and is left legal; the Trigger now says LEGAL BUT UNDRIVEN and points at `queue_controller.go` as the home if it is ever re-implemented (owner's decision, recorded in the step F entry above). Nothing was implemented for it here.
- 2026-07-29 (agent-judged approval gate, step G review fixes) Four findings from the whole-branch review, all observability / test-attribution / doc integrity, none a correctness or security defect. **(I1) TWO REACHABLE REFUSAL PATHS EMITTED NO TELEMETRY.** `verifyApprovalScope`'s `len(issues)==0` and `len(out)==0` arms return before `VerifyApproval` is ever called, and the reason constant, the `operator_approval_refused_total{reason}` increment and the `action=approval_refused` INFO all live INSIDE that call - so a human closing the only Issue of a clarify Task, the strongest veto they have and the exact hole fix L3-14 closed, refused with NO attribution. New sixth reason `ApprovalRefusedNoLiveIssue = "no-live-issue"`, the ONLY one emitted outside `verifyOneIssue` (it is a property of the SCOPE, so no single Issue can be blamed for it). `verifyApprovalScope` gained a third return, the refusal reason, EMPTY on a grant AND on the per-Issue path - re-reporting the per-Issue refusal there would split one refusal across two series. The refusal WARN said "the citation did not verify on every owned issue" with `issues=len(issues)`: the wrong CAUSE for this shape (no citation was evaluated) and the wrong NUMBER (it counted the very Issues the refusal was about excluding), which sent triage at the clarify agent instead of at the human. It now carries `reason` plus `owned_issues` and `live_issues`. The park itself was always alerted - `alerts/tatara-operator.yaml` matches `stageReason!~"backlog-sweep|awaiting-human"`, a NEGATIVE match, so identity-unverified is covered - so this was degraded attribution, never a blind spot. `s.approval == nil` is deliberately left with NO reason: `cmd/manager/wire.go` always sets it, so it is not production-reachable and giving it `no-live-issue` would be a lie about the Task's Issues. **(I2) `mirror_refresh.go` asserted a safety property that does not hold, and step G's own sweep edited that line and preserved it** - a term rename, not a claim audit. "identity-unverified needs a recorded approval, not an edit, so it stays parked" is FALSE at HEAD: `handleIssueEdited` early-returns for bot actors, so any surviving event is human; `hasNonBotEvent` (`stage.go`) tests `Author != botLogin` and does NOT filter on Kind; and the identity-unverified arm's only remaining question is `ConversingHasRoom`. **A human editing an issue body un-parks the Task to conversing.** Benign, because re-entry there is a RESPONSIVENESS decision and never an authorization one, but the comment is the one place in the webhook package telling a maintainer which event classes can move a Task parked in front of "write code and merge to prod". Same class swept at `mirror_test.go` (`TestSyncIssueOnDemand` claimed a sync-BEFORE-verify ORDERING that no longer exists - the sync's surviving purpose is to put the cited comment in the mirror `verifyApprovalScope` checks against LATER), `agent_comment_test.go` (a row name crediting the C.6 check for a refusal that is really "a bot comment is not a non-bot pendingEvent"), `unpark.go`, and two corrections inline above. **(I3) THE COVERAGE THIS BRANCH RECORDED AS TAKEN WAS NOT TAKEN.** `isMaintainerComment`'s `botLogin != "" && c.Author == botLogin` disjunct had nothing pinning it: at the one production call site `botLogin` IS `proj.Spec.Scm.BotLogin`, making it byte-equivalent to `IsMaintainer`'s own test, and `EffectiveMaintainerLogins` runs `withoutBotLogin` first anyway. VERIFIED BY MUTATION: deleting the disjunct AND `logins.go:87-89` leaves the full suite green; only deleting `withoutBotLogin`'s strip goes red. **The reviewer's suggested fix does not work** - a per-Repository `MaintainerLogins` override does NOT escape `withoutBotLogin`, which strips the bot from the repo override too. Kept the disjunct (deleting a redundant layer on the WHO check that releases code to prod is the wrong trade) and pinned it at the boundary where it is still a real parameter: `TestVerifyOneIssue_BotLoginDisjunctIsTheLastLayer` passes `verifyOneIssue` a `botLogin` the other two layers cannot see, so the disjunct is the only thing that can refuse. Fault-injection verified. The comment now names `withoutBotLogin` as the operative layer and this disjunct as defence in depth. **(I4) A GUARD THAT NEVER RUNS, AND A TEST CREDITING IT.** `outcome.go`'s `ev == nil` arm in the write loop is unreachable - `verifyApprovalScope` refuses on `!ok || ev == nil` and the loop walks exactly its map's keys - and deleting it leaves the package green. KEPT, reframed as a DRIFT DETECTOR: it is reachable again the moment the write loop's scope skip drifts from the identical one in `verifyApprovalScope`, a one-line regression that HAS already happened once. **The reviewer's knock-on claim was WRONG and I verified it rather than repeating it:** `outcome_test.go`'s `NotContains "approver-less approval"` is NOT a vacuous absence assertion - deleting the write-loop scope skip makes it RED (mutation-verified), so it is exactly the negative pin the guard needs. Only what the test docs CREDIT was corrected. **PROCESS, pre-existing and it will bite anyone validating this branch:** a bare `mise exec -- go test ./...` PANICS in TestMain for `internal/controller`, `internal/own` and `internal/webhook` on a missing `/usr/local/kubebuilder/bin/etcd`, so ZERO controller tests run. Only `make test` sets `KUBEBUILDER_ASSETS` (via `setup-envtest`). Filter that output and a false RED reads as a false GREEN. Always `mise run test`, or export `KUBEBUILDER_ASSETS` by hand for a targeted run.
- 2026-07-29 (issue #495, and the whole defect class behind it) A `+kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength` marker is a COMMENT: no Go code can read it, so a bounded status field is only bounded if every writer independently remembers the number. `TaskEvent.Body` (4096) had four construction sites and none clamped, so any forge comment over 4 KB made the API server reject the entire Task status update - `status.pendingEvents[0].body: Too long`. That was not log noise: the rejected write was the `mr_comment` redelivery to a review Task parked `awaiting-human`, and `redeliverMRComments` returns BEFORE advancing `newest`, so `LastMirroredCommentID` never moved past the oversized comment and every LATER comment on that MR was blocked behind it on every 4-hourly sweep, forever. Three Tasks were live in that state for up to four days. Fixes: (a) the clamp lives in `AppendTaskEvent`/`AppendAgentTaskEvent`, the one funnel every TaskEvent passes through, not at the four call sites - a fifth call site cannot reintroduce it; (b) `api/v1alpha1/limits.go` holds ONE exported constant per capped body plus `TruncateUTF8`, replacing three divergent private copies of 4096 (`restapi.noteBodyMaxBytes`, `agent.maxNoteBody`, `controller.commentBodyLimit`), and `TestCRDMaxLengthMatchesConstants` reads the GENERATED CRDs so a constant can never drift from its marker again; (c) an audit of every MaxLength-bounded field fed by external text found NINE more unclamped writers, all now clamped - the worst was `appendOperatorNote` (`reviewpost.go`), whose only caller feeds it `reviewBeltNote`, a concatenation of up to 30 findings each permitted 8192 bytes, against a 4096-byte `Note.Body`; that belt note is the sole handoff from a request_changes review to the next implement pod, and it was unwritable on any non-trivial review. Also fixed: `agent.truncateNoteBody` cut with a bare byte slice and could itself emit invalid UTF-8; `QueuedEventPayload.Goal` carried no marker at all while being copied verbatim into a 16384-capped `TaskSpec.Goal`, so an over-long incident goal was accepted at enqueue and only rejected two hops later at admission. Truncation is marked, not silent - a clamped TaskEvent body ends with a pointer to the mirrored thread, which still holds the text up to its own 8192 cap, so nothing is actually lost. NOT done here: `fail()` still has no permanent-vs-transient classification, so a genuinely impossible sweep item is still retried every pass; that is #477's fix target 2 and stays there.
- 2026-07-29 (#497 x #492 merge) **The Note.Body clamp lives in `appendOperatorNoteTo`, not in `appendOperatorNote`.** #492 split the append into a driver-free form so the red-CI gate could reach it from a bare client, which created a SECOND way to write a Note; #497's clamp sat on the wrapper and would have covered only the review belt, leaving the ci-red bounce note unclamped - the exact defect class #497 exists to close. It is also the first statement in the driver-free form, ahead of the `n.Body == body` idempotency scan, because that scan compares the STORED (already truncated) body: clamp after it and every over-long note compares unequal to its own stored copy and is re-appended on every reconcile.
- 2026-07-29 (instrumentation, same issue) `obs.sweepSeedReasons` carried a "keep in sync with sweep.go's fail(reason, ...) call sites" comment and had drifted from FOUR of them, including `reconcile_ownership`. An unseeded reason has no series at all until its first error, and a counter born AT its first error has no earlier sample to increase from, so `increase(operator_sweep_errors_total{reason=...}[1h])` is blind to exactly the first failure after every pod roll - which, at one error per 4-hourly sweep, is most of them. Confirmed live: every seeded reason showed 3 series (one per Project) at zero, `reconcile_ownership` showed 1 (born at its error) and the other three showed none, and `increase(...[2h])` read 0 while Loki counted the ERROR lines. That is why this class had to be alerted on from Loki log lines instead of the counter that exists for it. The prose instruction is now `TestSweepSeedReasonsCoverEveryFailSite`, which scans `fail("...")` out of the source and fails both ways (unseeded producer, and seeded reason with no producer left).
- 2026-07-29 (issue #466 scope correction) Part (a) of #466 (duplicate concurrent ingest Jobs) was already fixed by #468/e114718 (deterministic `ingest.JobName` + Create/AlreadyExists adopt guard) - verified as an ancestor of `main` before writing any code. Part (b) (400ms retry budget vs the server's `Retry-After: 5`) as WRITTEN in #466 cites `tatara-memory-repo-ingester`'s `internal/push/push.go` - a different repo/module entirely, not reachable from a `tatara-operator` worktree and not a tatara-operator dependency (confirmed: no `push.go`, no `tatara-memory-repo-ingester` reference anywhere in this module). tatara-operator DOES carry its own analogous defect though: `internal/memclient/memclient.go`'s `do` retried 5xx/429 on a fixed `{200ms, 1s}` schedule and never read the response's `Retry-After` header at all. Fixed there instead, since that is the only client-side retry loop against tatara-memory that actually exists in this repo. The exact `tatara-memory-repo-ingester` fix from the issue text is still open and belongs to that repo, not this one.
- 2026-07-29 (issue #480) `stage.Enter` re-stamped `StageEnteredAt` on EVERY transition including the merge-timeout/deploy-timeout un-park re-entry, so `MergeReentries`/`DeployReentries` bounded the LOOP (3 laps) but not the COST per lap - each lap bought a fresh 4h/2h budget, turning a capped 3-retry into ~16h of re-testing a deterministically red CI (live-confirmed: `stageEnteredAt` read the UN-PARK instant, not the original entry, and `operator_task_stage_age_seconds` under-reported true residency by up to a full budget per re-entry). Fixed with a new `Status.StageElapsedCarrySeconds`, written in exactly two places inside `Enter` itself (never by a caller, never keying off `ParkedFromStage` - which stays observability-only per its own doc comment): accumulated into the carry the instant a podless stage's budget elapses into parked(merge-timeout|deploy-timeout), before `StageEnteredAt` is overwritten, and preserved (not reset to zero like every other edge) on the matching un-park re-entry. `ArmedClock`'s podless WORK clock subtracts the carry from `since` [superseded 2026-07-30 by #513: it no longer does - a timeout re-entry gets a fresh `TimeoutReentryBudget` window from `StageEnteredAt` instead]; the new `stage.StageElapsedSeconds` helper does the same addition for `operator_task_stage_age_seconds` (`project_controller.go`) and `operator_merge_cursor_stalled_seconds` (`merge.go`), so neither gauge sawtooths across a re-entry anymore. DELIBERATE CONSEQUENCE, not a follow-on: with the carry fully preserved (no separate small re-entry budget), the re-entered clock reads AT OR PAST budget the instant it re-enters, so the very next reconcile pass re-parks a still-red MR almost immediately instead of waiting out another full budget - this collapses the whole capped cycle from ~16h to roughly one budget plus a few reconcile ticks, which is why the issue's "Decision 2" (gate the re-entry on changed state) and "Decision 1 option (c)" (a separate small re-entry budget) both turned out to be unnecessary once (a) was implemented fully [superseded 2026-07-30 by #513: option (c) was NOT unnecessary - it is exactly what #513 had to implement. The "DELIBERATE CONSEQUENCE" above is the bug: a timeout park happens only once the budget is already spent, so "re-parks a still-red MR almost immediately" was in fact "re-parks on the SAME reconcile pass, in milliseconds, buying zero merge/deploy time"]. `TestMergeTimeoutCarryDoesNotLeakIntoAFreshMergingEntry` guards the one way this could regress: a HEAD-MOVED merging exit (fix M3-9) or any other non-timeout edge must still reset the carry to zero, or a stale carry would silently shrink an unrelated later attempt's real budget.
- 2026-07-29 (#471) **`internal/refine/goal.go` was the ONE remaining phantom-tool-name producer, not all nine claimed in the issue.** `internal/promptguidance/toolnames.go` (from #474/7467d83) had already fixed and pinned `projectscan.go`'s brainstorm goal and `controller`/`incident`'s goal builders - `TestGoalBuildersNameOnlyRealTools` and `TestIncidentGoalsNameOnlyRealTools` were already green against real names before this change, and grepping `projectscan.go` for the phantom names (`propose_issue`, `list_handoffs`, `code_related`, etc.) found zero hits. `internal/refine/goal_toolnames_test.go`'s own doc comment already named the gap explicitly as "tracked separately." Fixed `GoalProject` to name `scm_read`(kind=issues|commits), `task_list`, and `issue_write`(action=edit), backticked so `promptguidance.UnknownToolNames` actually sees them (the check only sees backticked spans - that is why the old unbackticked `list_issues`/`close_issue`/etc. passed this exact guard vacuously for months). **Non-obvious part:** the old goal described refine closing issues via a `close_issue` tool call per issue; the REAL contract, per tatara-cli's `refineOutcomeSchema` (`internal/mcp/outcome.go`) and its handler (`internal/restapi/outcome.go`'s `refine()`), is that `submit_outcome` batches every closure into a `closes[]` array (live-revalidated against the forge immediately before each close) alongside `links[]` (cross-references) and `folds[]` (duplicate-Task consolidation, a feature `#363` added AFTER this goal was last touched at the `#318` task-centric redesign and that the goal never taught at all) - at least one of the three must be non-empty, and that IS refine's only terminal call. Rewrote the Termination section to that real shape rather than a 1:1 tool-name swap; deliberately did NOT invent detailed fold-selection policy beyond "a duplicate Task with no work in flight", since that is new-feature guidance, not a name-drift fix, and deserves its own decision. Updated `goal_test.go` and `goal_toolnames_test.go` to assert the real names/shape instead of the stale ones.
- 2026-07-29 (#439, #438) A transient dependency outage amplified into 58 ERROR lines because the amplifier is `Owns()`, not the retry rate limiter. `applyMemoryStack` returned every apply error as a hard reconcile error via `failMemory` (phase=Failed), and the Project controller `Owns()` all eight kinds in the stack - so during the 2026-07-24 rolling node reboot the stack's own objects churned, each churn drove an immediate `Add()` that bypassed the workqueue's exponential failure delay, and each pass re-hit the unreachable `mcluster.cnpg.io` webhook for one more controller-runtime "Reconciler error" line. Backing off the requeue would NOT have fixed it; only not returning an error does. `isTransientApplyError` (`internal/controller/project_memory_apply.go`) now soft-requeues a retryable apply failure exactly as the health-read path already absorbed an API blip. The classifier is narrow ON PURPOSE: an admission webhook that cannot be REACHED is `InternalError` + a message naming the webhook, an admission webhook that DENIES is Forbidden/Invalid and stays a first-pass hard failure - conflating them is how a broken Project spec (the #248 cnpg storage shrink) would become invisible. The grace before escalating back to phase=Failed is WALL TIME from the first failure, never a pass count, for the same `Owns()` reason: a pass counter escalates a 30s blip during a reboot storm and sits patiently through a genuinely dead webhook on an idle cluster, exactly backwards. Second-order win, and the reason this also serves #438: phase=Failed is what the critical `operator_memory_stacks{phase=~"Provisioning|Failed"}` alert reads, so the old behaviour let a 6-minute webhook outage feed the project-halting page.
- 2026-07-29 (#438) The circular self-heal dependency is now empirically confirmed, and it is NOT fixable in this repo. The observability plane (Prometheus/Grafana/Loki/Alertmanager) rebooted in the same wave as the workload it monitors, so the incident agent dispatched to investigate was BLOCKED on Grafana being unreachable (operator log 18:47:46Z, `action=agent_internal_issue`) and Prometheus had lost the TSDB history for the escalated window - the alert's own annotation warns about exactly this and was proven right. The operator cannot break the cycle: it owns neither the monitoring stack's scheduling nor its retention. Belongs to `tatara-helmfile`/`tatara-observability` as anti-affinity + topology spread for the monitoring plane (same gap class as closed #365 for the memory stack). Recorded rather than fixed, so the next incident agent that hits a blank Grafana knows it is a known architectural hole and not a new fault. Related: the "whole project stalls, INCLUDING the incident agent" half of that annotation is now STALE - #470 (2026-07-26) removed the memory hard-gate and agents run degraded, so the annotation text and arguably the critical severity need re-tuning in `tatara-observability`.
- 2026-07-28 (#482) GitHub review posts sent `subject_type: "file"` INSIDE the atomic create-review `comments[]`. That field exists only on `POST /pulls/{n}/comments`; on a `DraftPullRequestReviewComment` it 422s the WHOLE review, and `classifyReviewPostError` makes a 422 terminal, so one line-less finding parked the Task and discarded a completed review round (14 parked at `review-post-refused` when the alert fired). Introduced by `b3f2c6e`, which fixed the line=0 422 by inventing this one and gave the unanchorable degrade path to GitLab only. Fix mirrors GitLab: `GitHub.PostReview` now reads `GET /pulls/{n}/files` ONCE (paginated), builds the hunk index and pre-checks every finding. The degrade TARGET differs by forge and that is deliberate - GitLab degrades to a plain MR note (it already posts N+1 calls), GitHub folds the finding into the review BODY, because the body is the only unanchored part of the atomic post and adding a second call would break the round marker's "everything landed" meaning and introduce a partial-success state. `glHunkIndex`/`glHunkHeaderRE`/the range-building loop moved to a shared `hunkIndex` in `review.go` (`addPatch`) rather than being copied. A file GitHub returns with NO `patch` (binary/too large) has no anchorable line, so every finding on it degrades - safe, and the alternative (guessing) is what 422s. The 422 stays TERMINAL and there is no degrade-and-retry: two providers reasoning the same way beats a second, weaker classification path.
- 2026-07-29 (issue #467, fold GC liveness) The B.6 fold skip list keyed on "the marker is set", with no clock and no check on the umbrella - so a refine umbrella stopped mid-adoption pinned its 26 members off the reaper with nothing anywhere able to release them (an operator rollout does not help; the marker is in etcd). The gate is now `v1alpha1.FoldInFlightActive`: `TaskDone(umbrella)` false AND within `FoldInFlightTTL` (1h) of `FoldInFlightSince`, a NEW status field stamped by `foldMembers` step 1 and cleared everywhere the marker is. TTL, not just the terminal check, because a live umbrella can lose the request between steps; terminal check, not just the TTL, because parked is never aged out at all so its marker outlives every retention the reaper has. The anchor falls back to `StageEnteredAt` when absent, which is what releases the already-stranded CRs on the first pass after rollout with no manual surgery. `releaseTerminal` also clears the marker, ABOVE its `AnnTerminalReleased` short circuit and above every fallible forge write, for the reason the wrapper teardown leads `ApplyIssueClosedStop`: a tombstone that blocks OTHER Tasks' GC must not wait on an SCM 403. `errFoldUnverified` now clears it too - the step-3 doc comment claimed "foldInFlight cleared" and the code cleared nothing, which was a 7-day (`FailedRetention`) block on members it had deliberately NOT deleted.
- 2026-07-29 (issue #467, self-termination) The originating bug is that `refine`'s closes[] gate only refused a close target owned by ANOTHER Task; closing one THIS Task owns was allowed, and that is precisely what fires the WS3-I3 stop edge on the closer (refining is one of the ten `issueClosedStopStages`). Fixed at both ends because the two orderings are different bugs: `refine` now 409s `close-target-self-mid-fold` when closes[] names an owned issue and folds[] is non-empty (rejected in the read-only block - after `foldMembers` nothing is recoverable, its step 4 has deleted the members), and `ApplyIssueClosedStop` DEFERS while `FoldInFlightActive`, which covers the async ordering where the close drains and webhooks back mid-request. The defer is safe only because the same TTL bounds it; without the liveness gate above it would be a new permanent stall.
- 2026-07-29 Metric hygiene for `fold_in_flight` mirrors `docHoldStuck` (#424): the counter is incremented only past `FoldInFlightGrace` (5m), since a fold is one HTTP request and counting the healthy window re-counted one event per pass per member and tripped the warning alert on every ordinary fold. New `operator_fold_in_flight_blocked_tasks{project}` gauge is the DISTINCT count the alert should key on (rule `ffrz32fcqvta8d` in tatara-observability still says `sum by (reason) (increase(operator_gc_blocked_total[1h]))`, which also sums across pod replicas - that rule change is a separate repo and was NOT made here). `operator_fold_stranded_released_total{reason}` counts the releases, because otherwise the fix is silent: members simply start being collected and nobody learns an adoption was lost.
- 2026-07-30 (issue #513, correcting #480's "DELIBERATE CONSEQUENCE" above) A timeout re-entry's DEADLINE is now decoupled from its cumulative REPORTED residency: `ArmedClock`'s podless branch stopped subtracting `StageElapsedCarrySeconds` and arms a fresh `v1alpha1.TimeoutReentryBudget` (30m) window from `StageEnteredAt`, while `StageElapsedSeconds` (and every metric reading it) stays cumulative - a carry-adjusted deadline is self-defeating because a timeout park only happens once the budget is ALREADY spent, so every re-entry arrived over budget and re-parked on the same pass (live: laps of 7.2ms/8.8ms/11.3ms, terminal `failed` 107s after the first timeout, all 3 `MaxDeployReentries` spent buying zero deploy time; ONE timeout cycle is now bounded at base + 3x30m - 3h30m deploying, 5h30m merging - still far under the ~16h #480 killed. That is PER CYCLE, not a stage total: a HEAD-MOVED (`HeadMoved`) or ci-red (`CIRed`) exit from merging leaves via reviewing/implementing, where `Enter` zeroes the carry, so the next merging entry opens a new cycle with a fresh full 4h base - up to ~17h30m across all `MaxHeadMoveReentries` bounces. NOT a regression: `1a9f676` laundered the carry on the identical edges, and those bounces are bounded by their own counters). The discriminator is `carry > 0` while IN merging/deploying, NOT `MergeReentries`/`DeployReentries`: `Enter` zeroes the carry on every non-timeout edge (HEAD-MOVED merging exit, `enterFreshImplementing`) so it is exactly "this is a timeout re-entry", whereas the counters stay set across an unrelated later cycle and would hand a genuinely fresh entry the 30m window; the stage half of the condition is load-bearing too - the carry is already live while the Task is still PARKED, whose budget is the 7-day `ParkRetention` reap clock, so a bare `carry > 0` would have cut an un-un-parkable parked Task's retention to 30m (`TestParkedWithACarryKeepsParkRetention`). `Enter` itself is unchanged. Also, observability: `deploying` was not wholly silent - `resolveDeployed` logs `deploy_unsupervised`/`deploy_not_pinned`/`deploy_await_tag`/`deploy_poll` - but its THREE most likely waits returned a bare `false` with no line at all: no green tatara-helmfile apply run, a green apply predating the merge, and an applied pin still behind our tag. The FIRST is #513's own wedge (the ARC runner was out of disk, so `apply.yaml` never went green), which is why 2h07m produced zero evidence and `parked(deploy-timeout)` was the first anyone heard of it. `resolveDeployed` now returns a per-MR sub-reason string alongside its bool, and `ReconcileDeploying` logs `deploy_waiting` on both requeue returns with `pending`/`pending_mrs` (each pending MR NAMED with its own sub-reason)/carry-adjusted `stalled_seconds`, mirroring `stallMerge`'s `merge_waiting`. A bare count would not have distinguished the four.

- 2026-07-30 (pod-name stamp gap) #517's `<type>-<project>-<repo>-<i|p><id>` pod name landed on the QUEUE path only: `agent.StampPodName` had exactly one call site (`internal/queue/enqueue.go`), so every DIRECT Task create fell back to the legacy `wrapper-<task-name>` - the intake mints (`MintIssueTask`, `MintReviewTask`), the nightly `MintDocBatch`, `MintOrUnparkTakeoverTask`, and the brainstorm-proposal `mintClarifyTask` in `internal/restapi/outcome.go`. Live proof: `incident` Tasks carried the annotation (`inc-tatara-529b4f14f695838f`) while all six `review` Tasks minted the same day had `<NONE>` and pods named `wrapper-mt-r-tatara-chat-59-...`, both on the same running v1.42.0 image. All five direct mints now stamp with the Repository CR name as repoRef (`docsRepo.Name` for the doc batch, matching the queue path's `PodRepo` for a documentation event), so a repo names the same pod segment whichever path minted it. Two properties this depends on and that are now tested: `createTaskRaceSafe` is ADOPT-OR-CREATE and never writes the stamped literal on the adopt path, so an adopted pre-#517 twin keeps NO annotation and keeps working through `PodName`'s fallback; and the annotation is written once at creation, so the pod name does not follow `status.agentKind` as the Task advances.

- 2026-08-07 (#521, MR1) **CORRECTED 2026-08-07 after the v1.43.0 deploy - see the correction entry at the top of this file. The causal claim originally made here was WRONG.** `IsOrphanIssue` clause (b) called `own.ControllerOwner(cr)`, which returns `owned=true` for ANY ownerRef carrying `controller=true` and never checks the named Task exists, so an Issue CR whose owning Task was reaped would keep a dangling controller ownerRef forever and the sweep would skip it silently on every pass. That defect is REAL and the fix below is right, but it was NOT what stranded `iss-tatara-operator-510/512/520/523/524`: those five Tasks all exist and are legitimately `parked(backlog-sweep)`, and `operator_sweep_stale_owner_repaired_total` measured ZERO across the first full sweep pass after rollout. Read the rest of this entry as defensive hardening, not as a diagnosis. The fix is not "also check the Task" inside the predicate: the predicate now takes a RESOLVED `liveOwner string` and `Minter.resolveLiveOwner` fills it in, which makes "resolve liveness first" structural rather than a convention the next caller forgets - the same reason `stage.UnparkDetailed` exists. THE MR ARM CARRIED THE SAME DEFECT AND IS FIXED IN THE SAME CHANGE, not left as a follow-up: the reaper releases Issue and MergeRequest ownership symmetrically (one `releaseOwnership` over `own.OldestSurvivingOwner` for both), so `ClassifyPR` reading `own.ControllerOwner(cr)` in clause 1b AND clause 3 meant an MR mirror whose controller-owning Task was reaped kept a dangling ref, an open HUMAN PR classified `PRIgnore` forever with no mint/counter/log, and an adoptable bot PR was held `PRClaimed` by a claimant that no longer existed. `ClassifyPR` now takes `liveOwner string` instead of the `cr` it only ever read ownership off, and `EnsureTaskForMRComment` (which returned the dead owner's name straight to the webhook) and `redeliverMRComments` (which then delivered every replayed comment to it) both resolve through the SAME `resolveLiveOwner`, which is why that function takes a `client.Object`: two arms of one defect get one resolver, and a parallel copy is how they drift. The two typed fronts (`resolveLiveIssueOwner`/`resolveLiveMROwner`) exist only because a typed nil pointer boxed in a `client.Object` is not `== nil`. The stale ref is DROPPED, not ignored, because the identical ref also misroutes `ownerTaskRequests` (stage_controller.go), the reaper cascade and `ourMR`; ignoring it would have fixed one of four consumers. The liveness Get is UNCACHED for the same reason `createTaskRaceSafe`'s is (fix F3-1): a stale informer that has not observed a freshly created Task would make this drop a LIVE owner's ref and steal an issue out from under a running Task, which is strictly worse than the bug. `own.DropOwner` is the pure mutation half and stays in `internal/own`; the Get does NOT, because that package is memory-only by its own package doc. Also replaced the intake funnel's `created bool` with a typed `MintOutcome` - it collapsed FOUR states into one bit, which is how `resume.go` logged "re-minted the issue fresh" for a return where a Task had just been DESTROYED and nothing replaced it. `EnsureTaskForMRComment` KEEPS its `(owner string, minted bool, err)` signature and turns `MintTombstoneDeleted` into an error instead: its `("", false, nil)` already means "accepted-ignored", so a third meaning would be the same collapse again, and both its callers (`webhook/server.go`, `ownership_redeliver.go`) already handle an error correctly (5xx-and-redeliver, and a requeue). `MintTombstoneDeleted` now requeues the sweep at 30s (`sweepRemintDelay`) rather than a full hourly pass; 30s and not 0 because the deleted tombstone's owned mirrors cascade in the BACKGROUND and an instant re-mint could bind a fresh Task to a mirror the GC is still collecting (that same rationale is why the owed mint is NOT re-driven inline in the same pass). THE REQUEUE ALONE WAS A NO-OP and the fix is two-part: `stampScan` writes `Status.LastIssueScan`, which IS `reposDueForScan`'s `dueBase`, so a pass that stamped left the owed repo not-due for a full cron period and the 30s wake found `len(dueRepos) == 0` - the silent-next-pass shape wearing a 30s hat. `runScans` therefore SKIPS the stamp while a tombstone requeue is pending, which leaves exactly the repos that were due still due. Bounded because `createTaskRaceSafe` deletes each tombstone once and a Task carries no finalizer, so the name is free immediately and the next pass mints AND stamps. Rejected alternatives: an in-memory per-repo "force due" marker (new shared mutable state on the reconciler, a mutex, and a leak per deleted Project, to re-sweep one repo instead of the handful that were already due), and an inline second `MintIssueTask` (contradicted by the background-cascade rationale above). `MintOrUnparkTakeoverTask`'s tombstone retry, previously unbounded self-recursion, is now bounded at one via an unexported `mintOrUnparkTakeoverTask(..., retried bool, ...)`. Observability half: the only sweep alert was a LIVENESS heartbeat, which structurally cannot catch this class (the sweep WAS running, correctly, doing nothing), so `operator_sweep_orphan_stranded_seconds{project,repo,number}` measures the WORK, cleared per `(project, repo)` on every pass because `SweepProject` is called with `dueRepos` and a project-wide clear would wipe repos this pass never looked at. It is set on EVERY unserved orphan, including the per-item ERROR exits - setting it only on the budget-bound and tombstone paths made the deadman read zero exactly when the sweep was failing, the literal negation of its own Help text. Because the per-pass clear only ever runs from INSIDE a pass, `obs.RetainSweepOrphanStranded(project, keep)` retracts the four transitions no pass can reach: sweep disabled, `issueScan` cron emptied/unparseable, `Repository` unenrolled (`runScans`), and `Project` deleted (`Reconcile`, next to the existing `SweepNextExpectedTimestamp` retract). It enumerates via `Collect` because `DeletePartialMatch` can only say "delete these labels", never "keep only these", and the repos that leak are by definition the ones the caller no longer has a name for. `operator_sweep_skipped_total` and `operator_sweep_stale_owner_repaired_total` gained `activity="webhook"` producers (`Minter.Activity`): a mint driven by a live forge delivery is not a sweep pass, and labelling it `sweep` made the stale-owner counter's own triage instruction wrong for that half. `operator_intake_mint_outcome_total` moved into `createTaskRaceSafe` (the natural-key arbiter, so the takeover path is counted too) plus `mintErr`/the classify declines, so it no longer counts only the good news. STILL OPEN at the time of writing: issues 502/503/505/521/525 carry ZERO ownerRefs, so clause (b) passed for them; their skip branch is not determinable from outside - the new `action=sweep_skip_issue` line is what states it on the first pass after rollout, and the MR description deliberately asserts no cause. **It stated it, and the answer was the per-pass mint budget, which this entry originally recorded as "ruled out from the live cluster". It was not ruled out; it was the cause. See the correction entry at the top.**

- 2026-08-08 (#547) **`handoffCondition` armed on the outcome that CAUSED the transition, and every approved implement Task since v2.0.1 parked `handoff-stalled` 5 minutes later.** The predicate's doc comment claimed it "resolves to exactly one case: awaiting-review after a review outcome commits", and justified that with "every other kind's commit calls `stage.Enter` in the SAME write, so its condition Reason can never name the NEW state's agent kind". That inference held only while every state had its own agent kind. #521 folded clarify into implement and #539 rewrote the table as `AgentKindFor(state, specKind)`, which returns `originAgentKinds[specKind]` for BOTH `refined` and `under-implementation` - so the `OutcomeAccepted{Reason=Implement}` that `/outcome` writes on the approval edge names the agent of the state it lands in, and `OutcomeCommittedFor` matched. The occupancy guard that should have caught it compared `cond.LastTransitionTime.Before(StateEnteredAt)`, and both stamps come from that ONE etcd write: `metav1.Time` is SECOND-granular, so they are EQUAL, and a strict `Before` is false. Result: B2 suppressed the implement pod for the whole 5m `HandoffDeadline` (`action=pod_stage_suppressed_committed_outcome`, once per 30s reconcile) and then parked, `UnparkHuman` - so an approved Task needed a SECOND human comment to do the work the first one had just authorised. Live: 2 of 2 approvals in the 24h after v2.0.1 hit it (`mt-i-mtg-decks-18-...`, `mt-i-tatara-operator-529-...`), zero code written on either. The fix is TWO gates, not one, because they fail independently. GATE 1 is structural and stamp-independent: `state != awaiting-review` returns nil, awaiting-review being the only state that defers its advance to a second reconciler (`MergeRequestReconciler -> DrainPendingReview -> advanceAfterReview`) and therefore the only one that can hold an outstanding handoff at all. This asserts the invariant the doc comment always ASSUMED, so a future `AgentKindFor` change cannot silently re-break it - the same "make it structural rather than a convention the next caller forgets" reason `resolveLiveOwner` exists (#521 MR1). GATE 2 tightens the occupancy check from `Before` to `!After`: an outcome can only be this occupancy's handoff if it committed strictly LATER than the entry, which a genuine review commit always is (the review pod has to be scheduled, booted and run in between). Not redundant with gate 1 - it is what covers a same-second re-entry into awaiting-review itself. TWO EXISTING FIXTURES MODELLED THE IMPOSSIBLE SHAPE and had to be corrected rather than worked around: `tsReviewTaskWithOutcome` and `TestReviewTask_CommittedOutcomePlusLostPodReachesAwaitingHuman` both stamped `StateEnteredAt` and the commit at the same instant, i.e. they described an outcome written in the same write as the entry it caused, which is precisely the thing that is NOT a handoff. Both now enter awaiting-review before the commit. That they needed changing is the point: the old fixtures could not have caught this bug because they encoded it.

- 2026-08-08 (stalled-turn teardown) **A stalled turn was the ONE pod teardown that skipped G.7 entirely, and it destroyed uncommitted work.** `PollOnce` called `expireTimedOutTurn`, which did `DeleteSession` + delete Pod + delete Service outright: no `waitIdle`, no handoff turn, no handoff note, synthetic or otherwise. So a Task whose FIRST turn stalled was handed back to the stage machine with an EMPTY notes journal - the next pod started from nothing, redid the work, and burned `maxTurnsPerTask` - and anything the agent had written but not pushed died with the workspace. Live: `mt-i-mtg-decks-18` lost a `decklist.txt`, a meta capture and an hour of sim runs to this twice (`podRuns` reached 3) before the wrapper's frozen-anchor bug (see the tatara-claude-code-wrapper entry) was found to be what declared those healthy turns stalled in the first place. Now routed through the SAME `TTLStopper.StopWithHandoff` as a TTL rotation, via `stalledTurnStop`. THREE things made that non-trivial. (1) THE BOUND. The G.7 hard cap is `Deadline + 2*TurnTimeout + TTLGrace`, which is right when Deadline is a pod's t0 and absurd here: this path only runs BECAUSE a turn already burned its whole `turnTimeoutSeconds` plus `turnTimeoutGrace` with no activity, so re-deriving from `TurnTimeout` means waiting hours for the thing we just declared dead - and a hung wrapper never goes idle, so it is the FULL wait every time, not a worst case. New `TTLStopInput.MaxWait` REPLACES both the hard cap and the per-step wait when non-zero; zero keeps the TTL arithmetic byte-for-byte. `StalledTurnHandoffWait` is 90s, sized for the only thing still worth waiting for: the seconds-wide race where the turn completes between the stall check and the stop. (2) WHERE IT RUNS. NOT in `PollOnce`: `StopWithHandoff` blocks on real timers and `PollOnce` is a single pass over every Task in the namespace on one 30s ticker, so blocking it per stalled Task would delay result-recording for every OTHER live turn in the cluster. It runs from `TaskReconciler.reconcilePodStage`, immediately after the `TTLExpired` check, which is per-Task and already owns the identical stop. `PollOnce` keeps the DETECTION (the `turn_timeout` metric and log) and does nothing else; the reconciler re-evaluates the same pure `turnTimedOut` predicate itself, so there is no new annotation and no handoff protocol between the two. (3) THE EXPECTED OUTCOME IS `synthetic_handoff`, NOT `agent_handoff`. `POST /v1/messages` 409s while a turn is in flight and the Session interface has NO cancel primitive - `waitIdle` is the only way to a submittable handoff turn - so for a genuinely hung agent the sequence is: wait 90s, never idle, operator writes the synthetic note, pod stops. That is still the entire win: the note is what the next pod resumes from, and the old path left none. `expireTimedOutTurn` and its three tests were DELETED rather than left as a test-only orphan; the two properties they guarded (turn annotations cleared so a late callback cannot resolve the Task, and a stalled TURN never terminates the TASK) moved onto `stalledTurnStop` and are asserted there.

- 2026-08-08 (false stand-down) **A tatara-owned MR classified BEFORE its head SHA was known flipped itself to external on its own first commit, closing the PR and restarting the issue from nothing.** `ReconcileOwnership`'s backfill seeds `LastBotHeadSHA = liveHead` on a tatara classification exactly so the drift check below it cannot fire on the classification's own backfill - the comment there says it outright: "which is not a drift, it is day one". But that seed was gated on `liveHead != ""`, and a mirror is routinely classified on a reconcile that has not synced a head yet (a freshly opened PR whose head arrives a beat later). `LastBotHeadSHA` then stayed EMPTY, and the first reconcile that DID know the head evaluated `liveHead != "" && liveHead != ""` as drift and called `flipToExternal` against tatara's own commit. That parks the owning Task `ownership-lost`, hands the MR mirror to the review Task, posts the "a commit outside tatara landed on this branch" stand-down, and the issue restarts from scratch. LIVE EVIDENCE, and it is tatara destroying its own delivered work: `mr-mtg-decks-19` and `mr-mtg-decks-15` both ended `ownership=external` with `reason=external-push:<the agent's own head>` and `lastBotHeadSHA` EMPTY, while `mr-mtg-decks-17`, seeded normally, was untouched - 2 of 3. mtg-decks#19 was a FINISHED 4199-line deck PR (decklist, evidence, sim profiles, meta snapshots, analysis protocol); it was closed 4 minutes after the flip and the task rebuilt from nothing on the same branch as #20. Nothing was lost only because the branch is not deleted. Fix: when ownership is tatara and `LastBotHeadSHA` is EMPTY, SEED it to the live head and return - do not flip. With no baseline there is nothing to compare against, and the code's own stated policy for that state is already "whatever is on the branch right now was put there by the bot"; flipping on an unknowable question is the bug. The genuine-drift arm is unchanged and still fires once a baseline exists (the regression test asserts both halves - no flip on the agent's own first head, then a real flip on an unattributable one - so the fix cannot be mistaken for disabling the mechanism). Interim mitigation applied by hand before the fix shipped: `kubectl patch mergerequest mr-mtg-decks-20 --subresource=status` seeding `lastBotHeadSHA` to the live branch head, because #20 was sitting in the exact armed state (`ownership=tatara`, `lastBotHeadSHA` empty) and would have destroyed the second deck attempt too.

- 2026-08-08 (memory is optional, `spec.memory.enabled`) Disabling memory tears the stack's WORKLOADS down but must RETAIN every volume, and the retention has to strip ALL ownerReferences - not just the Project's. The cnpg PGDATA/WAL PVCs are owned by the cnpg *Cluster*, and the Cluster is exactly what the teardown deletes, so removing only the Project reference would still have cascaded the data away. There is no retention annotation, finalizer or reclaim-policy override anywhere in this operator, `memoryBackup.enabled` is off by default, and nothing calls the restore path automatically - so a cascade here is unrecoverable loss, which is why retention is unconditional rather than a flag. Retained objects are relabelled `tatara.dev/retained-for-project=<name>` because an object with no ownerReferences is otherwise unreachable from the Project; the re-enable edge re-adopts by that label and restores a NON-controller Project ref (cnpg wants the controller slot). The retention is NOT uniform across the stack, and the asymmetry is an EXPLICIT OWNER DECISION rather than an oversight: the repo owner approved DATA REMOVAL FOR LIGHTRAG specifically, so the lightrag PVC is DELETED on disable (skipped by the retention sweep, deleted explicitly in `tearDownMemoryStack` - it carries a Project ownerRef and the Project is not being deleted, so nothing would collect it otherwise), while the postgres corpus and the neo4j graph stay recoverable because the approval said nothing about them. Both the deletion site and the retention sweep carry a DO-NOT-UNIFY comment, and `TestReconcileMemory_DisabledRetainsPgAndNeo4jButDeletesLightrag` asserts BOTH halves, because the failure mode is somebody later making the four volumes 'consistent' in either direction - deleting all four loses the corpus and the graph unrecoverably, retaining all four contradicts the approval. Re-enable therefore re-adopts pg/neo4j by name but provisions a FRESH, empty lightrag volume; the lightrag index is derived data, rebuilt by re-ingesting. The neo4j PVC is found by NAME PREFIX (`data-mem-<proj>-neo4j-`), not by label: it comes from a StatefulSet volumeClaimTemplate, and volumeClaimTemplates are immutable on a live StatefulSet, so labels can never be added there without breaking every existing deployment's apply. Test-harness trap found while asserting the deletion: envtest CANNOT show a PVC disappearing - the apiserver's StorageObjectInUseProtection admission plugin stamps `kubernetes.io/pvc-protection` at CREATE and the controller that removes it lives in kube-controller-manager, which envtest does not run, so a deleted PVC sits there with a `deletionTimestamp` forever. Assert 'absent OR terminating' (`pvcDeleted`), and strip the finalizer by hand (`releasePVCFinalizers`) when a test needs to observe the object actually go. The teardown is guarded by `status.memory.disabledGeneration` so a permanently-disabled Project is not a per-reconcile delete storm. Two second-order fixes came with it: the repository ingest gate (the one unbounded 15s poll with no terminal state) now short-circuits on disabled instead of spinning forever behind `TataraIngestGated`/`TataraIngestStale`, and the turn-0 appendix switched from `MemoryDegradedGuidance` to a new `MemoryDisabledGuidance` - the degraded text tells the agent to call `report_internal_issue`, which on a project configured without memory manufactured one bogus platform-problem alert per turn (#523).
2026-08-08 (owner-ref writes vs. cache staleness, #524/#530/#545/#536) **Four separate alert firings over nine days were one fix surface: an owner-ref write acting on a copy the controller-runtime cache handed it.** `releaseOwnership` was the ONE such write in the tree outside the fresh-Get+`RetryOnConflict` discipline `ownership.go` documents and calls "the exact same conflict-safe discipline every other owner-ref write in this package uses" - `ownedIssues`/`ownedMRs` do a plain cached `r.Get` and it issued a bare `r.Update` on that copy. `MutateOwnerRefs` could not simply be reused because it was typed `*MergeRequest` and the reaper releases Issues too; generalising it (`MutateArtifactOwnerRefs[T,PT]`) rather than duplicating it is what made all of #524 fixable in one place. THE NON-OBVIOUS HALF: re-Getting is not enough on its own - the VERDICT has to be taken inside the mutation too. A caller that decides "no surviving heir, drop the ref" against the stale array and then only applies that verdict to the fresh copy has fixed its resourceVersion and kept its bug; that is exactly how a handover becomes an orphaning (mr-tatara-operator-504). Hence `ErrOwnerRefsUnchanged`, so a mutation that sees the fresh copy and finds nothing to do can decline the Update from inside the loop. Second: `retry.RetryOnConflict` MASKS #545 rather than fixing it - the second `releaseOwnership` per reap re-Gets, sees the flag already gone, and no-ops - so the `AnnTerminalReleased` guard on `deleteReapedTask` is still the fix, and it is a guard rather than a deletion of the call because `resume.go`, `reapDelivered` and the backlog-sweep park branch all reach it with no prior `releaseTerminal`. Third: a 409 is NOT a GC block. `operator_gc_blocked_total` has no decrement and its alert reads it as durable state ("accumulating in etcd"), so counting a conflict the requeue resolves in 0.9 s pinned the alert for ~1 h, three investigations deep (#467, #530, #545). Only failures a requeue will not resolve are counted now. Fourth, and the one that is NOT the reaper at all: `linkArtifact` was the only `own.AddPlainOwner` call site with no controller precondition, so a refine `links[]` entry landing in the reaper's deliberate zero-owner hand-off window (hours wide - 1 h 41 m observed) wrote the artifact's first and only ownerRef as PLAIN. It now claims the flag in the same Update, like the fold's `adopt`: claiming beats refusing because the umbrella is definitionally live while a refusal fails a legitimate link until the sweep runs, and beats promoting a pre-existing plain owner because that needs a liveness read this path does not have. TESTING NOTE: the fake client DOES produce these 409s - it enforces resourceVersion on Update - so a `Get` interceptor that serves one stale snapshot and then lets the cache "catch up" reproduces informer lag deterministically, where an envtest without a manager cache cannot produce it at all.
- 2026-08-08 (benign shutdown ERRORs, #538) **No reconciler classified `context.Canceled`, so every rolling restart logged benign shutdown aborts at ERROR and re-fired "Tatara operator error recurring" - 49 escalations, zero real faults.** controller-runtime logs ANY non-nil Reconcile error as `ERROR msg="Reconciler error"` (controller.go:494, v0.24.1) and the Loki rule counts ERROR lines regardless of cause, so a rollout that cancels >=2 in-flight SCM calls deterministically fires it; the two 2026-08-07 lines landed 261us and 363us after `Stopping and waiting for non leader election runnables`. The classifier is deliberately TWO-SIDED - `ctx.Err() == context.Canceled` AND `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` - because either half alone swallows real errors: ctx-only eats an etcd timeout that merely coincided with SIGTERM, err-only eats a per-call cancellation on a perfectly healthy manager. `context.DeadlineExceeded` is NOT matched; a timeout is a real failure whoever's clock it was. Applied at ALL EIGHT Reconcile boundaries via a `doReconcile` body + wrapper, not at `Complete(r)`, so the classification is reachable from a plain unit test that calls `Reconcile` directly. `LeaderElectionReleaseOnCancel` was re-examined as the amplifier and KEPT: turning it off does not remove the ERROR lines (the last old leader still dies mid-sweep, and one aborted sweep already trips the >=2/1h rule) and reinstates the #395 admission gap on every rollout.

- 2026-08-08 (contract skew, #544) **A strict-equality gate spanning two independently-rolled helm releases has a guaranteed non-empty failure window on EVERY release train, and this one was terminal.** The wrapper pin (`values/project-*/common.yaml`) landed 56 minutes before the operator pin (`helmfile.yaml.gotmpl`) on 2026-08-08; `AssertContractVersion`'s `!=` plus `podwatch.failContractMismatch`'s `ParkTask` destroyed 5 Tasks at turn-0 across 2 Projects inside it. Fix is a WINDOW of +-1 (`ContractSkewWindow`) reported as a distinct transient `ContractSkewError` - deliberately NOT a `*ContractMismatchError` and not unwrapping to one, because `IsContractMismatch` is the predicate that routes to the terminal park. The wait is not wishful: nothing has been submitted at handshake time, so waiting costs zero tokens, and when the train finishes by rolling the OPERATOR forward (which is what actually ended the outage) the SAME pod passes on the next reconcile with no respawn. Bounded at 2h from pod start, past which it degrades into exactly the old terminal park. `got <= 0` is excluded from the window on purpose: 0 is the sentinel for "the wrapper reported no contractVersion field at all", an old wrapper, not a neighbouring release. Metric side: skew is a STATE and was monitored with a COUNTER, which is why the rule was green for ~86% of a 71-minute outage - the series is born at 1 so `increase(...[5m])` needs a SECOND event, it goes flat while still fully broken, and an operator restart deletes it. Added `operator_agent_contract_skew` (gauge, retracted on a successful handshake for that image) and `operator_agent_contract_expected` (the compiled constant, which nothing had ever exported). The tatara-observability rule still has to be re-pointed at the gauge - not done here, different repo.
