- 2026-08-08 (empty synthetic handoff, #527) **The G.7 synthetic handoff note was content-free for ~19 days and the invariant it was written to satisfy held the whole time.** `ttlStop` built `agent.TTLStopInput` without `LastFinalText`/`PushedRepos` - with an in-code comment acknowledging it and concluding the non-empty-notes guarantee "still holds". It held SYNTACTICALLY and was worthless semantically: every synthetic note in production read exactly `TTL stop. Last turn's final text: (none). Repos pushed: none.`, so the next pod resumed from nothing, re-ran turn-0 and re-charged maxTurnsPerTask while the Task looked healthy. Both values arrive on every turn-complete callback and were simply never persisted; the fix is `status.lastTurnFinalText` + `status.lastTurnPushedRepos`, stamped by `CallbackServer.stampLastTurn` BEFORE `recordResult` (which stamps annTurnComplete and closes the window every other guarded write on that turn uses). `pushedReposKnown` is a real distinction, not defensive plumbing: the callback is authoritative for both, but the poll backstop reads `GET /v1/messages/{turnId}`, which has no pushedRepos field, so it must not clear what the callback recorded. Wired into ALL THREE `TTLStopInput` sites (ttlStop, stalledTurnStop, liveHandoffAndPark) - the stalled-turn one needs it most, since a genuinely hung wrapper never goes idle and always lands on the synthetic path. Added `operator_agent_synthetic_handoff_empty_total` so a note with nothing to say is never silent again.

- 2026-08-08 (fabricated human gates, #527 follow-up) **`awaiting-human` un-parks only on a non-bot comment, so a Task parked there having asked nothing waits forever on a reply nobody owes.** Live count in the tatara namespace: 45 Tasks parked awaiting-human, 20 with a real agent handoff, 8 with only the content-free synthetic note, 17 with no handoff note at all - 25 of 45 were pod-death wreckage, released only when an unrelated human comment happened to land on their issue. `liveHandoffAndPark` now tests for an AGENT-authored handoff note (`agent.HasAgentHandoffNote`; the operator's synthetic note deliberately does not count, however rich - it is continuation state for the next pod, not a question for a person) and re-arms for a replacement pod when there is none. THE `stateEnteredAt` RE-STAMP IN `stage.ReArmAfterPodLoss` IS LOAD-BEARING: clock 1 measures from it against AdmissionStarvedBudget, so leaving it stale re-elapses on the very next pass and parks `admission-starved`, which is UnparkNever - strictly worse than what it replaces, and exactly #513's "a retry that provides no retry" shape. `podRecreations` is NOT reset (unlike the un-park `reArm`): it is the only bound, and spending it terminates at `pod-recreation-exhausted`. The EVICTION path is exempt - the live-pod ceiling deliberately took that slot and handing it straight back defeats the mechanism.

- 2026-08-08 (unbounded deploy poll, #512) **`stage=deploying` is a pod-less POLL with no way to conclude that what it is waiting for is never coming.** The CD pin fan-out into tatara-helmfile stopped emitting bump PRs after 12:24:53Z on 2026-07-29 - v1.39.5 and v1.39.6 were tagged and published and no `cd: bump` commit or PR ever existed for either - so task mt-c-tatara-operator-506 polled for 2h06m59s emitting ZERO log lines, then spent all three MaxDeployReentries in 95 seconds against a frozen work clock and discarded merged PR #509 at deploy-blocked. THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE PIN AT tatara-helmfile's MAIN, not at an apply run: main is where the fan-out writes, so main still behind our tag means the bump was never merged (or never opened), and the apply is blameless - it had no commit to apply. All three remaining waits (no green apply, a green apply predating the merge, an applied pin behind the tag) collapse to the same failure once the bump does not exist, which is why they now share `fanoutBoundedWait`. The park is `deploy-timeout` and NOT `deploy-blocked` on purpose: a fan-out somebody repairs must still deliver the merge, and deploy-timeout is the UnparkTimer reason. `enqueueDeployTimeoutComment` became a free function so the driver posts the same notice the budget-elapse path does - a park nobody hears about is what made this invisible. CI side: `bump` reporting success is NOT evidence a pin landed (the failure mode was the writer producing nothing), so `verify-pin` reads the pin back off tatara-helmfile main for 10 minutes and `fanout-alarm` files an issue on failure - a red workflow run nobody looks at is what let this sit for ~9h. The tatara-helmfile `cd-release` action's own bug is NOT fixed here; it lives in a different repo.

- 2026-08-09 (the merge gate never compiled the Dockerfile, #556) **`ci-shared.yml` is five sibling repos' entire merge gate, and every job in it was driven by mise against `.mise.toml`, so NOTHING on the `pull_request` path built the Dockerfile - the artifact the merge produces was first compiled after the merge, on main.** `ARG GO_VERSION` is a hand-maintained third copy of `go.mod`'s `go` directive and nothing passes it as a build-arg, so the Dockerfile default is authoritative; the official `golang` images set `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, so a builder older than `go.mod` fails `go mod download` outright instead of fetching a newer toolchain. Four repos bumped Go 1.25 -> 1.26.3 in the same PR that adopted ci-shared and 4 of 4 shipped the latent break, each commit message listing only mise-driven checks as "verified"; `release.yml` is `on: workflow_run` gated `conclusion == 'success'`, so a red main means NO release at all and three repos shipped nothing for ten days. Fix is the whole build, not a pin-agreement check: the pin was one instance, and a renamed base image, a dropped apk package, a moved COPY source or a new import needing a C library in the builder all break the same artifact and are all invisible to a check that compares version strings. THE PR BUILD MUST BE THE SAME BUILD - the push job uses buildkit's REMOTE git context (`context=https://github.com/szymonrychu/${REPO}.git#<ref>`) with `GIT_AUTH_TOKEN` as a frontend secret for the private clone, so a PR-time build from the local checkout would exercise neither the clone nor the token path and would certify a build that is not the one that runs. `image-verify` therefore uses the identical context shape against `refs/pull/N/merge` (the merge result GitHub already publishes) and differs from `image` in exactly two places: the ref, and `--output type=cacheonly` instead of `push=true`. Not path-filtered on Dockerfile/go.mod/.mise.toml, because a Dockerfile breaks from source changes too and a filter would close the one instance and leave the class. Cost is controlled instead by `needs: [build]` (a PR that does not compile in Go never reaches buildkitd), by having NO checkout step at all (the context is remote, and VERSION is `pr-N` rather than `git describe`, which also keeps the ldflags layer a cache hit across pushes to the same PR), and by the runner being a self-hosted in-cluster ARC runner sharing buildkitd's Ceph-backed layer cache with the push builds. It is deliberately NOT gated on `enable-image`: that input exists only so two jobs never push the same immutable Harbor tag, and cacheonly pushes nothing - so the repos that own their image publish (`tatara-memory`, `tatara-chat`) get the gate too, which is the whole point, since they are two of the three that were dead. `enable-image-verify` is the escape hatch for a repo whose build needs build-args this workflow cannot supply. Guarded by `internal/cicontract`, a package that exists only to assert properties of the CI definition itself - the hole was invisible precisely because nothing in the Go tree could see it.
- 2026-08-09 (a caller's 400 is not an operator ERROR, #558) **`writeDecodeError` logged every malformed request body at `log.Log.Error`, so bytes the CALLER got wrong were counted by "Tatara operator error recurring" as the operator's own faults.** One agent guessing a payload shape read as five operator errors 77us after the operator had already logged, at INFO, that the condition was retryable and that it had released the claim to make the retry cheap - the same event classified twice, at two severities, in the same request. The only thing that can fail at this site is caller-supplied bytes, so the answer is always a 4xx; it is now `s.log.Warn`, which required making the free function a METHOD (all six call sites already had a `*Server`, and `s.log` is the injectable `*slog.Logger` a test can point at a buffer - `log.Log` is a controller-runtime global with no test seam). The other half is why the retries happened at all: the 400 said only `invalid JSON body`, so the agent re-guessed the shape 5 times over 48s against an answer that never named the rejected key. `decodeErrorMessage` now names it, from the two shapes the wire actually produces - `*json.UnmarshalTypeError` (typed) and `DisallowUnknownFields`, whose error is a bare `errors.New` with no typed form, so the name can only be recovered by parsing the `json: unknown field ` prefix. Everything else degrades to the old generic text ON PURPOSE: only names derived from the caller's own keys go back out, never the decoder's raw output, and the name is clamped to 64 runes because a JSON key is caller-controlled and otherwise bounded only by `maxBodyBytes`. NOT DONE and NOT the same bug: `writeClientErr`'s `restapi: client error` stays at ERROR - it answers 500, and a 500 IS the operator's, even when the underlying k8s error was a retryable 409 (the arguable defect there is the STATUS mapping, not the level). MEASUREMENT NOTE for anyone re-reading #558: its headline - "100% of the firing volume is non-final RETRY attempts that then SUCCEEDED" - did not survive 12 hours. Both classes it named had decayed (`restapi: decode body failed` to ZERO), and the largest class in its own 24h window was one it never mentioned: 37 `Reconciler error` + 37 `livepods: eviction failed` from the CRD rejecting `clarify` on `notes[].agent`, already fixed by #540 and merely not yet rolled out. A log-line-count alert read at one instant measures the deploy lag as much as the code.

- 2026-08-09 (`cacheonly` does not exist on this buildkitd, #556 follow-up) **The first cut of `image-verify` used `--output type=cacheonly` and was RED ON ITS OWN PR - `error: failed to solve: exporter "cacheonly" could not be found` - while every test in `internal/cicontract` was GREEN, because those tests only assert that the literal string appears in the YAML.** Not a version mismatch: client and daemon are both moby/buildkit v0.18.2 (`harbor.szymonrichert.pl/proxy-dockerhub/moby/buildkit:v0.18.2-rootless`, deploy/buildkitd in arc-runners), and the daemon's exporter registry simply has no such name. Probed the live daemon over its own socket: `cacheonly` ABSENT; `tar`, `image`, `local`, `oci` all present. THE FIX IS TO PASS NO `--output` AT ALL, not to pick another exporter - `tar` and `oci` take a client-side `dest=`, so they stream the entire image daemon->client over gRPC, which for a wrapper-sized image is real per-PR in-cluster traffic to write to /dev/null; `image,push=false` names something that is never published, which reads as a publish waiting to be re-enabled. With no exporter buildkit still solves the whole graph, so every stage runs and a failing step still exits non-zero - verified end to end against this repo's real Dockerfile over the real `refs/pull/N/merge` remote context: green as-is, and with `--opt build-arg:GO_VERSION=1.25` it exits 1 with `go: go.mod requires go >= 1.26.3 (running go 1.25.12; GOTOOLCHAIN=local)`, which is verbatim the break #556 exists to catch. THE SECOND HALF IS THE LESSON: a static text assertion over a workflow file cannot fail when the job fails, so it launders the failure and is worse than no test. Those tests are now all named `TestWorkflowText_*`, the package doc says outright that the real signal is the job going green, and the publish check asserts the ABSENCE of an exporter rather than the presence of a named one - absence needs no daemon support to be correct, whereas any name is only trustworthy after somebody probes the daemon, which nothing in Go can do from CI. `script()` also had to start stripping shell comment lines, because the job now carries a paragraph explaining why it must not use `type=cacheonly` and the naive matcher asserted against its own prose.

- 2026-08-09 (#566, the turn annotations are POD-SCOPED and #551 forgot to say so) **1109 `turn_timeout` INFO lines in 30 minutes on the v2.1.5 leader - 19 parked Tasks, each exactly 60 times, once every 30s, forever - with ZERO agent pods running: 100% of the operator's INFO volume was one loop that could never terminate.** #551 was right to stop `PollOnce` hard-deleting a stalled turn's pod (no handoff, no note, unpushed work died with the workspace) and to defer the graceful stop to `TaskReconciler.stalledTurnStop` - but the deferral ASSUMED A LIVE POD ON THE RECEIVING SIDE, and nothing said so. `stalledTurnStop` is behind `task.Status.PodStartedAt != nil` in `reconcilePodStage`, and `reconcileStage` returns early for a parked Task before any pod stage runs at all, so for a Task in either shape the deferral target never fires; `turnTimedOut` anchors on `max(turn-started-at, turn-last-activity-at)`, both frozen the instant the pod died, so the predicate is permanently true and the detection re-fires every tick. THE ROOT CAUSE IS NOT THE LOG: `stalledTurnStop` was the ONLY site in the repo that cleared the four turn annotations, so every other way a pod ends leaked them - `ParkTask` (the park choke point, hence eviction, idle exit, every park reason), `ttlStop`, `respawnLostPod`, `reArmWithoutHandoff` (the #561/#563 non-park exit, and `stage.ReArmAfterPodLoss` is a pure status mutation that CANNOT write metadata). A leaked in-flight annotation keeps `stage.TurnInFlight` true forever, which disarms the idle clock in `ArmedClock`, makes the Task permanently ineligible in `evictionCandidates`, exempts its pod from the idle-pod reaper's `!taskHasInflightTurn` guard, and gags the conversation follow-up turn in `reconcilePodStage` - i.e. it silently swallows every subsequent human comment. Fix is two-layer: `clearTurnAnnotations` called from all four teardown paths, plus a repair in `PollOnce` for a Task the reconciler provably cannot reach (parked, or `podStartedAt == nil`), which is the only loop in the operator that visits every Task on a fixed cadence - a parked Task gets NO timed requeue from its own reconciler, so a reconcile-side repair would never have drained the 19 already in etcd. `EnterStage` deliberately does NOT clear even though it tears down the pod of the state it left: the happy outcome path is submit_outcome -> transition -> turn-complete callback, and clearing `current-turn` there would 404 that callback and lose its usage stats and `LastTurnFinalText` (which is what the synthetic handoff note is built from, #527); the 30s `PollOnce` backstop retires it once the pod is confirmed gone instead. `operator_orphaned_turn_annotations_cleared_total{project}` reads like `operator_task_parked_with_live_pod_repaired_total`: a burst after rollout is the backlog draining, a sustained rate means a teardown path is still leaking.

- 2026-08-09 (#527 re-opened by its own fix; re-authors #531 against post-#539 main) **#557 closed #527 with one of the six mechanisms #531 proposed, and one of the five it left out was a live regression in #557 itself.** `stampLastTurn` wrote `status.lastTurnFinalText` UNCONDITIONALLY, so the next turn to finish with nothing to say erased what the previous turn had captured - and `state="failed"` is a real wrapper state that reaches the callback with an empty finalText and no pushedRepos. One failed turn was enough to leave the Task with no continuation state, and the next G.7 stop then wrote exactly the content-free note #527 was filed about, one turn after the operator was holding a real payload. The guard is `finalText == "" && len(pushedRepos) == 0`, never `finalText` alone (a push with no closing message is still continuation state), and it lives INSIDE `stampLastTurn` so it covers every caller present and future. The field is therefore the newest NON-EMPTY payload - "produced anything", not "completed" - which is what makes `handoff="none"` mean what the runbook claims. It does not weaken the `pushedReposKnown` signal #557 added: a turn that said something and pushed nothing still clears the repos, because its payload is not empty. **The generalisable lesson is the shape, not the line:** a fix that adds a field the operator must KEEP is only as good as its worst write path, and #557's review approved the read side (three call sites correctly wired) while nothing asked what happens when the write has nothing to write. **The other four, all absent from main and all re-authored rather than rebased** (#531 predates #539: it edits `conversing.go`, which no longer exists, and `Status.Stage*`, which is now `Status.State*`; it also misses the two newer `TTLStopResult` call sites, `stalledTurnStop` and `liveHandoffAndPark`): (1) nothing ever CLEARED the last-turn state, so a payload outlived both the state occupancy that produced it and the note that already consumed it - now cleared in `stage.stampEnter` alongside `PodStartedAt` and in all three `StopWithHandoff` callers, but deliberately NOT in `respawnLostPod`, which writes no note at all and where the status is the vanished pod's only surviving trace. (2) `operator_agent_pod_ttl_expired_total` carried ONE `outcome` label answering two questions and `finish()` overwrote the important one: any teardown error relabelled the stop `force_deleted` regardless of what the agent had written, which is why `synthetic_handoff` was STRUCTURALLY unreachable over 30d. Now `outcome` (graceful|force_deleted) and `handoff` (agent|synthetic|none) are independent, and `StopWithHandoff` returns `agent.TTLStopResult` so a caller cannot record one dimension while dropping the other. The label NAMES are load-bearing beyond this repo: tatara-observability#101 (merged) derives each metric's declared label set from the `[]string{...}` closing argument of this constructor call and FAILS CLOSED on any alert or panel selecting a label the producer does not declare, so `{handoff="none"}` in tatara-observability#96 is only lintable once this ships. (3) `#557` added `operator_agent_synthetic_handoff_empty_total` for the content-free case but left the NOTE unchanged, so the operator could see the loss while the next agent still could not - the body said "Last turn's final text: (none)", indistinguishable from a turn that had nothing worth saying. It now leads with `agent.SyntheticNoteLostMarker` and tells the agent to re-derive rather than continue. (4) the stop sequence assumed the wrapper was there: absent, it polled a corpse for the full `2*turnTimeout` and then fabricated `force_deleted`. `wrapperGone` probes up front, `finish` no longer counts a force-delete of an absent pod, and a stop that captured nothing logs at ERROR - it logged `"agent pod TTL-stopped; handed off"` at INFO on that path too, which is why grepping 299395 lines of operator stdout produced two entries both claiming success. (5) the reaper's idle backstop (30m) and the pod TTL (3600s) were unaware of each other and the reaper always won, so the handoff turn was never offered (live: reap 12:10:19Z, stop 12:37:58Z). It now stands down for the WINDOW `[t0, t0 + 2*turnTimeout + TTLGrace)` read off the pod's own env, because the reaper resolves no Projects. The far end is the stopper's own hard cap and NOT `IdlePodReapAfter`: at the stock `turnTimeoutSeconds=900` the cap is 31m, LONGER than the 30m backstop, so borrowing that constant would re-arm the reaper mid-sequence and re-open the race - and an open-ended stand-down would trade #237's backstop for the assumption that a reconcile reaches the TTL gate, which is exactly what wedges in the cases #237 exists for.

- 2026-08-09 (#564, a poisoned eviction victim starved the pass forever) `enforceLivePodCeiling`'s eviction loop kept its one `firstErr` in a stack-local variable, so a candidate that errored was forgotten the instant the function returned - and `evictionCandidates` re-sorts the SAME longest-idle Task to the front on the very next pass, so one permanently-erroring victim burned the pass's one eviction slot (`maxLivePodEvictionsPerPass`) every single pass forever while every other overflow Task sat untouched. Fix is an in-memory `ProjectReconciler.evictionFailures` map (consecutive-failure count + next-eligible time, exponential backoff 30s->10m cap) plus a new `attemptEvictions` helper that walks candidates in order, skips anyone still serving a backoff, and on error records the failure and falls through to the NEXT candidate in the SAME pass instead of returning - so the per-pass success cap is still met from the next-best victim. Two dead ends: (1) keyed the map by Task UID first, since that is what the API server actually guarantees unique - broke immediately in tests because `sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/client/fake` does NOT assign a UID to seeded objects (confirmed by reading `fake/client.go`: it only ever carries through `obj.UID`, never generates one), so every un-UID'd Task in a test fixture collided on the same zero-value key and each was treated as the other's backoff - re-keyed on `types.NamespacedName` instead, which costs only a stale-backoff edge case on a deleted-and-recreated same-named Task, bounded by the backoff cap anyway. (2) `fakeSession.submitErr` (the existing eviction-fixture error hook) is ONE error shared by the whole fixture's single session, so it fails every Task's handoff, not one victim among several - the regression test instead injects failure through `interceptor.Funcs.SubResourceUpdate` keyed on the Task's own name, since `patchTaskStatus`/`FitNoteAppender` write through `Status().Update` and that call carries the object identity the shared-session hook cannot express.

- 2026-08-06 (issue #525) **`operator_repository_ingest_failing` and `operator_repository_ingest_gated` were independent, and that pair deadlocks: gated means no ingest Job runs, and ONLY a successful ingest resets `Phase`/`IngestFailureCount`, so a repo that was already failing when the memory-readiness gate closed pinned the failing gauge - and `TataraIngestJobFailing` / the Grafana mirror `efr6fxwsdtloga` - for as long as the project's memory stack was down.** Live: `mtg-decks` held "Repository stuck in failing ingest state" 18.6h with `increase(operator_ingest_job_total[3h]) == 0` fleet-wide and its last ingest pod line 19.6h old, while `ingest_gated=1` since 03:35Z; no action on the repo could have cleared it, and every clause of the alert annotation ("a persistent analyzer/auth/graph failure survived the incremental-to-full self-heal and has not cleared on re-ingest") was false. This generalises to ANY repo in ANY project whose MemoryStack goes Degraded. **Fixed by MASKING THE REPORT, not by resetting the CR state**, and that choice is the whole decision: `IngestFailureCount` also drives the exponential back-off gate and the `incrementalFallbackThreshold` incremental-to-full escalation, so clearing it at the gate would restart back-off at 30s and drop the self-heal the instant a just-recovered memory stack came back - i.e. the recovery path would hammer the thing that had just recovered. The gauge is a REPORT ("this repo is stuck failing"), which is simply untrue of a repo that is not retrying at all. `publishIngestHealth(repo, gated, notReadyFor)` is now the SINGLE writer of both gauges (`masked = gated && notReadyFor >= ingestGateMaskDelay`, `failing = enabled && !masked && (Phase=="Failed" || count>0)`), so the two can no longer disagree; the two `SetRepositoryIngestGated` call sites in the gate branch and its fall-through are gone. THE PREDICATE IS "the gate is what is holding this repo's NEXT ingest", which is why `status.jobName == ""` is part of it: the Job concurrency guard returns ~30 lines BEFORE the gate is ever consulted, so a memory blip mid-ingest would otherwise report a running repo as gated AND mask its failing gauge while it was genuinely retrying (`TestRepoReconcile_ActiveJobIsNotReportedGated`). That is a REPORTING predicate only - `memoryGated` (pure `MemoryStablyReady`) still drives the gate branch itself, deliberately kept as a separate variable, because `patchStatus` does not write back into the local `repo` and a stale `status.jobName` in the gate condition would silently let an ingest past the gate. The mask is not latched - it is recomputed per reconcile, so the failing gauge un-masks on the FIRST pass after the gate opens, with no successful ingest in between (`TestPublishIngestHealth_Gated` cycles it both ways; `TestRepoReconcile_GatedRepoDoesNotReportFailing` drives the same through a real envtest reconcile). NON-OBVIOUS ORDERING: the gate state is an INPUT to a gauge published near the top of `Reconcile`, so the owning-Project `Get` moved up there too and its error is now carried in `projectErr` and surfaced at its ORIGINAL position further down - deliberately, so the `IngestEnabled=false` early-return and the Job-concurrency-guard early-returns keep their exact behaviour while still publishing both gauges (previously those paths left `ingest_gated` stale at its last value). A repo whose Project cannot be read reports not-gated, which preserves the old failing semantics for that (already broken) case. That deferral is the fragile part of the diff - it separates a `Get` from its error check by ~55 lines, and deleting the check leaves the whole suite green while falling through to `ingest.BuildJob` with a ZERO-VALUE Project - so `TestRepoReconcile_MissingProjectErrors` now pins it; there was previously not one test in the package asserting a `Reconcile` error at all. UNCONDITIONAL masking is a REGRESSION, and closing that is the second half of this entry (caught in review of MR #533, first attempt). `applyMemoryStack` clears `readySince` on ANY non-Ready phase and health is polled every 10s, so one memory-pod restart lasting >10s gates the whole project for a fresh 3m stabilization window. A stack restarting every ~10m masks `ingest_failing` for 3m out of every 10, which stops `Repository stuck in failing ingest state` (`for: 1h`) from ever reaching its window on a repo that IS stuck - coverage collapses to `TataraIngestStale`'s 26h backstop, strictly worse than the deadlock. THE FIRST ATTEMPT'S MITIGATION WAS IN THE WRONG PLACE: `max_over_time(operator_repository_ingest_gated[15m])` in this chart's own `TataraIngestGated`, whose alerts carry only `severity` and therefore do NOT match the `system=tatara` notification policy - the two rules that actually page are both in tatara-observability (`alerts/tatara-ingester.yaml`) and both read the BARE gauges at `for: 1h`. A PromQL smear in a rule nobody routes fixes nothing. THE FIX IS A QUALIFICATION ON ENTERING THE MASK, NOT A DWELL ON LEAVING IT: `masked` requires `memoryNotReadyFor(project) >= ingestGateMaskDelay` (`memoryStuckAlertFor` 15m + `MemoryReadyStabilizationWindow` 3m = 18m). Under chatter the gate never holds 18m, the mask never engages, and `failing` stays continuously 1 exactly as it did pre-MR. The reviewer's suggested dwell-after-reopen was REJECTED for two independent reasons, both worth keeping: (a) a dwell holds `failing=0` AFTER the gate opens, i.e. while the repo is genuinely retrying and failing - a false negative in the SOURCE metric for every consumer, which is worse than the smear it replaces; and (b) a dwell is not reconcile-implementable here - while gated the reconciler requeues every 15s, but once the gate OPENS the next requeue comes from `scheduleNextReingest` (up to `maxScheduleRequeue`, 6h) or the back-off (capped 30m), so a dwell deadline could only be honoured by a scrape-time collector or a Reconcile-wide requeue clamp. Qualification has no such problem BY CONSTRUCTION: the mask can only ENGAGE inside the gate branch's own 15s requeue loop, and it un-masks on the first reconcile after the gate opens. A dwell is also not restart-safe - one false-negative health poll after an operator rollout nils `readySince` and gates every project for 3m, which under a dwell would blank `failing` fleet-wide for a full dwell period. THE CLOCK IS `Project.status.memory.provisioningSince`, NOT the Repository's own `MemoryNotReady` condition: several Repository reconcile paths (`ingestEnabled` toggled off, an unreadable Project, a failed `computeRepoCounts` patch) return BEFORE that condition is cleared, so it can stay True across a recovery and hand a LATER gate close an hours-old transition time, masking instantly. `provisioningSince` is maintained unconditionally by the Project reconciler, cleared on Ready, and is the EXACT quantity `operator_memory_stacks{phase=~"Provisioning|Failed|Degraded"}` measures - which is what makes 18m comparable to that alert's 15m `for` and buys the invariant that the mask can never engage on a repo whose project is not already eligible to page in its own right (`Memory stack stuck not ready`, severity critical). REQUIRED SUB-FIX: `failMemory` now stamps `provisioningSince` when nil. Every `failMemory` caller returns immediately, so `reconcileMemory`'s stamp block is only reached on the Failed->Provisioning recovery edge, and reaching Ready clears the field - a stack that went Ready->Failed therefore had NO non-Ready clock at all and 18m could never elapse, leaving #525 unfixed for exactly that phase. Stamp only when nil, or a stack that keeps failing resets its own clock every pass (`TestFailMemoryStampsProvisioningSince` pins both halves). Chart consequences: `TataraIngestGated`'s expr is back to the bare gauge, and `TataraIngestJobFailing` moved `for: 15m` -> `20m` so it sits strictly after the 18m mask - otherwise a repo that starts failing at the moment its memory stack goes down fires at 15m and resolves at 18m, a guaranteed flap. The two gauges are mutually exclusive only for a SUSTAINED gate; both alert descriptions say so, and `TestPublishIngestHealth_Gated` / `TestRepoReconcile_ChatteringGateKeepsFailingVisible` pin the chatter case at unit and envtest level. OUT OF SCOPE (separate issues, deliberately not adopted): the tatara-observability rule `efr6fxwsdtloga` needs NO `unless on(project,repo) ...gated == 1` now that the source metric is correct, but its annotation text is still wrong for a gated repo; and #520's stated root cause for the `mem-mtg` wedge (a v0.4.4 rollout crash-recovery loop, disk explicitly "ruled out") is contradicted by 1168 kubelet `NodeExpandVolume`/`VolumeResizeFailed` events on `pvc-4708b4bd-a5c5-4ea2-99be-69f6caccec07` starting 2026-07-29T07:23:43Z, ~20h BEFORE that rollout, with Ceph `HEALTH_OK`. `go test ./...` green (`make test` adds `-race`, which needs cgo and a C toolchain the agent pod does not have; CI covers it).

- 2026-08-09 (#525 recut of #533) #533 reached this exact fix, was reviewed to approval at `409af54` (round 2), and closed unmerged anyway - not on any surviving review objection but because the tatara task that opened it ended `failed`/`merge-blocked` and its branch was deleted with it, a process/infra closure with no code content. Re-cut as `gh pr create` #572 by cherry-picking both of #533's commits (`7b90745`, `409af54`) straight onto current main (16 commits ahead of #533's base): both applied clean except a documentation-only `MEMORY.md` conflict from unrelated entries appended in between, resolved by keeping both sides. No production code needed manual resolution. Confirmed the newer `MemoryDisabled` short-circuit (`4d0497e`, added after #533's base) is an unrelated early-return earlier in `Reconcile` and does not interact with the gate/mask logic here - it bypasses the gate for fully-disabled projects only, and does nothing for a Degraded stack, which is exactly the case this fix covers.

- 2026-08-09 (control-plane scheduling) `internal/grafanamcp` CANNOT import `internal/agent` to reuse `agent.Scheduling`: `internal/agent/pod.go` already imports `internal/grafanamcp` for `MCPURL`, so it is an import cycle. `grafanamcp.Config` therefore carries flat NodeSelector/Tolerations/Affinity fields, mirroring `agent.PodConfig`, and `cmd/manager/wire.go` copies them from `cfg.MCPSchedulingParsed` (parsed by `config.Load` with the same `agent.ParseScheduling` + `DisallowUnknownFields` contract as `AGENT_SCHEDULING`). The stamped Deployment is server-side-applied on EVERY reconcile (`project_grafana.go`), so a placement change moves the running pods without any delete/recreate.

- 2026-08-10 (assignment-resume-merge-first) **The operator provably CANNOT tell whether a task's remote branch already exists, so the resumed-branch rule is stated UNCONDITIONALLY in turn-0 and the wrapper supplies the conditional fact.** Checked all three routes and none exists: there is no `ls-remote` anywhere in the operator, `agent.TaskBranch` is a pure function recomputed from the Task spec (it never reads the forge), and no SCM interface has a branch-existence method. Adding one would be a per-Task forge round trip on every assignment render for a fact the wrapper already learns for free during clone. So `resumedBranchMergeRule` is a single package-level const spliced into the kinds that PUSH code - implement and documentation - and deliberately not into brainstorm/incident/refine/review, which push nothing (a review Task checks its target out READ-ONLY via CHECKOUT_BRANCH with TASK_BRANCH empty, so it has no branch to reconcile and the sentence would be pure noise). One const rather than two literals, because two copies of a rule this specific drift. Pairs with tatara-claude-code-wrapper `bootstrap-conflict-not-fatal`, which stopped failing the pod boot on an unresolvable base merge and now writes the naming detail (repo directory, task branch, base ref) into the injected global CLAUDE.md; without this operator half the agent would meet that block with nothing in its assignment telling it the block outranks the assignment. Also corrected the `agent.TaskBranch` doc comment, which claimed "the turn prompts" agree on the branch: they do not and never did - no assignment names it, the only branch string in turn-0 is a merge request's own head branch attribute, and the branch reaches the pod as the TASK_BRANCH env var (`branchEnvValues`) and the sweep/reaper head-branch comparisons.
